# Subagent Learnings Log

Append-only log where every Navier subagent leaves feedback to improve future runs. The parent agent periodically promotes high-value learnings into the instruction/schema files themselves.

Newest entries at the bottom. Read this before starting any task.

## Entries

---
date: 2026-05-27
subagent: navier-city-retrofit.md
task: Retrofitted Dubai city file with Operating Constraints section (single-node, no sub-clusters).
learnings:
  - methodology: For Gulf cities, decompose sea state by named sub-water-body (sheltered creek/marina vs. semi-exposed coastal vs. exposed open-Gulf) rather than a single citywide number — the operability story differs by 5–10× across these zones and drives which wedges are weather-robust.
  - methodology: N30 range section is most useful when it explicitly maps each named route in Waterways to "comfortably in / edge / out of range" — turns the constraint into routing guidance rather than abstract spec.
  - source-discovered: Dubai↔AUH straight-line is ~65–75 nm depending on routing, right at N30 edge; Dubai↔Ghantoot (~45 nm) is the natural sub-corridor pilot before claiming full express.
  - schema-gap: CITY_TEMPLATE Operating Constraints bullet on regulatory blackouts could explicitly prompt for Eid + national day marine-traffic restrictions, not just Ramadan/elections/audit — these recur predictably across MENA cities and are easy to miss.
suggested-update:
  target-file: /agent/home/navier/world-map/_schema/CITY_TEMPLATE.md
  change: In Operating Constraints, expand the regulatory bullet to "Ramadan, Eids, national days with marine-traffic restrictions, election cycles, audit seasons, fiscal year-end."

---
date: 2026-05-27
subagent: navier-city-retrofit.md
task: Retrofitted Abu Dhabi city file with Sub-clusters + Operating Constraints (multi-node cluster).
learnings:
  - methodology: For UAE-style multi-node emirates, define each sub-cluster's role (anchor/hub/leisure spoke/industrial node) AND tag it against N30 range buckets (in-range / edge / out-of-range) — this makes the Sub-clusters section drive pilot route selection rather than being a glossary.
  - methodology: For Gulf cities, the operability story is dominated by summer heat (Jun–Sep) collapsing demand even though seas are calm — explicitly warn against building pilot economics on summer utilization. This is the inverse of the temperate-coast intuition.
  - methodology: Fog (Nov–Mar western Gulf morning fog) is a real published-schedule constraint and is often missed in MENA city files; worth a dedicated bullet in Operating Constraints, not lumped under "weather".
  - source-discovered: AUH↔Sir Bani Yas is ~135 nm one-way — *outside* N30 single-leg range without a hybrid Quanta or intermediate charging node. This materially changes the Anantara wedge framing and should be flagged in the wedge itself, not just buried in constraints.
  - schema-gap: CITY_TEMPLATE doesn't currently prompt for a "summer-vs-winter demand inversion" question, which is critical for MENA and tropical-monsoon markets where the engineering window and the demand window diverge. Worth a dedicated sub-bullet under Daylight/tourist seasonality.
suggested-update:
  target-file: /agent/home/navier/world-map/_schema/CITY_TEMPLATE.md
  change: In Operating Constraints > N30 implications, add explicit prompt to "tag each route from Waterways as in-range / edge-of-range / out-of-range, and call out any wedge whose viability depends on out-of-range nodes (hybrid Quanta required, or intermediate charging node build)."

---
date: 2026-05-27
subagent: navier-city-retrofit.md
task: Retrofitted Red Sea Global (RSG / AMAALA) KSA file with Sub-clusters + Operating Constraints (multi-destination greenfield portfolio).
learnings:
  - methodology: For *greenfield* destinations (RSG, NEOM, future Saudi/PIF builds), the charging-infrastructure section flips from a constraint to a strategic *integration window* — call out that marina electrical spec must be influenced *before steel is cut*, because retrofitting DC fast-charge into an already-poured marina is the same uphill fight as in legacy ports. This is a unique-to-greenfield methodology and deserves its own schema prompt.
  - methodology: When a file covers a portfolio strung along a long coastline (RSG ~250km, Maldives atolls, Indonesian archipelagos), use a Sub-clusters *table* rather than prose, with explicit columns for distance-from-anchor and N30 range bucket. The table format forces the route-economics question to be answered for every node, which a prose list lets you skip.
  - methodology: Daylight/VFR seaplane limitation = structural Navier advantage at any destination where seaplane is the incumbent transfer (RSG, Maldives, BVI). Worth a standing wedge bullet whenever Fly-X-style seaplane operator is the named incumbent — not just a footnote in Operating Constraints.
  - source-discovered: Saudi Arabia adds *two* national days to the regulatory blackout calendar (National Day Sep 23, Founding Day Feb 22) on top of Ramadan + two Eids + Hajj — KSA government dead-zones are noticeably denser than UAE; pipeline planning should assume only ~8 productive months/year for KSA procurement.
  - source-discovered: The Red Sea ↔ AMAALA corridor (~150km / ~80nm) is just outside N30 single-leg range — this is a real Navier-spec edge case worth keeping in a "known out-of-range marquee routes" list (alongside AUH↔Sir Bani Yas ~135nm from prior retrofit) to inform N80 / hybrid-Quanta product positioning.
  - schema-gap: CITY_TEMPLATE Operating Constraints doesn't currently distinguish *greenfield* from *brownfield* charging context. For PIF/giga-project files this is the single most important framing — needs its own bullet or sub-prompt.
  - schema-gap: Sub-clusters section as written invites prose; a table format with mandatory columns (name / role / distance-from-anchor / N30 bucket / what's distinct) would be more rigorous and prevent skipped nodes.
suggested-update:
  target-file: /agent/home/navier/world-map/_schema/CITY_TEMPLATE.md
  change: (1) In Sub-clusters section, prescribe a table with columns "Sub-cluster | Role | Distance from anchor | N30 range bucket | What's distinct". (2) In Operating Constraints > Charging/shore-power bullet, add sub-prompt: "If greenfield (PIF giga-project, NEOM-style new build), flag the marina-spec integration window — DC fast-charge must be designed in pre-construction, not retrofitted." (3) In regulatory bandwidth bullet, expand KSA-specific blackouts to "Ramadan, Eid al-Fitr, Eid al-Adha, Hajj, National Day Sep 23, Founding Day Feb 22" as a named example.

date: 2026-05-27
subagent: navier-city-retrofit.md
task: Retrofitted Jeddah KSA city file with Operating Constraints (single-node mainland gateway, no sub-clusters).
learnings:
  - methodology: When a city is positioned as a "gateway" to a remote hospitality cluster (Jeddah→RSG/AMAALA/NEOM; analogously Malé→atolls, Cancún→Riviera Maya outer cays), the N30 range mapping often **invalidates the gateway narrative** for line-haul — distances are typically 80–300+ nm. The retrofit should explicitly call this out and reframe the city as **demo/staging/marketing** rather than line-haul origin. This is a recurring pattern worth a standing schema prompt for any "gateway city" file.
  - methodology: Hajj is a uniquely large blackout window for Jeddah specifically (city is the Hajj airport gateway, Coast Guard fully committed, ~3 weeks effective shutdown) — heavier than the generic KSA Hajj blackout that applies to Riyadh/Dammam. Worth tagging in any Jeddah/Makkah-region file as a distinct sub-bullet, not lumped with other KSA blackouts.
  - methodology: Fringing-reef coastlines (Jeddah, much of Red Sea, parts of Caribbean and East Africa) create a hard structural constraint — foil-up cruising must respect charted pass geometry and depths, and night/low-tide ops are gated. This is Navier-specific (deeper draft when foils down, foil clearance when up) and deserves its own bullet whenever the coast has fringing reef rather than being lumped under "sea state".
  - source-discovered: Jeddah Corniche is recreational not commuter; intra-city mobility wedge is near-zero; the city's value is purely upstream (corporate/royal capital + airport + demo venue) and downstream (gateway marketing) — confirms ride-hail and PTA archetype scores should stay low for any KSA Red Sea coastal city except RSG/AMAALA/NEOM proper.
  - source-discovered: Jeddah↔KAEC is ~65 nm, right at N30 edge — the only credible Jeddah-origin in-range coastal corridor, and worth flagging as a potential sub-corridor pilot (analogous to Dubai↔Ghantoot from prior retrofit). Coastal-corridor edge-of-N30 sub-pilots are an emerging pattern across MENA retrofits.
  - schema-gap: CITY_TEMPLATE has no prompt for **cultural/protocol constraints on water** (female-only modes, alcohol-free framing, prayer-time scheduling) which are first-order product-design constraints in KSA and parts of MENA/SEA Muslim-majority markets. Currently buried as a holds note; should be a dedicated Operating Constraints bullet.
suggested-update:
  target-file: /agent/home/navier/world-map/_schema/CITY_TEMPLATE.md
  change: (1) Add an Operating Constraints sub-bullet "**Cultural / protocol constraints on water**: female-only / family-only modes, alcohol policy, prayer-time scheduling, modesty norms — where these materially shape product design." (2) Add a standing prompt under N30 implications: "If the city is positioned as a 'gateway' to a remote cluster, explicitly test whether the gateway-to-cluster legs are in N30 range; if not, reframe city role as demo/staging/marketing rather than line-haul origin."

date: 2026-05-27
subagent: navier-city-retrofit.md
task: Retrofitted NEOM/Sindalah/Oxagon KSA file with Sub-clusters + Operating Constraints (multi-node PIF giga-project).
learnings:
  - methodology: For NEOM-class giga-projects, treat **leadership churn** as a first-class regulatory-blackout overlay — not a footnote. Multiple senior changes 2023–25 mean every entry attempt gets reset; calendar entry *after* a confirmed transition, not before. This is distinct from generic Ramadan/Eid blackouts and should be a separate Operating Constraints prompt for any PIF/sovereign-developer file.
  - methodology: The **greenfield charging-integration window** bullet (from RSG retrofit) is even more acute at NEOM because Sindalah is *already built* (2024) — the window is closing per-node, not per-cluster. Sub-cluster table should arguably tag each node with "spec window: open / closing / closed" for charging integration. This converts the greenfield framing into a time-pressured action item.
  - methodology: The **demand–engineering inversion** (calm-sea summer = no guests; rough-sea winter = peak guests) is now confirmed across three MENA retrofits (Dubai, AUH, NEOM). It is universal for Red Sea + Gulf and should be promoted from per-file note to a schema-level standing prompt under Daylight/tourist seasonality.
  - methodology: Seaplane VFR/daylight limits as a structural Navier advantage (from Jeddah/RSG learnings) applies cleanly at NEOM too (Fly Red Sea adjacency) — this is now a confirmed pattern across the entire Red Sea coast and should be a standing wedge bullet for any Red Sea or Maldives-style file, not re-derived each time.
  - source-discovered: NEOM ↔ AMAALA is ~200 nm — far out of N30 range. Adding to the running list of known out-of-range marquee routes: AUH↔Sir Bani Yas ~135 nm; Red Sea↔AMAALA ~80 nm; NEOM↔AMAALA ~200 nm. NEOM↔Oxagon ~50 nm is the new "edge of N30, plan charging at the far end" data point. These collectively define the N80 / hybrid-Quanta product case.
  - source-discovered: Trojena (alpine, Asian Winter Games 2029) is non-maritime and should be explicitly marked "N/A — non-maritime" in any NEOM sub-cluster table to prevent future agents from trying to force a wedge there.
  - schema-gap: Sub-clusters table would benefit from an explicit "spec window: open / closing / closed" column for charging-integration status at each node — converts the greenfield insight into a checklist.
  - schema-gap: Operating Constraints should explicitly prompt for **organizational-instability blackouts** (leadership churn at giga-project developers, e.g. NEOM CEO transitions), separate from religious/national-day blackouts. This is structurally different from the calendar blackouts and applies broadly to sovereign-developer markets (NEOM, RSG to a lesser extent, possibly Egypt's NUCA).
suggested-update:
  target-file: /agent/home/navier/world-map/_schema/CITY_TEMPLATE.md
  change: (1) In Sub-clusters table columns, add "Charging-spec window: open / closing / closed" for greenfield-context files. (2) In Operating Constraints > Regulatory bandwidth, add sub-bullet: "**Organizational-instability blackouts**: sovereign-developer leadership churn (NEOM-style CEO transitions) resets initiatives — sequence entry after confirmed transition windows." (3) In Daylight/tourist seasonality, add standing prompt: "Check for demand–engineering inversion (calm season ≠ peak demand season) — universal in MENA/Gulf/Red Sea; flag explicitly if present so pilot economics don't assume calm-season utilization."

date: 2026-05-27
subagent: navier-city-retrofit.md
task: Retrofitted Doha Qatar city file with Sub-clusters + Operating Constraints (multi-node bay-arc cluster).
learnings:
  - methodology: Doha is the **inverse N30 case** vs. AUH/NEOM/RSG — the entire operationally relevant cluster (Corniche → Katara → Pearl → Lusail → Banana Island → HIA) sits inside a ~12 nm arc, so range is *not* the constraint and multi-leg single-charge loops are feasible with margin. Worth flagging "cleanest-N30-fit MENA city" as a positioning datapoint when comparing pilot geographies — Doha trades range-headroom for political concentration risk, the opposite tradeoff from UAE.
  - methodology: For Gulf cities with both winter and summer shamal, split the wind bullet into two distinct events (winter = episodic 1–3 day high-intensity; summer = persistent moderate). Lumping them as "shamal" misses that the lost-day profiles and demand overlap are different — winter shamal eats into peak tourism, summer shamal doesn't because demand is already collapsed.
  - methodology: When a city has dense royal/Diwan waterfront residences overlooking the demo water (Doha West Bay, analogously parts of AUH Corniche and Jeddah), treat **Diwan-side awareness of demo timing/lighting/flight paths** as a soft *operational* constraint not just a relationship one — it gates the operating envelope independently of the formal Mwani/equivalent permit. Belongs in Operating Constraints, not just Holds.
  - source-discovered: Qatar grid (Kahramaa) is LNG-revenue subsidized and heavily over-built — grid capacity is *not* a constraint for marina DC fast-charge; the constraint is marina-pedestal spec and concession-holder cooperation. This inverts the usual "grid is the bottleneck" framing and is worth checking for other hydrocarbon-rich Gulf states (UAE, KSA, Kuwait, Bahrain) before assuming grid is the gate.
  - source-discovered: Qatar National Day (Dec 18) carries a ~1-week marine-traffic restriction in Doha Bay for naval parade rehearsals + day-of — heavier than analogous UAE National Day. Add to running MENA national-day blackout catalog.
  - source-discovered: F1 Qatar GP weekend + Qatar International Boat Show both impose West Bay marine-traffic restrictions but are *also* the marquee demo windows — same week is simultaneously a blackout and the highest-visibility opportunity. Worth a standing prompt: "for cities with marquee marine events, distinguish blackout-vs-demo-opportunity and plan dual-use of the window."
  - schema-gap: CITY_TEMPLATE Operating Constraints doesn't currently distinguish between "grid-constrained" and "marina-pedestal-constrained" charging stories. For hydrocarbon-rich states these are very different problems with different counterparties — worth a sub-prompt.
suggested-update:
  target-file: /agent/home/navier/world-map/_schema/CITY_TEMPLATE.md
  change: (1) In Operating Constraints > Charging/shore-power, add sub-prompt "Distinguish grid-capacity constraint (regulated utility, scarce) from marina-pedestal-spec constraint (concession-holder, retrofit). In hydrocarbon-rich states grid is usually over-built; the gate is the marina concession-holder." (2) Add standing prompt under Regulatory bandwidth: "For cities with marquee marine events (F1, boat shows, regattas), tag each as blackout-and-demo-opportunity and plan dual-use rather than treating as pure blackout." (3) Add to Operating Constraints a new sub-bullet "**Royal/Diwan-overlook constraint** — if demo water is overlooked by royal residences (Doha West Bay, AUH Corniche, Jeddah Corniche), treat Diwan-side timing/lighting awareness as an operational gate, not just a relationship one."

date: 2026-05-27
subagent: navier-city-retrofit.md
task: Retrofitted Manama Bahrain city file with Sub-clusters + Operating Constraints (small archipelago, all nodes inside N30).
learnings:
  - methodology: Bahrain is the **third confirmed "cleanest-N30-fit" MENA market** alongside Doha and intra-Dubai — every operationally relevant leg is comfortably in-range, including the diplomatically-gated Manama↔Al Khobar causeway corridor (~25 nm). Worth maintaining a running "low-engineering-risk pilot geography" list across MENA retrofits so pilot-geography ranking is evidence-based, not impressionistic.
  - methodology: For small-archipelago city files (Bahrain, analogously Bermuda, Malta, Bocas del Toro), Sub-clusters table still adds value even when all nodes are inside N30 — because the *commercial/regulatory* differentiation between nodes (royal-overlook, protected area, semi-greenfield marina) drives wedge selection even when range doesn't. Don't skip Sub-clusters just because the cluster is geometrically tight.
  - methodology: The "charging-spec window: open / closing / closed" column from NEOM retrofit transferred cleanly to Bahrain and was high-signal — Bahrain Marina (still under build-out, Mumtalakat-linked) was flagged as *open*, a directly actionable pre-commissioning lever. Should be standardized across all Sub-clusters tables, not just greenfield-heavy ones.
  - source-discovered: Bahrain EWA grid is LNG/hydrocarbon-subsidized and over-built — confirms the Qatar Kahramaa pattern that **hydrocarbon-rich Gulf states never have grid as the bottleneck**; the gate is always marina-pedestal spec + concession-holder. Now confirmed across Qatar, UAE (implicitly), Bahrain; safe to assume for Kuwait and KSA coastal cities too.
  - source-discovered: Bahrain is the **most culturally liberal Gulf jurisdiction** (alcohol-permitted, mixed-gender norms, no female-only requirement) — meaningfully reduces product-design surface vs. KSA. Flag as a *positive* sandbox attribute in any cross-MENA pilot-geography comparison.
  - source-discovered: Bahrain National Day Dec 16 + Accession Day Dec 17 = back-to-back two-day public holiday. Running MENA national-day blackout catalog: UAE Dec 2, Qatar Dec 18, KSA Sep 23 + Feb 22, Bahrain Dec 16–17.
  - schema-gap: CITY_TEMPLATE Operating Constraints would benefit from a sub-prompt on **"sandbox attributes"** — for jurisdictions positioned as regulatory sandboxes (Bahrain, Singapore-ish, Estonia, Rwanda), the *positive* attributes (cultural liberalism, fast regulator, small jurisdiction, single national authority, prior fintech/mobility sandbox track record) deserve a structured callout, not just being scattered across Snapshot prose.
  - schema-gap: A workspace-level **"MENA national-day + religious blackout calendar"** reference file would prevent each retrofit from rediscovering the same dates; could live at `/agent/home/navier/_reference/mena-blackout-calendar.md`.
suggested-update:
  target-file: /agent/home/navier/world-map/_schema/CITY_TEMPLATE.md
  change: (1) Standardize Sub-clusters table columns as "Sub-cluster | Role | Distance from anchor | N30 bucket | Charging-spec window (open/closing/closed) | What's distinct" — applies even when range is not the constraint. (2) Add Operating Constraints sub-bullet "**Sandbox attributes** (if applicable): for jurisdictions positioned as regulatory sandboxes, structure cultural liberalism, single national regulator, speed of decision, prior sandbox track record as a deliberate proof-point framing." (3) Create `/agent/home/navier/_reference/mena-blackout-calendar.md` as a shared reference and link retrofits to it instead of re-listing dates.

date: 2026-05-27
subagent: navier-city-retrofit.md
task: Retrofitted Singapore city file with Sub-clusters + Operating Constraints (tight multi-node + cross-border to Indonesia/Malaysia).
learnings:
  - methodology: Singapore is the **fourth confirmed "cleanest-N30-fit" market** (after Doha, Bahrain, intra-Dubai) — every operationally relevant leg including the cross-border volume play (Batam ~11 nm) and resort play (Bintan ~24 nm) sits comfortably inside N30. The running "low-engineering-risk pilot geography" list now spans MENA + SEA, so it's worth promoting to a standing reference file (`/agent/home/navier/_reference/n30-fit-markets.md`) rather than reconstructed per-retrofit.
  - methodology: For cross-border city files (Singapore↔ID/MY, future HK↔PRD, Miami↔Bahamas, Trieste↔Croatia), Sub-clusters must split by *counterparty jurisdiction* not just geography — the regulatory/charging/cultural counterparties differ per leg even when distances are short, and bundling them hides the real friction. The Singapore table treats each cross-border node as its own row with its own charging-spec window; this should be the standard pattern.
  - methodology: **Shipping-lane / TSS crossing constraint** (Singapore Strait, also relevant for Dover, Strait of Hormuz, Bosphorus, Malacca, Panama approaches, San Francisco Bay) deserves its own Operating Constraints bullet *separate from sea state* — it's an SOP / VTIS / situational-awareness constraint, not a wave-height constraint, and foil-up speed actually helps it. Worth a schema-level prompt for any city sitting on a major TSS.
  - methodology: The **"blackout + demo opportunity dual-use"** framing (from Doha F1/boat show) transferred cleanly to Singapore (NDP rehearsals, F1 SG GP, Singapore Maritime Week) — confirms it as a general MENA+SEA pattern, not Doha-specific. Should be promoted to schema-level standing prompt.
  - methodology: The **sandbox-attributes bullet** (from Bahrain retrofit) is *more* applicable to Singapore than to Bahrain — Singapore's MAS/CAAS/LTA sandbox precedents are the strongest case anywhere for "this regulator knows how to do a sandbox." Worth flagging Singapore as the gold-standard sandbox reference in cross-market comparisons.
  - methodology: **Marina-pedestal-spec gating** (from Qatar/Bahrain) holds in Singapore *despite* Singapore not being hydrocarbon-rich — the constraint structure (grid fine, concession-holder spec is the gate) is jurisdiction-agnostic for any market with a competent grid. Reframe the prior learning: it's not "hydrocarbon-rich Gulf" that makes grid non-binding, it's *any competent grid*; hydrocarbon-rich is just the easy heuristic.
  - methodology: **Year-round tourist demand** in equatorial markets (Singapore, future Bali, Penang, Phuket low-volatility months, Maldives shoulder) is the *inverse* of the MENA demand-engineering inversion — calm engineering AND steady demand, no off-season trough. This is a structural pilot-economics advantage worth flagging explicitly for any equatorial SEA file vs. MENA peers.
  - source-discovered: Singapore↔Batam ~11 nm allows ~3 round-trips per single charge with N30 — this is the strongest single-route volume economic case across any city file retrofitted to date (vs. MENA's typical 1-leg-per-charge constraint). Should anchor the Grab "GrabWater" pilot economics modeling.
  - source-discovered: **Sumatra squalls** (predawn 0400–0900 line squalls off Sumatra, SW monsoon Jun–Sep, 40–60 kt gusts + lightning) are the single biggest published-schedule disruption in Singapore waters, but mostly miss commercial day-ops window. Worth adding to a SEA-specific weather reference alongside NE/SW monsoon and haze season.
  - source-discovered: Singapore Strait TSS = world's 2nd-busiest shipping lane (~80,000+ transits/year); right-angle crossing protocol gated by VTIS. Real operational constraint for Batam/Bintan ops, not a sea-state problem. No other Navier market currently has comparable TSS constraint except possibly future Hong Kong / Istanbul / Panama files.
  - source-discovered: Singapore Maritime Week (early April) is the natural MPA-anchored demo window; Singapore government FY is Apr–Mar so budget commitments land Feb–Mar for following FY — sequencing MPA sandbox conversations to land *before* the Feb–Mar budget close is the high-leverage timing.
  - schema-gap: CITY_TEMPLATE has no prompt for **shipping-lane / TSS / VTIS constraints** which are first-order for any city on a major strait. Currently buried in sea state, which misframes it as a wave problem rather than an SOP/situational-awareness problem.
  - schema-gap: Sub-clusters table doesn't currently prompt for **counterparty jurisdiction** column for cross-border city files — should be added when any sub-cluster crosses a national border.
  - schema-gap: No standing reference file for the running "low-engineering-risk N30-fit pilot geographies" list (now 4 entries across 2 regions) or for the running "out-of-range marquee routes" list (now 3 MENA entries) — both are accumulating high-value cross-retrofit signal that should be promoted out of the learnings log.
suggested-update:
  target-file: /agent/home/navier/world-map/_schema/CITY_TEMPLATE.md
  change: (1) Add Operating Constraints sub-bullet "**Shipping-lane / TSS / VTIS constraint** — if the city sits on a major Traffic Separation Scheme (Singapore Strait, Dover, Bosphorus, Hormuz, Malacca, Panama approaches, SF Bay), treat right-angle-crossing SOP + VTIS coordination + deep-draft wake as a primary operational constraint separate from sea state." (2) For Sub-clusters tables in cross-border city files, add column "Counterparty jurisdiction" (e.g. MPA / Indonesian DGSC / Malaysian Marine Dept). (3) Reframe the marina-pedestal-spec learning at schema level: "**Charging-spec gate** — in any market with a competent grid (Gulf hydrocarbon, Singapore-class developed, Northern Europe), grid is rarely the bottleneck; the gate is marina-pedestal spec + concession-holder cooperation. Identify the concession-holder per node." (4) Promote "blackout + demo opportunity dual-use" to standing prompt under Regulatory bandwidth. (5) Create `/agent/home/navier/_reference/n30-fit-markets.md` and `/agent/home/navier/_reference/out-of-range-marquee-routes.md` as standing cross-retrofit reference files.

date: 2026-05-27
subagent: navier-city-retrofit.md
task: Retrofitted Phuket / Phang Nga / Krabi / Phi Phi / Koh Yao / Similan Thailand cluster with Sub-clusters + Operating Constraints (multi-node Andaman hospitality cluster).
learnings:
  - methodology: For **monsoon-driven Andaman / Bay-of-Bengal / SCS markets**, the demand–engineering windows *align* (calm NE monsoon = peak tourism; rough SW monsoon = low season) — the **opposite** of MENA's demand–engineering inversion. Structurally favorable for pilot economics; flag explicitly on any SEA monsoon-coast file as a positive vs. MENA peers. Confirmed for Phuket; expect to recur at Krabi, Langkawi, Penang, Sri Lanka, Maldives.
  - methodology: **DNP / national-park hard closures** (Similan + Surin closed by regulation mid-May to mid-Oct) cap certain itineraries at <6.5 productive months/yr regardless of weather — this is *regulatory* seasonality on top of weather seasonality and deserves its own sub-bullet, not lumped with monsoon. Likely also applies to Komodo, Raja Ampat, Tioman, Perhentian.
  - methodology: **Sub-cluster sea-state decomposition** matters more for SEA than MENA — Phang Nga Bay (sheltered karst, 0.3–0.8 m) and open-Andaman crossings (1.5–3 m+ SW monsoon) are an order of magnitude apart within the same file and drive completely different operability + wedge stories. Always split sea state by named sub-water-body when the cluster spans sheltered + exposed waters.
  - methodology: **Resort-jetty DC-charging spec window is per-resort, not per-cluster** — at hospitality-led clusters (Phuket, Maldives, Bali, Saudi Red Sea) the concession-holder for each remote jetty is the *individual resort owner*, not a marina operator. Sub-clusters table "Charging-spec window" should be tagged per-resort-node; Soneva/Aman/Six Senses expansion plans become spec-window intercept opportunities.
  - methodology: **Maya Bay / Phi Phi rotational closures + daily visitor quotas** are a new constraint class (rotating per-site caps, not blackout windows) — tag separately under National-park concession constraints; expect analog at Komodo, Sipadan, Galápagos, Lord Howe.
  - source-discovered: Phuket↔Surin ~80 nm — adds to running out-of-range marquee-route list (AUH↔Sir Bani Yas 135, Red Sea↔AMAALA 80, NEOM↔AMAALA 200, NEOM↔Oxagon ~50 edge, Phuket↔Surin ~80). List now spans MENA + SEA — justifies the `_reference/out-of-range-marquee-routes.md` file proposed earlier.
  - source-discovered: Phang Nga Bay entirely inside N30 with multi-leg loop headroom — adds to "low-engineering-risk N30-fit" list alongside Doha, Bahrain, intra-Dubai, Singapore. Phuket joins specifically for the *hospitality* wedge (intra-Phang-Nga loops); the open-Andaman day-trip wedge (Phi Phi/Krabi) is in-range but with weather-margin pressure.
  - source-discovered: Thai Songkran (~Apr 13–15) is simultaneously a 1-week government blackout AND the highest-visibility domestic tourism week — classic dual-use blackout/demo-opportunity window per Doha/Singapore pattern. Also Loy Krathong (Nov full moon).
  - source-discovered: **Longtail boat cooperatives** at each Thai pier are politically protected — analogous to gondolieri Venice, abra drivers Dubai. Worth a standing "protected indigenous water-craft cooperative" prompt for any port where they exist, because it shapes route framing (private/premium vs. public/scheduled).
  - schema-gap: CITY_TEMPLATE Operating Constraints has no prompt distinguishing **weather seasonality** from **regulatory seasonality** (DNP/MPA hard closures). At hospitality clusters with national-park overlap (Phuket, Komodo, Galápagos, Maldives marine reserves, Bonaire) the regulatory cap is often tighter than the weather cap.
  - schema-gap: No prompt for **rotating per-site visitor quotas** (Maya Bay model) — these constrain day-trip product volume independent of fleet size.
  - schema-gap: A SEA-region reference file (`/agent/home/navier/_reference/sea-monsoon-and-blackout-calendar.md`) parallel to the proposed MENA one would prevent each SEA retrofit from re-deriving Songkran / Loy Krathong / NE-vs-SW monsoon dates / DNP closures / Eid (Indonesia/Malaysia/southern Thailand).
suggested-update:
  target-file: /agent/home/navier/world-map/_schema/CITY_TEMPLATE.md
  change: (1) Split Operating Constraints seasonality into two bullets: **Weather seasonality** (monsoon/typhoon/hurricane/khareef/shamal/ice) AND **Regulatory seasonality** (national-park hard closures, marine-reserve seasons, rotating site quotas like Maya Bay). (2) Add to Daylight/tourist seasonality the standing question: "Do demand and engineering windows *align* (SEA monsoon-coast pattern) or *invert* (MENA Gulf/Red Sea pattern)? Drives whether pilot economics should assume calm-season utilization." (3) Add Operating Constraints sub-bullet "**Protected indigenous water-craft cooperatives** (longtails Thailand, gondolieri Venice, abras Dubai, batel Goa) — where they exist, route framing must be premium/private tier; visibly displacing them invites political pushback." (4) Create `/agent/home/navier/_reference/sea-monsoon-and-blackout-calendar.md` as the SEA parallel to the proposed MENA blackout calendar.

date: 2026-05-27
subagent: navier-city-retrofit.md
task: Retrofitted Malé/Maldives national file with Sub-clusters (7 rows) + Operating Constraints (multi-atoll hospitality archipelago).
learnings:
  - methodology: For **archipelago-national** files (Maldives, future Seychelles, BVI, Indonesia, Philippines), Sub-clusters must be organized by **distance-band from the single national gateway** rather than by geographic name — because in archipelagos the gateway is structurally singular (MLE for Maldives, Malé equivalent in others) and every route is a radial from that anchor. This forces the N30 bucket column to do real work (in-range / edge / out-of-range) and makes the table the master pilot-geography ranking, not a glossary.
  - methodology: **Atoll-pass (kandu) tidal-current constraint** is a Maldives-specific geometry constraint that doesn't fit cleanly under "sea state" — it's a tide-window scheduling constraint with strong tidal flows (3–5 kt) at reef-pass mouths. Should be flagged whenever a market involves atoll/lagoon-pass geometry (Maldives, French Polynesia, Marshall Islands, parts of Seychelles, Bahamas cuts). Generalizes the Jeddah fringing-reef bullet — there it was depth+clearance; here it's also current+tide-windowing.
  - methodology: **Equatorial daylight is not the constraint — seaplane VFR cutoff is.** At Maldives / RSG / NEOM / BVI / Phuket, the per-day daylight is ~12 hours year-round and not a Navier limit; what matters is that seaplane incumbents stop ~30 min before sunset. The N+30 vs N-30 latitude framing misses this. Standing wedge framing for any seaplane-incumbent market: "night-capable + all-weather transfer is the only credible product gap" — not "Navier is faster" (often it isn't, vs. seaplane on long legs).
  - methodology: **Resort-island microgrid charging story is qualitatively different from marina-pedestal-spec story.** At resort archipelagos (Maldives, French Polynesia, parts of Indonesia) there is no national grid linking islands — each is its own diesel-genset microgrid with variable solar. So "marina concession-holder gate" (from Qatar/Bahrain/Singapore learnings) becomes "**resort owner gate + genset/solar headroom check + saltwater pedestal spec**" — three separate gates per node, not one. Sub-clusters table charging-spec window must be per-resort, not per-cluster. Soneva/Aman/Six Senses renovation cycles are the intercept windows.
  - methodology: **Maldives is the cleanest SEA-pattern demand-engineering alignment** — Dec–Apr is simultaneously the dry/calm engineering window AND the tourist peak; May–Nov is wet+shoulder. Confirms the SEA-coast alignment pattern (vs. MENA inversion) and *strengthens* it — Maldives sits below the cyclone belt so no typhoon overlay either. Strongest possible pilot-economics setup in any market retrofitted so far. Worth flagging in cross-market pilot-geography comparisons.
  - methodology: **Cultural-protocol constraint inversion** — Maldives is the rare case where the *country* is 100% Muslim with alcohol/modesty constraints, but the *resort islands* are legally zoned exempt. So unlike KSA, the guest-facing product on resort water is unconstrained. Worth structuring as a "**zoned cultural exemption**" sub-prompt — applies to Maldives resort islands, Saudi RSG/AMAALA tourism zones (alcohol pending), possibly Brunei tourism zones. Different from "sandbox attributes" (Bahrain) and from full cultural constraint (KSA mainland).
  - source-discovered: **Maldives → out-of-range marquee routes** (extending running list): Cheval Blanc Randheli ~81 nm (out-of-range single-leg, range-gates the LVMH wedge), Soneva Fushi / Four Seasons Landaa / Anantara Kihavah ~65–70 nm (N30 edge), Six Senses Laamu ~135 nm (far out-of-range, N80-only). Premium-tier customers + real distances + no good incumbent alternative = strongest cross-retrofit argument for N80 / hybrid-Quanta product positioning.
  - source-discovered: **Maldives sub-equatorial = no cyclones.** Unique among major SEA / Bay-of-Bengal markets — Sri Lanka, Andaman, Bangladesh, Myanmar coast all sit above the cyclone track. Structural Navier-favorable attribute worth flagging in any Maldives positioning vs. Phuket/Sri Lanka peers.
  - source-discovered: **Hulhumalé Phase II reclamation is the only credible greenfield charging/MRO hub site** in Maldives — national grid (Stelco), MPL adjacency, room for marine-industrial parcels. Different model from per-resort jetty charging; this is the *shared* hub piece of the asset-holder thesis. Worth a dedicated entry in any future Maldives subagent payload.
  - source-discovered: **TMA / Blackstone is both incumbent and template** in a way no other Navier market replicates — the asset-holder PE play (Blackstone-TMA-DB-$1.7B-exit) is literally the on-the-ground template Navier wants to copy on water. Worth tagging as a *unique* market attribute, not just an "incumbent" — it pre-validates the asset-holder thesis with the same financiers Navier wants.
  - source-discovered: **Maldives politics swings India-friendly vs. China-friendly per administration** (Solih → Muizzu 2023, etc.) — commercial commitments survive but new licensing initiatives reset. Different blackout pattern from MENA religious calendar; sequence new licensing asks for early/mid administration term, not pre-election or first-100-days.
  - schema-gap: CITY_TEMPLATE has no prompt for **single-gateway archipelago geometry** (one national entry point, radial spokes). For Maldives, Seychelles, BVI, this changes the Sub-clusters table from geographic listing to distance-banded radial — worth a schema sub-prompt.
  - schema-gap: No prompt for **atoll-pass / cut tidal-current scheduling constraint** — distinct from open sea state and from fringing-reef-depth (Jeddah). Applies to atoll markets globally.
  - schema-gap: No prompt for **zoned cultural exemption** (resort islands in Muslim-majority countries; KSA tourism zones; etc.) — currently the cultural-constraint bullet (added per Jeddah retrofit) assumes uniform application. Reality is often zoned.
suggested-update:
  target-file: /agent/home/navier/world-map/_schema/CITY_TEMPLATE.md
  change: (1) Add Sub-clusters sub-prompt for **single-gateway archipelago files** (Maldives, Seychelles, BVI, FP, parts of Indonesia/Philippines): "Organize sub-clusters as distance-banded radials from the single national gateway, not by named geography — forces the N30 bucket column to function as the master pilot-geography ranking." (2) Add Operating Constraints sub-bullet under sea state: "**Atoll-pass / channel-cut tidal currents** — at atoll/lagoon-pass geometries (Maldives kandus, Tuamotus passes, Bahamas cuts), tidal flows of 3–5 kt at pass mouths create a tide-window scheduling constraint distinct from wave/swell sea state." (3) Refine the cultural-protocol bullet (from Jeddah retrofit) to add: "Check whether protocol applies *uniformly* (KSA mainland, Iran) or is *zoned-exempt* on tourism islands/zones (Maldives resort islands, Saudi RSG/AMAALA, future Brunei tourism zones) — zoned exemption inverts the constraint for guest-facing product on resort water." (4) Standardize seaplane-incumbent wedge framing: "At any seaplane-incumbent market (Maldives, RSG, NEOM, BVI, Vancouver-Harbour-Air), the credible product gap is *night-capable + all-weather transfer*, not 'faster than seaplane' — pilot positioning should lead with the daylight/VFR cutoff, not speed."

date: 2026-05-27
subagent: navier-city-retrofit.md
task: Retrofitted Jakarta Indonesia file with Sub-clusters + Operating Constraints (multi-node: HQ city + Thousand Islands archipelago + out-of-range markers for Bintan/Batam).
learnings:
  - methodology: For files where the anchor city is HQ-relevant but *not* the launch waterway (Jakarta for GoTo, analogously Mumbai for several SEA majors, future HCMC for Grab/VinFast), Sub-clusters table should explicitly tag the anchor as "HQ + departure terminal only — not a passenger commute corridor" to prevent future agents from re-pitching the broken intra-city wedge. This is a distinct sub-cluster role beyond gateway/hub/spoke; worth adding "HQ-only node" as a recognized role.
  - methodology: **Debris / floating-flotsam as a foil-strike operational hazard** (Jakarta Bay, analogously parts of Manila Bay, Lagos Lagoon, Mumbai harbour, Ganges delta) is a Navier-specific constraint that doesn't fit cleanly under sea state (it's not a wave-height issue) or under regulatory (it's not a permit issue). Deserves its own Operating Constraints sub-bullet for any harbor with major river-outflow plastic load or active fishing/aquaculture clutter.
  - methodology: **Out-of-range pitch traps** — partners in archipelago nations (Indonesia, Philippines, Greece, Caribbean) repeatedly suggest cross-archipelago direct routes (Jakarta↔Bali, Manila↔Boracay, Athens↔Mykonos direct) that are 400–600+ nm and impossible at any current/N80 Navier spec. Worth a standing "known out-of-range pitch traps" entry in any archipelago-nation file so the conversation can be redirected gracefully rather than freshly re-litigated each meeting.
  - methodology: **Informal zoned cultural exemption** (Jakarta urban moderate + Bali Hindu + Aceh strict, all in one country) is distinct from *legal* zoned exemption (Maldives resort islands, KSA tourism zones). Both fall under the cultural-constraint bullet but the operational implication differs: informal zoning means norms shift by geography but you don't get statutory cover, so product design should still be conservative-defaultable. Worth distinguishing in the schema sub-prompt.
  - source-discovered: Indonesia just transitioned to Prabowo administration (Oct 2024) — ministerial seats including Kemenhub still settling through 2025–26; sovereign-track conversations (INA, Danantara, Pelni, PT PAL) should be sequenced after visible policy clarity. Parallel to NEOM-style organizational-instability blackout; this pattern (new administration → 6–12 month sovereign blackout) is now confirmed across NEOM (giga-project) and Jakarta (national executive) — likely generalizable.
  - source-discovered: Indonesia Lebaran (Eid al-Fitr) week is paradoxically *low* demand for resort water — domestic tourists head to home villages (Mudik), not to Thousand Islands / Bali. So Lebaran is a *triple* event: government blackout + national mass-mobility (road/ferry safety priority) + resort-demand trough. Distinct from Gulf Eid pattern where peak hospitality demand actually rises during Eid.
  - source-discovered: Java Sea sits *below* the Western Pacific typhoon track — no typhoon overlay on Jakarta operations. Structural advantage vs. Manila/Cebu (Philippines) and southern Japan. Worth flagging in any cross-SEA pilot-geography comparison.
  - source-discovered: Indonesian fiscal year = calendar year (unusual for SEA — Singapore Apr–Mar, Thailand Oct–Sep); APBN budget review peaks Aug–Oct in DPR (parliament); sovereign asks land best Q1 or early Q3. Worth maintaining a SEA fiscal-calendar reference alongside blackout calendars.
  - schema-gap: CITY_TEMPLATE Sub-clusters role types ("gateway/hub/spoke/leisure node") doesn't include **"HQ-only node"** for cities where the anchor is commercially central but not a viable launch waterway. Add as a recognized role.
  - schema-gap: Operating Constraints has no dedicated bullet for **debris / floating-flotsam foil-strike hazard** at major river-outflow / fishing-clutter harbors — currently miscategorized under sea state. Important Navier-specific operational gate.
  - schema-gap: Cultural-protocol bullet should distinguish **legal-zoned-exempt** vs. **informal-zoned-norm** exemption — the operational implication differs (statutory cover vs. norm-following).
  - schema-gap: Sub-clusters tables would benefit from explicitly listing **known out-of-range partner-pitched routes** as a row tagged "OUT OF SCOPE — pitch trap" to graceful-redirect future conversations rather than rediscover.
suggested-update:
  target-file: /agent/home/navier/world-map/_schema/CITY_TEMPLATE.md
  change: (1) Expand Sub-clusters role taxonomy to include "**HQ-only node** — commercially central but not a launch waterway; departure terminal at best." (2) Add Operating Constraints sub-bullet "**Debris / floating-flotsam foil-strike hazard** — at major river-outflow harbors (Jakarta Bay, Manila Bay, Mumbai harbour, Lagos Lagoon, Ganges delta) plastic + timber + net flotsam is a Navier-specific operational gate distinct from sea state." (3) Refine cultural-constraint bullet to distinguish *legal* zoned exemption (Maldives, KSA RSG) from *informal* zoned norm (Indonesia: Aceh strict / Jakarta moderate / Bali Hindu) — the latter still requires conservative-defaultable product design. (4) For archipelago-nation files, add Sub-clusters convention: include a row tagged "OUT OF SCOPE — known partner-pitched out-of-range route" (e.g. Jakarta↔Bali 600 nm) to graceful-redirect repeated conversations.

date: 2026-05-27
subagent: navier-city-retrofit.md
task: Retrofitted Bangkok Thailand city file with Sub-clusters + Operating Constraints (multi-node: Chao Phraya urban spine + Upper Gulf leisure arc Pattaya/Hua Hin/Samet/Chang + airport intermodal).
learnings:
  - methodology: **Forced two-tier pilot architecture** — Bangkok is the first retrofitted file where the anchor city does NOT directly reach its own leisure cluster within N30 (BKK↔Hua Hin 90 nm out, BKK↔Samet 130 nm out direct, but Pattaya ~80 nm is edge-of-N30 and itself serves Samet ~25 nm / Chang ~70 nm in-range). The architecture is forcibly *line-haul-to-secondary-hub + secondary-hub-to-spokes*, not anchor-direct. This pattern likely recurs at: Mumbai↔Goa/Alibaug, Tokyo↔Izu/Hayama, HCMC↔Phu Quoc, Lima↔Paracas/Pisco, Lagos↔Lekki/Badagry. Schema-level prompt warranted.
  - methodology: **Per-pier concession fragmentation** on the Chao Phraya (BMA-public, ICONSIAM-CP/Siam-Piwat/Magnolia, Asiatique-TCC/AWC, Peninsula/MO/FS-private) means the marina-pedestal-spec gate (from Qatar/Bahrain/Singapore learnings) becomes a **per-pier conversation matrix**, not a single concession-holder dialogue. Sub-clusters charging-spec window must be tagged per-pier in dense urban-river files. Same pattern likely at Venice (per-palazzo), Mumbai (per-jetty), HCMC (per-developer-pier), Hong Kong (HKMW + per-private-club).
  - methodology: **Calendar-predictable seasonal foil-debris events** — extends the Jakarta stochastic-debris learning with two Bangkok-specific phenomena: (a) Aug–Oct upstream water-hyacinth mat releases (annually predictable), (b) one-night Loy Krathong krathong-flotilla blackout (calendar-fixed). Schedulable, not stochastic — pilot SOPs can plan. Likely analog: Hindu river festivals on Ganges/Mumbai (Ganesh Chaturthi, Chhath), Mekong floating-lantern festivals.
  - methodology: **Historical jetfoil graveyard signal** — multiple Bangkok↔Pattaya hydrofoil/jetfoil services have launched and failed over decades; recurring lesson is demand thesis right, *unit economics killed each attempt*. Retrofits of any corridor with documented jetfoil graveyard (Bangkok-Pattaya, Tokyo-Izu, HK-Macau pre-Cotai, NY-Atlantic City) should explicitly flag "operating-economics failure mode" as primary pilot risk, not demand. Worth a standing Operating Constraints sub-prompt.
  - methodology: **Demand–engineering alignment + urban-commute floor** — Bangkok stacks favorable SEA monsoon-alignment (cf. Phuket/Maldives) with an *additional* dampener: a year-round commute/transit floor on the Chao Phraya that smooths off-season utilization risk. Strongest demand profile of any retrofitted SEA file so far — better than pure-leisure Phuket/Maldives, better than HQ-only Jakarta. Worth flagging in cross-SEA pilot-geography ranking.
  - source-discovered: Bangkok↔Pattaya ~80 nm sits at the *exact* edge of N30 — neither comfortably in nor clearly out — making Pattaya the ideal proof-point for "edge-of-N30 with hub charging" as a product story. Catalogue: Dubai↔Ghantoot ~45 nm in-range, Jeddah↔KAEC ~65 nm edge, NEOM↔Oxagon ~50 nm edge, BKK↔Pattaya ~80 nm edge.
  - source-discovered: **Upper Gulf of Thailand sits above the Western Pacific typhoon main track** — only southern Gulf sees rare landfalls (Pabuk 2019). Confirms Java-Sea / Andaman-coast pattern that most SEA non-Philippine markets avoid typhoon overlay. Philippines is the SEA typhoon outlier.
  - source-discovered: Thai FY Oct–Sep — third unique SEA FY pattern after Singapore Apr–Mar and Indonesia Jan–Dec. SEA-FY reference file would now have three concrete patterns documented.
  - source-discovered: **Royal-palace overlook constraint** at Hua Hin (Klai Kangwon) + Grand Palace / Wat Arun on Chao Phraya — Bangkok confirms the Doha Diwan-overlook pattern generalizes to any monarchy with active waterfront residences. Now confirmed across Doha West Bay, AUH Corniche, Jeddah Corniche, Bangkok Grand Palace + Hua Hin. Promote to schema-level standing prompt.
  - source-discovered: Thai political instability (coups 2006/2014, repeated PM changes, constitutional court interventions) is recurring NEOM-style organizational-instability blackout. Catalogue: NEOM CEO churn, Jakarta Prabowo 2024 transition, Bangkok Pheu Thai still settling 2026. Pattern: any market with sub-5-year average government tenure = perpetual rolling sovereign-track blackout.
  - schema-gap: No prompt for **"forced two-tier pilot architecture"** when anchor city cannot single-leg reach its leisure cluster — currently buried in N30 implications.
  - schema-gap: No prompt distinguishing **calendar-predictable** from **stochastic** seasonal foil-debris events.
  - schema-gap: No prompt for **historical jetfoil graveyards** on a corridor — mandatory check for prior failed fast-ferry attempts.
  - schema-gap: SEA fiscal-year + blackout reference file (proposed in Phuket learnings) now has three documented distinct FY patterns — strong case to actually create it.
suggested-update:
  target-file: /agent/home/navier/world-map/_schema/CITY_TEMPLATE.md
  change: (1) Add Sub-clusters convention: "**Forced two-tier architecture check** — if the anchor city cannot single-leg reach its leisure cluster within N30, identify the secondary hub (Pattaya for Bangkok-Gulf, Trat for Koh Chang, etc.) and treat it as a co-anchor in Sub-clusters with its own charging-spec window and concession-holder. Don't try to force a direct line-haul story." (2) In Operating Constraints debris bullet, split into "**stochastic harbor debris** (Jakarta/Manila/Lagos)" vs. "**calendar-predictable seasonal foil-debris events** (Chao Phraya hyacinth Aug–Oct, Loy Krathong, Ganesh Chaturthi idol immersion, monsoon timber surges)" — schedulable vs. stochastic drives different SOPs. (3) Add Operating Constraints sub-prompt: "**Historical jetfoil/hydrofoil graveyard check** — for any corridor with prior failed fast-ferry attempts (Bangkok↔Pattaya, Tokyo↔Izu, HK↔Macau pre-Cotai, NY↔Atlantic City), document root cause (demand vs. unit economics vs. weather) before claiming corridor as pilot route." (4) Promote royal/Diwan-overlook constraint from Doha-specific to schema-level prompt for any monarchy with waterfront residences (confirmed across Qatar, UAE, KSA, Thailand). (5) Create `/agent/home/navier/_reference/sea-monsoon-and-blackout-calendar.md` and `/agent/home/navier/_reference/sea-fiscal-calendar.md` now that three SEA retrofits have documented distinct patterns.

date: 2026-05-27
subagent: navier-city-retrofit.md
task: Retrofitted Bali Indonesia (Bali + Nusa + Gili + Lombok + Sumba + Komodo) with Sub-clusters (7 rows incl. OUT-OF-SCOPE Komodo redirect + Banyuwangi MRO node) + Operating Constraints.
learnings:
  - methodology: **Demand–engineering *anti-*alignment within a single market** — Bali is the first retrofitted SEA market where the SEA-coast alignment pattern (dry-season = tourist peak = calm water) partially *breaks*: SE trade winds (Angin Timur) roughen Badung Strait *during* peak Jul–Aug tourist season. So the high-volume Sanur↔Lembongan leg has its worst sea state precisely when guest demand peaks. Stronger framing than binary "aligned vs. inverted" — markets can have **per-corridor alignment inversion** even when the regional pattern aligns. Schema sub-prompt should ask not just "do windows align" but "does any *specific high-volume corridor* invert the regional pattern". Likely analog: Cape Town summer SE Cape Doctor on Table Bay, Greek Aegean Meltemi Jul–Aug.
  - methodology: **Nyepi as statutory 24-hour absolute provincial blackout** is a Navier-unique constraint class — not a weather window, not a regulatory procurement blackout, but a *hard provincial operational blackout* with no commercial exemption (Ngurah Rai airport literally closes). Different from Ramadan rhythm-shifts, different from typhoon waits. Worth a distinct sub-bullet: "hard cultural/religious provincial operational blackouts" — likely analog: Bhutan certain Buddhist days, Thai royal mourning periods, partial Catalan Diada in Barcelona, Tibet certain festivals. Annual Nyepi date is the natural maintenance window — operationally clarifying.
  - methodology: **Lombok Strait ITF (Indonesian Throughflow) tidal currents** at 2–4 kt is the second documented atoll/pass-style tide-window scheduling constraint after Maldives kandus. Generalizes the constraint beyond pure-atoll geometries to any narrow strait between major basins (Lombok Strait connects Pacific-Indian basins; analogs: Pemba Channel, Strait of Tiran, parts of Sunda Strait, Bahamas cuts). Worth promoting from atoll-specific to "**basin-connecting strait / pass tidal-window constraint**" in schema.
  - methodology: **Per-resort jetty + Banjar (village council) layer** at hospitality nodes in Bali adds a *third* layer below the pier concession-holder gate: pier owner → resort owner → local village banjar. Most Bali resort jetties cross banjar customary land or use beachfront under banjar adat rules. Generalizes per-pier concession matrix (Bangkok) and per-resort genset gate (Maldives) to a *three-tier* gate: regulator → asset owner → local custodian. Likely analog: Hawaii hui, Fijian iTaukei, Indian gram panchayat, Maori iwi.
  - methodology: **Pelindo merger context (2021 Pelindo I-IV merger into single Pelindo)** is now the single state port operator handling Benoa + national footprint — concentrates the marina/cruise-port conversation into one counterparty instead of four. Worth flagging as commercial simplification for any Indonesian port-anchored wedge. Same monopoly-state-operator pattern as KSA Mawani, UAE AD Ports / DP World — but distinct in being recently consolidated; relationship-mapping is fresh.
  - source-discovered: **Bali↔Sumba (NIHI) ~210 nm + Bali↔Komodo ~250+ nm** — adds two more out-of-range marquee routes to the running cross-retrofit list (now: AUH↔Sir Bani Yas 135, Red Sea↔AMAALA 80, NEOM↔AMAALA 200, Phuket↔Surin 80, Cheval Blanc Randheli 81, Six Senses Laamu 135, Bali↔Sumba 210, Bali↔Komodo 250, Jakarta↔Bali 600). The list is now strong enough to justify creating `_reference/out-of-range-marquee-routes.md` immediately — it would drive both N80 product positioning and the "graceful redirect" Sub-clusters convention.
  - source-discovered: **North Sea Boats / Lundin Industry Invest (Banyuwangi, East Java)** is the only credible composite + electric MRO partner in the Indonesian archipelago — military patrol-craft carbon-fiber builder, ~70 nm from Benoa across Bali Strait. Strategic, not commercial — but it means a Bali pilot can have *in-archipelago* refit support, unlike Maldives (must ferry to Sri Lanka/Singapore) or Phuket (Singapore yards). Quietly important industrial geography.
  - source-discovered: **Bali island-wide PLN grid blackout August 2024** — full provincial outage; relevant context for any "grid-tied charging hub" pitch. Resort microgrids (Aman, Bulgari, Soori) held up; PLN-only fast-boat terminals (Sanur new terminal) went dark. Reinforces per-resort-microgrid charging story over centralized PLN-pedestal story for Bali.
  - source-discovered: **Bali jukung outriggers** at Lembongan are *seaweed-farming* work boats, not fishing — Lembongan seaweed farming is itself a declining livelihood being displaced by tourism. Different framing from Thai longtails (passenger) or Mumbai koli (fishing): jukung displacement is part of a *broader* livelihood transition story Navier can engage with constructively (community benefit framing) rather than purely defend against.
  - source-discovered: **Sanur Terminal (Pelabuhan Sanur)** opened October 2022, ~IDR 470bn Pelindo/Kemenhub investment — purpose-built modern fast-boat terminal with formal ticketing, dwell space, security. This is the closest thing to a "modern fast-boat infrastructure showcase" in SEA outside Singapore; concession framework is *new* and uncluttered — much better wedge for shore-power pilot than retrofitting a legacy port. Adds to greenfield-charging-hub catalogue (Hulhumalé Phase II Maldives, Mandalika Lombok, now Sanur Terminal Bali).
  - schema-gap: Schema should distinguish **regional demand-engineering alignment** from **per-corridor alignment inversion** — Bali shows you can have macro-alignment but specific high-volume corridors invert.
  - schema-gap: No schema entry for **hard provincial cultural blackouts** (Nyepi, royal mourning, Bhutan Buddhist days) — distinct from procurement blackouts and weather windows; statutory and absolute.
  - schema-gap: Concession-holder gate should be expanded to **three-tier**: regulator → asset owner → local customary custodian (banjar/hui/iwi/panchayat). Currently schema implies one or two tiers.
  - schema-gap: Atoll-pass tide-window constraint (added per Maldives retrofit) should be broadened to **basin-connecting strait / pass tidal-window** to cover Lombok Strait, Pemba Channel, Strait of Tiran, Sunda Strait.
  - schema-gap: Standing schema prompt for **prior fast-ferry safety/casualty history** at any incumbent-served corridor (Bali Lembongan capsizings, Padang Bai 2016, Gili 2018) — not just unit-economics graveyard but safety-record context which shapes pilot positioning and PR posture.
  - efficiency: Three SEA retrofits in rapid sequence (Jakarta, Bangkok, Bali) confirm the SEA monsoon/blackout/FY reference file would now save material work on each subsequent SEA market (Manila, HCMC, KL, Singapore-retrofit). High-value action.
suggested-update:
  target-file: /agent/home/navier/world-map/_schema/CITY_TEMPLATE.md
  change: (1) Refine the demand–engineering alignment prompt (proposed in Phuket retrofit) to ask *two* questions: "Does the *regional* monsoon pattern align with tourist peak?" AND "Does any *specific high-volume corridor* invert the regional pattern (Badung Strait SE-trade roughening in Bali peak season, Aegean Meltemi in Greek peak)?". (2) Add Operating Constraints sub-bullet "**Hard provincial cultural/religious operational blackouts** — distinct from procurement blackouts and weather windows: statutory absolute provincial closures with no commercial exemption (Bali Nyepi 24hr, Bhutan Buddhist days, Thai royal mourning weeks). These are the natural annual maintenance windows; plan accordingly." (3) Expand concession-holder framing to **three-tier**: regulator → asset owner → local customary custodian (Bali banjar, Hawaii hui, Fijian iTaukei, Maori iwi, Indian gram panchayat). Currently buried in cultural-protocol bullet. (4) Broaden the Maldives-derived atoll-pass tide-window prompt to "**basin-connecting strait / pass tidal-window scheduling constraint** — any narrow strait between major basins (Lombok Strait ITF 2–4 kt, Pemba Channel, Sunda Strait, Bahamas cuts, Strait of Tiran) creates a tide-window scheduling constraint distinct from open sea state." (5) Add Operating Constraints sub-prompt: "**Prior fast-ferry casualty record** at incumbent-served corridors (Padang Bai 2016, Gili 2018, Mediterranean / Aegean ferry incidents) — informs both pilot positioning (lead with comfort/reliability/safety) and PR posture (never weaponize competitor safety in writing)." (6) **Create `/agent/home/navier/_reference/out-of-range-marquee-routes.md` now** — list crosses 9 documented routes spanning MENA + SEA + Maldives, strong enough to centralize. (7) **Create `/agent/home/navier/_reference/sea-monsoon-and-blackout-calendar.md` + `sea-fiscal-calendar.md` now** — four SEA retrofits documented (Phuket, Jakarta, Bangkok, Bali) with distinct patterns each.

date: 2026-05-27
subagent: navier-city-retrofit.md
task: Retrofitted Hong Kong city file — single-node (no Sub-clusters), added Operating Constraints with corridor-specific sea-state, typhoon SOPs, club-microgrid charging architecture, and HK-Macau edge-of-N30 analysis.
learnings:
  - methodology: **Edge-of-N30 *cross-border* corridor as a distinct constraint class** — HK↔Macau (~37 nm one-way / 74 nm round-trip) sits inside N30 with effectively zero margin AND crosses a separate maritime jurisdiction (Macau Maritime Authority). This compounds the BKK↔Pattaya / Jeddah↔KAEC edge-of-N30 pattern with a *jurisdictional* gate (turn-side pedestal requires foreign-regulator concession). Catalogue: HK↔Macau, future Doha↔Bahrain (~75 nm cross-border), Singapore↔Batam (~25 nm cross-border but easy range). Worth flagging as "edge-of-N30 + cross-jurisdiction" sub-class.
  - methodology: **T8+ typhoon-signal port shutdown is a *statutory* blackout, not a weather window** — Marine Dept rule mandates total ferry suspension at T8+. Operationally equivalent to Bali Nyepi (statutory absolute closure with no commercial exemption) but stochastic-triggered rather than calendar-fixed. Generalizes the Bali "hard provincial cultural/religious blackouts" learning into a parallel "**hard statutory weather-trigger blackouts**" class — Typhoon T8 in HK/Macau, JMA Bosai warnings in Japan, NDRRMC Signal 3 in PH, USCG Port Condition Zulu in Gulf/SE US. Distinct from "we choose not to sail" weather windows.
  - methodology: **N30 non-binding for intra-city + binding for the city's *one* premium-corridor extension** is a specific HK pattern worth naming. All intra-HK legs <25 nm (range is a non-issue); HK↔Macau is the city's signature premium route AND the only N30-stressing leg. Same pattern probably at: Singapore (intra-island non-issue, Singapore↔Batam/Bintan the stressing leg), Doha (intra-bay non-issue, Doha↔Bahrain the stressing leg), Miami (intra-bay non-issue, Miami↔Bimini the stressing leg). Schema sub-prompt: "is range binding *only* on the cross-border premium corridor?"
  - methodology: **Cross-harbour pier slotting as a pre-commercial gate** — at Central Piers, getting a slot for a new commute route is a TD concession allocation conversation that must precede any schedule reality. Mirrors per-pier concession fragmentation (Bangkok) and three-tier custodian gate (Bali) but the gate here is *slot scarcity at a single TD-managed pier complex*, not custodial-rights diversity. Worth flagging as a fourth pier-access archetype: (a) per-pier concession matrix (Bangkok), (b) per-resort jetty + banjar (Bali), (c) resort-microgrid (Maldives/PH), (d) **slot-allocated public pier complex** (HK Central Piers, possibly Sydney Circular Quay, Istanbul Eminönü, Mumbai Gateway). Different counterparty, different bandwidth, different ask.
  - methodology: **HK as the cleanest "club/marina-microgrid pilot, public-pier deferred" case** — Royal HK Yacht Club, Aberdeen Marina Club, Discovery Bay Marina Club, Gold Coast all have pedestals (no fast-charge); installation at private clubs is a membership-board conversation (months), at TD-managed Central Piers is a capex cycle (5+ years). Pilot architecture should explicitly route through clubs first and treat any commute pilot as DB Marina-anchored, not Central Pier-anchored. Mirrors PH/Maldives resort-microgrid pattern in a non-resort city — extending the microgrid pattern to **yacht-club microgrid** as a recognised charging-architecture sub-type.
  - source-discovered: **HKSAR fiscal year Apr–Mar** (UK-legacy) — adds another distinct East Asia FY datapoint alongside Japan Apr–Mar, Singapore Apr–Mar, Thailand Oct–Sep, Indonesia/PH Jan–Dec. East Asia FY-calendar pattern set now spans HK + Japan + Singapore at Apr–Mar (Commonwealth/Japan-aligned).
  - source-discovered: **HK Observatory typhoon-signal system (T1/T3/T8/T9/T10)** is the most standardized statutory typhoon-response trigger in any retrofitted market — operators literally have T8 SOPs scripted. Pilot can borrow DB Ferry's T3/T8 SOP verbatim rather than build one. Cheaper than reinventing.
  - source-discovered: **CLP + HK Electric dual-monopoly grid** is *unusually* stable and capacity-adequate vs. most retrofitted markets (Bali PLN blackout Aug 2024, Maldives diesel-resort microgrids, PH brownouts). HK is structurally the *least* grid-constrained market retrofitted — capacity is genuinely a non-issue; only pedestal availability matters. Worth noting in cross-market grid-quality ranking.
  - source-discovered: **First-of-class certification path** for a foiling electric passenger vessel at HK Marine Dept has no precedent — expect 12–18 months. Likely similar for Singapore MPA, Tokyo JCG, Sydney AMSA — all non-EU-reciprocal regimes where Candela/Artemis EU type-approval doesn't carry. Worth a `_reference/first-of-class-certification-regimes.md` to track these vs. EU-reciprocal (Med, UK) and US-reciprocal (USCG).
  - source-discovered: **Jul 1 (HKSAR Establishment Day) + Oct 1 (PRC National Day) as politically sensitive launch-blackout weeks** in HK specifically (post-NSL). Distinct from procurement blackout — it's a PR/optics blackout. Catalogue with KSA National Day (Sep 23), UAE National Day (Dec 2), Bahrain National Day (Dec 16): markets where specific national-day weeks are PR-blackouts for any waterfront-visible activity by non-domestic operators.
  - schema-gap: "**Edge-of-N30 + cross-jurisdiction**" is a sharper sub-class than current edge-of-N30 prompt — the foreign-regulator pedestal-concession ask materially changes pilot feasibility.
  - schema-gap: "**Hard statutory weather-trigger blackouts**" (T8 typhoon, JMA tsunami, USCG Port Condition Zulu, NDRRMC Signal 3) should sit alongside "hard cultural/religious blackouts" (Nyepi) as a parallel statutory-blackout class — currently both buried in weather-window prompt.
  - schema-gap: Pier-access archetype taxonomy now has four distinct patterns across retrofits — schema should enumerate them explicitly: per-pier concession matrix, per-resort jetty + custodian, resort/club-microgrid, slot-allocated public pier complex.
  - schema-gap: **National-day PR-blackout weeks** (HK Jul 1 / Oct 1, KSA Sep 23, UAE Dec 2, Bahrain Dec 16) distinct from procurement-bandwidth blackouts — they gate *visible* activity, not bandwidth. Worth a separate sub-bullet.
  - schema-gap: **Grid-quality ranking** across retrofitted markets is now meaningful (HK best, KSA/UAE strong, Bali/PH/Maldives constrained, Antalya intermediate) — could seed a `_reference/grid-quality-by-market.md` to drive charging-architecture defaults.
  - efficiency: HK is the first East Asia retrofit; six prior retrofits (5 SEA + Antalya/MENA) provided strong analog coverage. Next East Asia retrofits (Tokyo, Seoul, Taipei, Singapore-retrofit) should be faster — most operating-constraint patterns (typhoon, FY Apr–Mar, dual-grid, first-of-class cert, slot-allocated piers) will recur.
suggested-update:
  target-file: /agent/home/navier/world-map/_schema/CITY_TEMPLATE.md
  change: (1) Refine edge-of-N30 prompt to two sub-classes: "**edge-of-N30 single-jurisdiction**" (BKK↔Pattaya, Jeddah↔KAEC) vs. "**edge-of-N30 + cross-jurisdiction**" (HK↔Macau, Doha↔Bahrain, Miami↔Bimini, Singapore↔Batam) — the latter adds a foreign-regulator pedestal-concession ask that gates feasibility independent of range. (2) Add Operating Constraints sub-bullet "**Hard statutory weather-trigger blackouts**" — HKO T8+, JMA tsunami, USCG Port Condition Zulu, PH NDRRMC Signal 3, JMA Bosai — alongside "hard cultural/religious blackouts" (Nyepi, royal mourning). Statutory mandatory closure with no commercial exemption, but stochastic-triggered. (3) Enumerate **pier-access archetype taxonomy**: (a) per-pier concession matrix (Bangkok Chao Phraya), (b) per-resort jetty + customary custodian (Bali banjar, Maldives), (c) resort/club-microgrid (PH, Maldives, HK clubs), (d) slot-allocated public pier complex (HK Central Piers, Sydney Circular Quay, Istanbul Eminönü, Mumbai Gateway). State explicitly per sub-cluster which archetype applies. (4) Add separate **national-day PR-blackout** sub-bullet distinct from procurement-bandwidth blackouts — gates *visible* activity by non-domestic operators, not bandwidth. Catalogue: HK Jul 1 + Oct 1, KSA Sep 23, UAE Dec 2, Bahrain Dec 16, Indonesia Aug 17, PH Jun 12. (5) Create `_reference/first-of-class-certification-regimes.md` — tracks which national regimes (HK Marine Dept, Singapore MPA, Japan JCG, Australia AMSA) require 12–18 month first-of-class submission for foiling electric passenger vessels vs. EU-reciprocal (Med + UK) and USCG-reciprocal regimes. (6) Create `_reference/grid-quality-by-market.md` — defaults charging-architecture choice (centralized-pedestal feasible in HK/UAE/KSA, microgrid-mandated in Bali/PH/Maldives, hybrid in Antalya/Bangkok).

date: 2026-05-27
subagent: navier-city-retrofit.md
task: Retrofitted Manila/Cebu/Palawan/Boracay Philippines file with Sub-clusters (5 rows incl. 2 OUT-OF-SCOPE pitch-trap redirects) + Operating Constraints.
learnings:
  - methodology: **Philippines is the structural SEA typhoon outlier** — confirmed across now four SEA retrofits (Phuket sheltered by Andaman geometry, Jakarta below typhoon track, Bangkok above main track, Bali below track; only PH directly exposed). This is a *Navier-meaningful* structural disadvantage vs. all peer SEA markets — 4–6 month materially reduced operating window per exposed corridor per year. Worth flagging in any SEA cross-market pilot-geography ranking as a first-order discriminator.
  - methodology: **Habagat/Amihan seasonal beach-side flip** (Boracay White Beach W-facing in SW monsoon, Bulabog E-facing in NE monsoon) is per-property operational rhythm hospitality GMs already manage — pilot SOPs can borrow the resort's existing seasonal-side calendar verbatim rather than reinvent. Likely analog: any island with two monsoon-side beaches (Phuket Patong vs. Rawai, Koh Samui Chaweng vs. Lipa Noi, Goa Calangute vs. Palolem).
  - methodology: **Four-sub-cluster + two-OUT-OF-SCOPE-row pattern** — PH is the densest sub-cluster table yet (6 rows total). Beyond a certain density, the Sub-clusters table effectively replaces the rest of the file as the primary navigation surface; the per-sub-cluster wedge / counterparty / charging-spec drill-downs need their own sub-section structure under Players. Schema-level: above 4 sub-clusters, recommend per-sub-cluster sub-headings throughout the file, not just in Sub-clusters table.
  - methodology: **No purpose-built fast-boat terminal anywhere in PH** — Sanur (Bali), Mandalika (Lombok), Hulhumalé Phase II (Maldives), Tuas-area Singapore have credible greenfield charging-hub sites; **PH has none**. Civil-works greenfield is materially harder than concession-pedestal retrofit. Pushes PH pilot architecture toward **resort-microgrid-anchored** charging not **public-terminal-anchored** — different pilot economics, different counterparty (resort GM not port concessionaire).
  - methodology: **Marcos-Duterte coalition volatility** as rolling sovereign blackout — adds PH to the catalogue (NEOM CEO churn, Jakarta Prabowo transition, Bangkok Pheu Thai, now Manila Marcos-Duterte split). Generalizable pattern: any market with active intra-coalition power struggle = rolling sovereign blackout, regardless of formal election calendar. Worth promoting from per-market footnote to standing schema prompt.
  - source-discovered: **El Nido ↔ Coron ~70 nm intra-Palawan edge-of-N30 loop** — opens a credible single-charging-stop circular pilot entirely within one sub-cluster, avoiding inter-sub-cluster N30 fail. Adds Palawan to the catalogue of "in-sub-cluster N30 loops" alongside Bacuit Archipelago short legs and Coron Bay legs. Strong single-Palawan-cluster pilot story without needing Manila link.
  - source-discovered: **Tsuneishi Cebu + FBMA + Cebu Shipyard cluster (Balamban, Cebu)** makes Cebu the only PH sub-cluster with credible in-archipelago MRO + composite-build optionality. Bali has North Sea Boats (Banyuwangi); Maldives has none (Sri Lanka/Singapore reach-back); PH-Cebu now confirmed as third documented in-archipelago industrial cluster. Worth maintaining `_reference/in-archipelago-mro-clusters.md` since the list is now meaningful.
  - source-discovered: **Boracay 2018 Duterte-ordered closure (6 months) + DENR Inter-Agency Rehabilitation Management Group permanent overlay** — Boracay is the *only* PH (and possibly SEA) tourism node with statutory permanent environmental-permitting overlay above MARINA/DOT baseline. Materially harder permitting than peers; mirrors KSA RSG/AMAALA environmental-scrutiny posture but driven by historical closure not greenfield design. Adds **"post-closure permanent environmental overlay"** as a distinct regulatory archetype.
  - source-discovered: **Dona Paz 1987 + recurring Visayan fast-craft incidents** — institutional memory in PH passenger maritime is uniquely scarred globally (Dona Paz = worst peacetime maritime disaster). This *helps* premium-safety positioning more than any other SEA market — passengers willing to pay specifically for visibly-safer product. Inverts the usual "safety is table stakes" framing into "safety is the wedge."
  - source-discovered: **Aboitiz SuperCat lineage descends from 1980s–90s PH jetfoil services** — explicit PH-historical jetfoil graveyard datapoint extending the Bangkok schema prompt. PH unit-economics killed jetfoil at scale despite proven demand. Worth maintaining `_reference/historical-jetfoil-graveyards.md`.
  - source-discovered: **PH fiscal year = calendar year (Jan–Dec)** like Indonesia — confirms SEA FY pattern set now spans Singapore Apr–Mar, Thailand Oct–Sep, Indonesia + PH Jan–Dec. Four SEA FY datapoints across five retrofits; the SEA fiscal-calendar reference file proposed in Phuket/Bali learnings now has full coverage to seed.
  - schema-gap: Above 4 sub-clusters in a single file, the per-sub-cluster wedge/players/charging drill-downs warrant their own per-sub-cluster sub-headings throughout Players and Wedges sections, not just the Sub-clusters table — schema should prescribe this transition.
  - schema-gap: **Resort-microgrid-anchored vs. public-terminal-anchored** pilot architecture is a first-order pilot-economics fork (different counterparty, different unit economics, different timeline) that isn't currently called out in either Sub-clusters or Operating Constraints. Worth a dedicated charging-architecture sub-prompt.
  - schema-gap: **Post-closure permanent environmental overlay** (Boracay) is a distinct regulatory archetype not currently in schema — sits between greenfield environmental scrutiny (KSA RSG) and baseline regulator stack (Phuket/Bali baseline).
  - schema-gap: **Safety-as-wedge inversion** (PH Dona Paz institutional memory) — schema currently treats safety as table stakes; PH (and possibly Greek Aegean, Bangladeshi ferry, Egyptian Red Sea) markets actually allow safety to be the leading wedge. Worth distinguishing "safety = table stakes" vs. "safety = wedge" markets.
  - efficiency: Five SEA retrofits done (Phuket, Jakarta, Bangkok, Bali, Manila/Cebu/Palawan/Boracay). The proposed SEA reference files (monsoon-blackout calendar, fiscal calendar, out-of-range marquee routes, in-archipelago MRO clusters, historical jetfoil graveyards) are now backed by enough datapoints to seed *immediately* with high confidence rather than incrementally.
suggested-update:
  target-file: /agent/home/navier/world-map/_schema/CITY_TEMPLATE.md
  change: (1) Add Sub-clusters convention: "**>4 sub-clusters → per-sub-cluster sub-headings throughout file** — when a file spans more than 4 sub-clusters, repeat the sub-cluster as a sub-heading inside Players, Wedge hypotheses, and Operating Constraints rather than weaving them inline; the Sub-clusters table becomes the file's navigation index." (2) Add Operating Constraints charging-architecture sub-prompt: "**Charging architecture fork: resort-microgrid-anchored vs. public-terminal-anchored vs. greenfield-civil-works** — first-order pilot-economics determinant. Microgrid-anchored (PH, Maldives, El Nido) = resort GM counterparty, fast cycle, no civil works. Public-terminal (Sanur, Hulhumalé, future Mandalika) = port-concessionaire counterparty, medium cycle, retrofit pedestal. Greenfield (PH bangka landings, NEOM, AMAALA) = developer counterparty, slow cycle, civil-works dependency. State explicitly per sub-cluster." (3) Add regulatory archetype: "**Post-closure permanent environmental overlay** (Boracay post-2018 IATF, possibly future analogs at over-touristed sites) — distinct from greenfield environmental scrutiny and baseline regulator stack; permitting materially harder than peer sub-clusters in same country." (4) Distinguish **safety-as-table-stakes** (most markets) from **safety-as-wedge** markets (PH Dona Paz memory, Greek Aegean post-Norman Atlantic, Bangladeshi launch incidents, Egyptian Red Sea ferry record) — inverts pilot positioning from "comfort + speed" to "demonstrably safer than incumbent." (5) Promote **rolling sovereign blackout from intra-coalition volatility** (NEOM, Jakarta, Bangkok, Manila) to standing schema prompt, separate from formal-election-calendar blackout. (6) **Create the five SEA reference files now** — datapoint density across five retrofits supports immediate seeding rather than incremental: `_reference/sea-monsoon-and-blackout-calendar.md`, `_reference/sea-fiscal-calendar.md`, `_reference/out-of-range-marquee-routes.md`, `_reference/in-archipelago-mro-clusters.md`, `_reference/historical-jetfoil-graveyards.md`.

To be populated during the labored city retrofit pass.

Trigger: Jaideep flagged that ~15 city files erroneously used "N30 = 70 nm" as a single binding constraint, ignoring N30 Quanta-LR Hybrid (2,000 nm, H2 2026+). This caused many marquee Quanta-LR-viable routes to be stamped OUT OF SCOPE. Re-retrofit of all existing city files is the next action.

Going forward: parent agent will periodically (every ~5–10 entries) consolidate learnings into schema/reference/subagent files using this PROMOTION NOTE pattern, so the log stays a working queue rather than an unprocessed archive.
