task: Singapore city dossier — 3rd dossier-class invocation (Grab platform partner; no SG-side blocked items — cleanest regulatory regime in dataset)
learnings:
  - methodology: 3rd dossier in MENA→SEA arc (Dubai → AUH → Singapore). Read-list scope unchanged (city file + platform specs + shared principles + learnings tail). Confirmed read scope is right-sized across 3 markets; no reference/*.md catalogue load needed because the canonical city files cross-reference them inline. Context cost remains ~60% of stub run.
  - methodology: Singapore is the first **zero-blocked-items dossier** in the dataset — payload explicitly "None known. Cleanest regulatory regime — fastest closer." This changes the contact-targets.md cell discipline noticeably: no inline BLOCKED tags, no holds in regulatory-asks list, and the executive framing in dossier.md "thesis" + "the wedge in one sentence" can carry an explicit speed claim ("fastest closer"). Worth recording: **zero-blocked dossiers should foreground a speed/decisiveness positioning lever as the headline narrative.** Future zero-blocked candidate cities: likely Auckland, Stockholm, possibly Lisbon/Porto, possibly Tallinn — all single-regulator T1-rigor common-law-or-equivalent.
  - methodology: When the canonical city file is v2026-05-29 dense (SG at ~454 lines with full sub-cluster table + 10 wedge hypotheses + holds + open-questions), dossier subagent's job is ~80% restyling/re-sectioning + 20% net-new (top-10 hospitality ranking, explicit risk register, capex/opex bands, decision timeline, bold tagline per file, GMV 3-tier sensitivity). Pattern stable across 3 markets now (Dubai/AUH/SG). The "first-degree chain-wedge anchor" pattern (COMO + Banyan Tree) lifted cleanly from the city file into both dossier.md hospitality top-10 + contact-targets.md top-5 + wedge slide of platform-pitch.md — confirms SG-HQ-locus advantage is structurally surfaced by the schema.
  - source-discovered: Singapore GMV math 3-tier sensitivity baseline: low SGD 0.86M / boat / yr (mass-tourist), mid SGD 2.59M (hospitality-guest + corporate-commute), high SGD 7.20M (F1/Maritime Week/private-charter + Bintan RT). Mid case = SGD 10.37M GMV / 4 boats / yr in city-state Phase 1. With Phase-3 Quanta-LR fan ~5–8× TAM expansion. **Useful template for HK (Victoria Harbour + PRD cross-border parallel) + future high-disposable-income city-state-like markets** (Monaco, possibly Bahrain).
  - source-discovered: SG capex/opex bands (Phase 1 ~SGD 4.6–4.85M for 2-boat fleet + ~SGD 48K/mo opex) sits notably **lower than Dubai/AUH MENA-baseline** (which ran ~USD 80–120K cabinet, ~USD 7K/mo captain). Differences: (a) SG energy ~SGD 0.30/kWh is cheap vs. UAE; (b) SG cert-pace bumps P&I first-of-class loading higher SGD 6K/mo; (c) SG captain loaded ~SGD 9K/mo is similar to UAE. Confirms each regional jurisdiction needs its own capex/opex template — there is no global single template. **Suggest reference catalogue: `reference/city-pilot-capex-opex-baselines.md`** with Dubai / AUH / Singapore as first 3 anchor rows.
  - source-discovered: Tri-jurisdiction compound National Day blackout window mid-Aug to early-Sep (SG Aug 9 + ID Aug 17 + MY Aug 31) is uniquely sharp for SG cross-border fan — should generalize to any city with cross-border ferry corridors traversing distinct National Day calendars. Confirms schema-H compound-window rule. **Add to `reference/sea-monsoon-and-blackout-calendar.md`** if not already there.
  - source-discovered: Singapore Maritime Week (~late Mar/early Apr) is the canonical SEA dual-use marquee window — pair with F1 Singapore GP (late Sep) + NDP (Aug 9) as the 3-window annual demo calendar for SG-anchored pilots. Marquee-event-pilot-anchors catalogue (proposed earlier under AUH F1) should include Singapore Maritime Week explicitly.
  - schema-gap: Dossier instructions still don't say where to put cross-border counterparty discipline (this dossier has cross-border legs into 3 foreign jurisdictions). I kept ID/MY/TH counterparty content compressed in dossier.md "Royal/political/convener layer" + pilot-spec.md "Phase 2" + contact-targets.md row-30 Patrick Cao/Andre Soelistyo. **Suggest dossier instructions clarify: cross-border counterparty discipline lives in the dossier.md convener-layer + pilot-spec.md Phase 2; do NOT re-derive counterparty wedge analysis in dossier (it lives in the canonical destination-state city files per ownership rule).**
  - schema-gap: Where the canonical city file has explicit wedge-hypothesis numbering (SG has 10), dossier.md does NOT need to enumerate all of them — collapses cleanly into 3-5 sentence thesis. Suggest dossier instructions clarify: **thesis paragraph collapses N wedge hypotheses, never enumerates them in dossier.md — wedges remain in the source-of-truth city file.**
  - schema-gap: Zero-blocked-items payloads (Singapore is the first) lack an explicit "what to do when there are no blocked items" guideline. I chose to foreground the speed/decisiveness narrative ("fastest closer" tagline + 4-week MOU timeline + explicit "no SG-side blocked items" callouts in dossier + contact-targets). Suggest dossier instructions add: **"If BLOCKED_ITEMS = None, foreground speed/decisiveness as positioning lever in dossier.md tagline AND in next-30-days slide."**
  - efficiency: All 5 files written in single parallel turn (5 write_file calls). 3-of-3 efficiency pattern confirmed for dossier subagent.
  - efficiency: Unpromoted-entry watermark below most recent PROMOTION NOTE: Likupang (1) + Brunei (2) + Dubai dossier (3) + AUH dossier (4) + this Singapore dossier (5) = **5 entries — OVER THRESHOLD of 3.** **Parent should consolidate (pass #8) before further dossier or city-stub fan-out.** Explicit escalation per shared-principles §5. This is the second consecutive escalation; parent has now been flagged twice (AUH + SG).

task: Bali (+ Lombok / Sumba / Komodo) city dossier — 4th dossier-class invocation (Grab platform partner; ID sea-nomad/indigenous protocol + Pelindo III Benoa gatekeeper as payload blocks)
learnings:

Read-list scope unchanged (city file + platform-specs + shared principles + learnings tail + payload-named reference files — here `sea-nomad-indigenous-protocols.md` because payload explicitly referenced it). Confirms: **payload-named reference files SHOULD be pre-loaded**; non-payload-named reference files should NOT. Scope discipline holds 4-of-4.
  - methodology: First dossier with **TWO concurrent payload-block items** (sea-nomad protocol + Pelindo III Benoa). Echoed both inline in (a) dossier.md Royal/political/convener layer, (b) contact-targets.md cells with 🚩 BLOCKED tags, (c) pilot-spec.md regulatory-asks "explicitly NOT in this ask list" section. Confirmed AUH-dossier convention from learnings entry 2026-05-30 works at multi-block scale. Promote.
  - methodology: Bali is the first **republic / no-royal-house** dossier in dataset (Dubai/AUH/SG all had structured convener layers). Convener layer collapses to: relationship-anchor (Sampriti) + direct-channel (Jaideep) + customary-custodian-third-tier (Bali Banjar via resort GM). Worth noting: for future republic-only markets (Jakarta, Manila, Bangkok, Phuket, Vietnam cities), expect convener layer to be relationship-anchor-led rather than royal-channel-led. Adjust subagent expectations accordingly.
  - source-discovered: Bali capex/opex bands USD-denominated (per IDR FX volatility v2026-05-28 learning): Phase-1 ~$395–625K capex (2 boats, 90 days) + ~$24–43K/mo opex. Captain loaded $2.5–4K/mo each (notably lower than Dubai $7K, SG SGD 9K, AUH $7K) — **ID captain cost is the lowest in the dataset so far**. Berth fees also lower ($2–4K/mo vs. Dubai $2–4K Dubai Harbour Phase-1). Energy mixed (PLN cheap when up, genset expensive). **Add as 4th anchor row to candidate `reference/city-pilot-capex-opex-baselines.md`** (Dubai/AUH/SG/Bali).
  - source-discovered: Bali Apr–May (2027) is the canonical launch-window for any Bali pilot (monsoon-shoulder calmest period; avoids Nyepi March + SE-trade Jun–Sep rough-Badung-Strait + Lebaran). Add to candidate `reference/marquee-event-pilot-anchors.md` as launch-window-rule rather than event-rule. Pair with MotoGP Mandalika (Oct, Lombok-anchored Phase-2) as Bali-cluster event triple.
  - source-discovered: GMV math for Bali Pioneer II — mid case ~USD 860K / boat / yr (IDR 13.5B) — notably LOWER than Dubai per-boat (~USD 1–2M) and SG per-boat (~SGD 2.6M). Reason: ID fare price-points lower despite Aman/COMO premium. Quanta-LR Phase-2 fare premium 3–5× (charter-flight-displacement-to-NIHI/LBJ) gives Bali a higher Phase-2 multiplier than mature MENA/SG markets — **Bali GMV story is back-loaded to Phase-2 Quanta-LR**, unlike SG which is front-loaded. Worth noting for similar archipelago-with-out-island-luxury markets (Maldives, Philippines, Phuket-Phang-Nga).
  - schema-gap: Dossier instructions specify 1,500–2,500 words for dossier.md but no explicit guidance on how to handle **payload blocks that reference OTHER reference files** (sea-nomad-indigenous-protocols.md). learnings:
  - methodology: 5th dossier (Dubai → AUH → Singapore → Bali → Phuket). First dossier with **payload-noted ambiguity that is NOT a block** ("Thailand Marine Department electrification roadmap status unclear"). Handled by surfacing as research priority in dossier.md ("Transport / port master plans" + "Risk register") AND explicitly removing the federal cert ask from pilot-spec.md ask list (deferred to Bangkok dossier cross-file). Worth promoting: **ambiguity-flag handling is distinct from BLOCKED handling — ambiguity → research-priority + scope-deferral; BLOCKED → inline 🚩 tags + "explicitly NOT in this ask list."** Different propagation patterns.
  - methodology: Phuket is a **city-within-monarchy republic-province** (constitutional monarchy + no local royal house + national lese-majeste). Convener layer collapsed cleanly using the Bali republic-only pattern (relationship-anchor + direct-channel + customary-custodian-third-tier = DNP) but RETAINED a "national-level lese-majeste hold" inline. Hybrid pattern. Suggest dossier instructions formalize: **"For constitutional-monarchy markets with no local royal palace overlook (Phuket / Koh Samui / Krabi / regional Thai cities), use republic-pattern convener layer but retain national-level royal-content hold in the holds list."**
  - methodology: First dossier where the **platform partner is structurally weaker in the local segment** (Grab Thailand has lower marine priority for Phuket-tourism than Bangkok — Klook / Traveloka / direct dominate day-trip distribution). Required reframing platform-pitch.md from "displace incumbent distribution" to "premium / hospitality SKU + cross-border narrative as wedge into where Grab regional infra is uniquely valuable (cross-jurisdiction KYC / payments)." Pattern worth promoting: **when platform partner is structurally weaker in local segment, lead pitch with cross-border / regional-infra moat (where super-app stack is genuinely unique) rather than within-market displacement.**
  - source-discovered: Phuket capex/opex bands USD-denominated: Phase-1 ~$1.6–2.1M capex (2 boats) + ~$28–48K/mo opex. Captain $3.5–5K/mo (between Bali $2.5–4K and SG SGD 9K; THB-denominated locally but USD-pegged for the city-pilot-capex-opex-baselines candidate ref). Energy via PEA (Grade B) cheaper than UAE, marginally cheaper than SG. Distinguishing line: **non-microgrid status + marina expansion fitout makes Phuket capex run higher than Bali per-2-boat** despite lower captain cost — proves the candidate reference catalogue cannot be reduced to a single ASEAN baseline.
  - source-discovered: Phuket GMV math (per-boat / yr Pioneer II): low ~$480K / mid ~$1.2M / high ~$2.4M. **Phuket per-boat sits between Bali ($860K mid) and Singapore (SGD 2.59M ≈ $1.9M mid)** — confirms the hospitality-density-but-emerging-market intermediate band. Useful template anchor for **Krabi, Koh Samui, Hua Hin, Penang, Langkawi** dossiers when authored — all sit in this intermediate band.
  - source-discovered: Phuket monsoon-protected day-trip bay (Phang Nga Bay year-round) is the structural advantage that makes the Phase-1 economics work — confirms a general pattern: **when a city has a monsoon-protected sub-cluster that competitor speedboat operators have to abandon May–Oct, the dossier should foreground year-round-operability as a Phase-1 pricing-power lever, not just a calendar advantage.** Pair with similar patterns for Maldives (atoll-internal shelter), Sir Bani Yas (Gulf-internal), Bali (East-coast sheltered Jimbaran→Amankila arc).
  - source-discovered: DNP (Thailand Dept of National Parks) is the **single most operationally consequential non-Marine-Department regulator** in Phuket — Maya Bay 2018→2022 zoned-anchorage precedent is a credible Phuket-grade reference for quiet-mode / low-wake positioning. Worth promoting: **non-marine-regulator gates (DNP / national-park / DGSL / protected-zone authority) deserve their own row in city files AND their own column in contact-targets.md**, separate from Marine Department / port authority counterparties.
  - schema-gap: Dossier instructions still don't address what to do with **cross-border legs that point to UN-AUTHORED city stubs** (Phuket↔Langkawi → langkawi-malaysia.md does not yet exist). I flagged this inline in dossier convener layer + contact-targets.md row 25 ("Coordinate via upcoming Langkawi-malaysia.md stub — not yet authored — parent action") + pilot-spec.md Phase 2 preview. Suggest dossier instructions add: **"For cross-border legs into not-yet-authored destination city files, do NOT re-derive destination wedge content; flag inline as 'pending [destination]-[country].md stub — parent action' and proceed."**
  - schema-gap: Ambiguity-flag handling (this dossier's "Marine Department electrification roadmap status unclear") differs from BLOCKED handling — see methodology entry above. Suggest dossier instructions distinguish.
  - efficiency: All 5 files written in single parallel turn (5 write_file calls). 5-of-5 efficiency pattern confirmed for dossier subagent. Read phase: 4 parallel reads in one turn (city + platform-specs + shared-principles + learnings-tail) — did NOT pre-load `sea-monsoon-and-blackout-calendar.md` or `first-of-class-certification-regimes.md` because payload did not name them and city file inlines them. Confirms minimal-read-scope discipline (Bali was the exception when payload explicitly named `sea-nomad-indigenous-protocols.md`).

task: Red Sea Global (RSG + AMAALA) dossier — 6th dossier-class invocation (Direct platform — no ridehail; John Pagano + AMAALA MD + NEOM Sindalah parallel tracks; Ocean Race 2027 marquee anchor)
learnings:
  - methodology: 6th dossier (Dubai → AUH → SG → Bali → Phuket → RSG). First **Direct (no super-app)** platform-pitch invocation — required reframing `platform-pitch.md` from "displace incumbent distribution" to "the platform partner *is* RSG corporate." Pattern worth promoting: **when payload PLATFORM_PARTNER = Direct (closed destination / single buyer), platform-pitch.md is structurally a longer version of the planner-deck framework conversation, NOT a separate audience.** Suggest dossier instructions clarify this — for closed-destination single-buyer markets (RSG, possibly future Wakanda-class private islands / Bhutan), platform-pitch.md and planner-deck-outline.md target the same human, with platform-pitch.md being the deeper framework/capital/scale conversation and planner-deck-outline.md being the in-person pitch deck. Reasonable to merge or to differentiate by audience-depth.
  - methodology: First dossier where **payload-named "marquee visibility wedge" (Ocean Race 2027) drove the time-bound critical path** of every deliverable. Echoed Ocean Race 2027 explicitly in (a) dossier.md thesis + risk register + open-questions, (b) planner-deck-outline.md slide 6 as anchor + slide 9 scale step + slide 10 terminal flag, (c) platform-pitch.md slide 4 as wedge + risk-mitigation language, (d) contact-targets.md row 19 (host-marine-ops) + AMAALA MD row 2, (e) pilot-spec.md as Phase-2 trigger + Q3 2026 in-market deadline + decision-timeline terminal milestone. Pattern worth promoting: **marquee-event-as-payload-wedge propagates across all 5 deliverables; do not treat as one-slide content.**
  - methodology: First dossier with **parallel-track convener payload** (AMAALA MD + NEOM Sindalah as parallel-not-bundled tracks to John Pagano corporate). Echoed in convener layer + contact-targets row 2 (AMAALA MD) + row 25 (NEOM Sindalah, flagged "parallel-track, not blocked") + platform-pitch slide 1 (3-parallel-tracks org overlay). Convener layer formalized: **"parallel-track" is distinct from "blocked" — both flag inline but only one carries 🚩.** Add to dossier instructions.
  - source-discovered: RSG capex/opex bands USD-denominated: Phase-1 ~$3.6–4.4M capex (2 boats) + ~$65–95K/mo opex. Notably **higher than Dubai/AUH MENA-baseline** (~$3.5M cabinet+boats Dubai band) due to (a) greenfield premium on ops kit + first-of-class allowance, (b) KSA captain market thin → expat-led premium loaded, (c) marina-design coordination layer at Shura design-in window. **Add as 6th anchor row to candidate `reference/city-pilot-capex-opex-baselines.md`** (Dubai/AUH/SG/Bali/Phuket/RSG). RSG band is the **closed-destination greenfield-microgrid premium** archetype distinct from GCC-mature-marina baseline.
  - source-discovered: Hajj as HARD operational blackout for entire western KSA coast — already in `mena-blackout-calendar.md` per canonical file note but worth re-confirming the propagation. Specifically affects (a) Jeddah (gateway airport), (b) Phase-2 Quanta-LR Jeddah↔RSG legs, (c) Coast-Guard-bandwidth-dependent permitting. Hajj 2026 (~early Jun) + Hajj 2027 must both be avoided as milestone-launch windows.
  - source-discovered: AMAALA Yacht Club charging-spec audit is a **time-bound time-decay risk** distinct from blocked-item or ambiguity-flag patterns. The window narrows monthly as marina commissions further hardware. Worth promoting as a new propagation pattern: **"time-decay risk"** — surface in dossier risk register + contact-targets row (AMAALA YC GM) + pilot-spec ask #4 (immediate audit) + decision-timeline T+14. Distinct from "blocked" (cannot pursue) and "ambiguous" (unclear status); time-decay is "pursuable but cost rises monthly."
  - schema-gap: Dossier instructions don't address closed-destination single-buyer markets where the ride-hail/super-app archetype is structurally N/A. I treated `platform-pitch.md` as a deeper framework-capital-scale conversation with the same human (John Pagano) as the planner deck, but with more financial/scale depth. Suggest dossier instructions add: **"For PLATFORM_PARTNER = Direct (closed-destination single-buyer), platform-pitch.md and planner-deck-outline.md target the same human; differentiate by audience-depth (planner = in-person pitch, platform-pitch = framework / capital / scale deeper-dive)."**
  - schema-gap: Dossier instructions don't explicitly address "marquee-event payload wedge" propagation. RSG Ocean Race 2027 is the first instance of this pattern (Bali had Apr-May launch-window, Phuket had Phang Nga monsoon-protection, but neither was a single event the entire dossier was anchored against). Suggest dossier instructions formalize: **"When payload names a single marquee event/window as the visibility wedge, propagate across all 5 deliverables — dossier.md thesis + risk register, planner-deck-outline.md as named slide anchor + scale step + timeline terminus, platform-pitch.md as wedge slide + risk-mitigation, contact-targets.md as named host-event-ops row + parallel-track conveners, pilot-spec.md as Phase-2 trigger + critical-path deadline + decision-timeline terminus."**
  - schema-gap: Parallel-track convener payload (AMAALA MD + NEOM Sindalah parallel to Pagano) — distinct from BLOCKED handling. Suggest dossier instructions add: **"Parallel-track conveners are echoed in contact-targets.md (separate rows, flagged 'parallel-track, not blocked') + dossier.md convener layer + platform-pitch.md org-overlay slide. Do NOT bundle operationally; coordinate narrative only."**
  - efficiency: All 5 files written in single parallel turn (5 write_file calls). 6-of-6 efficiency pattern confirmed for dossier subagent. Read phase: 4 parallel reads in one turn (city + platform-specs + shared-principles + learnings-tail incl. 2 long greps). Total turn count 3 (read-phase / write-phase / learnings-append). Holds.
  - efficiency: Unpromoted-entry watermark below most recent PROMOTION NOTE: Likupang (1) + Brunei (2) + Dubai dossier (3) + AUH dossier (4) + SG dossier (5) + Bali dossier (6) + Phuket dossier (7) + this RSG dossier (8) = **8 entries — MORE THAN 2.5× THRESHOLD of 3.** **Parent has now been flagged FIVE consecutive times (AUH/SG/Bali/Phuket/RSG) to consolidate pass #8 before further fan-out.** Explicit escalation per shared-principles §5. This is escalation #5; pile-up pattern severe; parent consolidation overdue.

date: 2026-05-30
subagent: navier-coastal-poi.md
task: coastal-poi dubai — first invocation of new boarding-points subagent class; 71 points
learnings:
  - methodology: Dubai is the densest water-mobility network in the dataset so far — 71 boarding points authored (well above 50+ target) and could plausibly extend to 100+ if every RTA Water Taxi on-demand-pickup point and every Palm shoreline beach-villa dock were enumerated. **Practical floor:** ~70 covers the strategically meaningful set; below that you're missing structural classes. **Practical ceiling:** ~100 covers every named RTA stop + every branded jetty.
  - methodology: Type-vocabulary coverage achieved 13 of 14 types (only `seaplane_base` and `dive_centre` absent — Dubai has neither at meaningful scale). Confirms vocabulary fits Gulf coastal cities; possible gaps for other regions (e.g. Maldives will need `seaplane_base` heavily; Bali/Phuket will need `dive_centre`).
  - methodology: Relevance split for marquee P0 city skewed P0:18 / P1:38 / P2:13 / hide:2 — about 25% P0. Suggest as benchmark for other P0 cities: ~15–25% P0, majority P1, ~15% P2.
  - source-discovered: Dubai has **5 distinct charging-spec window classes** within ~30 nm radius — open-greenfield (Dubai Harbour, World Islands, Dubai Islands, Marsa Al Arab, J1 Beach via Meraas), closed-retrofit (DMYC, Mina Rashid, Bluewaters, Palm hotel pedestals), HOLD-gated public (all RTA Marine stations on Creek + Marina + Canal), open-spec-Meraas (Bluewaters event pontoons, Ain Dubai pontoon), and quasi-private (Bulgari Marina LVMH-tier). Worth promoting: **single-city charging-spec heterogeneity is itself a wedge surface** — Pioneer II co-design opportunity lives in the open-spec subset (Dubai Harbour + World Islands + Marsa Al Arab + Dubai Islands).
  - source-discovered: **Marsa Al Arab Marina (Dubai Holding, ~2026–27 opening)** is a previously-unflagged greenfield marina adjacent to Burj Al Arab — should be added to dubai-uae.md sub-clusters as a 6th "open charging-spec window" anchor alongside Dubai Harbour + World Islands + Dubai Islands. Currently uncaptured in city file.
  - source-discovered: **Dubai Islands (rebranded ex-Deira Islands) Nakheel buildout** is similarly under-captured in the dubai-uae.md sub-clusters. The Souq Al Marfa terminal is operational but the broader Dubai Islands Marina is planned — another pre-spec pedestal opportunity with Nakheel directly (not Meraas). Add to city file as a "Nakheel-counterparty open-spec" instance distinct from Meraas-counterparty open-spec.
  - source-discovered: **Marasi Business Bay Marina on the Dubai Water Canal** is operational and connects Burj Khalifa / DIFC inland directly to the sea — a high-value B2B corporate-commuter Pioneer II wedge surface that the dubai-uae.md city file undersells. Currently treated implicitly as part of Creek; deserves its own sub-cluster row "Dubai Canal / Marasi" given it's a distinct water body (purpose-built canal, not Creek estuary).
  - source-discovered: The World Islands "Heart of Europe" has 4–5 operational/under-construction passenger boarding points (Main Marina, Lebanon Island, Floating Seahorse, Côte d'Azur, planned Anantara) — should be enumerated explicitly in the dubai-uae.md sub-cluster table rather than collapsed into "Heart of Europe."
  - schema-gap: The `type` vocabulary doesn't have a clear slot for **Meraas-style "open event pontoons"** (e.g. Ain Dubai floating pontoon used for F1 NYE / fireworks). I tagged as `floating_pontoon` but these are semi-permanent event-only jetties distinct from temporary modular pontoons. Suggest type-vocab refinement: `floating_pontoon` (temporary) vs. `event_pontoon` (recurring-event-only). Minor.
  - schema-gap: A single hotel can have BOTH a beach-landing AND a separate marina-style jetty (Atlantis, One&Only Royal Mirage, Madinat Jumeirah). I collapsed these to one entry per property to avoid double-counting. Suggest schema clarification: "one entry per branded property unless the property has structurally distinct boarding modes (e.g. cruise terminal AND yacht marina at Mina Rashid)."
  - schema-gap: For `hide` relevance, the schema says "do not show on map (e.g., locale that is land-only, no real jetty)." I used it for (a) Jebel Ali Port (commercial cargo, no passenger fit) and (b) Ras Al Khor Wildlife Sanctuary (protected). Both are real coastal locations with real jetties but politically/operationally NOT viable. Suggest `hide` sub-vocabulary: `hide-commercial-only`, `hide-protected`, `hide-no-jetty`.
  - efficiency: Single-write pattern worked cleanly — one JSON write, one validation script, one learnings-append. Confirmed coastal-poi can be 1-turn-write subagent when knowledge of the city is dense in training (Dubai). For less-familiar cities (Komodo, Likupang, RSG, NEOM) expect web-search sub-turns to be needed.
  - efficiency: Did NOT web-search per-POI sources — used canonical official URLs (rta.ae, dubaiharbour.com, nakheel.com, jumeirah.com, etc.) from knowledge. Risk: some URLs may have changed. Mitigation: official-brand-root URLs are stable; per-property deep-links are not.
  - efficiency: Unpromoted-entry watermark below most recent PROMOTION NOTE: 9 entries (Likupang+Brunei+5 dossiers+RSG+this coastal-poi). **3× threshold.** Parent has now been flagged SIX consecutive times. Parent consolidation severely overdue.
suggested-update:
  target-file: /agent/home/navier/world-map/regions/mena/dubai-uae.md
  change: |
    (1) Add Marsa Al Arab Marina (Dubai Holding, ~2026–27) as a 6th open charging-spec window sub-cluster alongside Dubai Harbour + World Islands + Dubai Islands.
    (2) Add Dubai Islands (Nakheel) as a distinct sub-cluster row separate from Heart of Europe / World — Nakheel-counterparty open-spec, distinct from Meraas-counterparty open-spec.
    (3) Add Marasi / Dubai Water Canal as its own sub-cluster row (currently implicit in Creek treatment).
    (4) Enumerate Heart of Europe boarding points explicitly: Main Marina + Lebanon Island + Floating Seahorse + Côte d'Azur + planned Anantara (5 entries within the single sub-cluster).
suggested-update:

Bab Al Nojoum + the broader Hudayriyat masterplan is in active buildout 2026 with greenfield pedestal-spec window genuinely open. Should be added to abu-dhabi-uae.md sub-clusters as a distinct row (currently absent — only Yas/Saadiyat/Maryah/Ghantoot/Sir Bani Yas appear). Worth promoting to abu-dhabi-uae.md "charging architecture fork" as the cleanest Aldar Pioneer II co-design hook (the existing Saadiyat Beach Club is a beach-landing, not a marina).

Marked `hide` (Reem = future Aldar masterplan candidate; Lulu = long-rumoured government redevelopment, undeveloped 2026). task: coastal-poi singapore — 51 boarding points
learnings:
  - methodology: SG yields 51 boarding points (target 20+ comfortably exceeded; below Dubai 71 and AUH 43 norm-adjusted because SG has NO RTA/abra equivalent — Singapore River Cruise bumboats + nascent MPA water-taxi pilot are the only meaningful scheduled-water-mobility network, vs. Dubai's ~20 named RTA stations). Relevance split P0:9 / P1:17 / P2:21 / hide:4 — only ~18% P0 (slightly below the proposed 15–25% benchmark band lower end) because most boarding density is at SDC/Sentosa secondary tier and at industrial yards which read as P2 (Penguin / Seatrium / ST Engineering / Strategic Marine — kept because of local-build narrative).
  - source-discovered: SG has **three distinct sovereign-adjacent marina counterparties** stacking neatly — SUTL (ONE°15, founder Arthur Tay), Keppel Land (Marina at Keppel Bay, Temasek-anchored), Raffles Marina (independent + diesel-refuel-ready). Plus SDC across all Sentosa Cove/Tanjong + Southern Islands. Four-counterparty stack at the marina layer is denser than AUH (Aldar+Modon dominant) and explains why a 2-boat Phase-1 SG pilot has higher counterparty optionality than equivalent AUH/Dubai. Worth feeding back to `singapore.md` charging-architecture section.
  - source-discovered: **MPA Maritime Drone Estate / Maritime Innovation Lab at Marina South** is a structurally critical "boarding point" that's actually a sandbox-water — the canonical SEA first-of-class cert-anchor instantiation surface. Should be elevated to a named anchor row in `singapore.md` sub-clusters and in `reference/first-of-class-certification-regimes.md` (currently described in prose, not as a physical asset).
  - source-discovered: **Marina South Pier + Tanah Merah + Changi + Changi Point + HarbourFront** are 5 distinct MPA/SCC-concession passenger ferry terminals — together they form a coherent "MPA-controlled cross-border ferry quintuple" that the SG city file treats as background. The 5-terminal quintuple is the actual operational front-line of the SG cross-border volume play (~5M+ pax/yr Riau corridor + Desaru/Pengerang MY). Worth promoting as a named asset class in the city file (parallel to Dubai's RTA Marine network).
  - source-discovered: **Greater Southern Waterfront (URA masterplan)** + Pulau Brani decommissioning is the largest single greenfield urban-water pedestal-spec design-in window globally in the dataset (30-year rolling masterplan, currently 2025–55 horizon). Multiple POIs in this file (Pulau Brani, Pasir Panjang/GSW future cluster) are placeholder rows for the same masterplan — should perhaps consolidate to one row with a "30yr-rolling-greenfield" tag, or split by phase. Schema question.
  - source-discovered: **MPA-designated water taxi pilot stops** (Esplanade / Bayfront / Promontory) are the smallest-footprint, highest-leverage Pioneer II retrofit opportunity in SG — sub-10m floating docks, sub-meter draft, MPA-direct gate. Singapore's mid-2020s analog to RTA water-taxi rollout in Dubai 2010s. Should be elevated to a named row in `singapore.md` waterways section.
  - schema-gap: Industrial yards (Penguin, Seatrium, ST Engineering, Strategic Marine) are not really passenger boarding points but are POI-relevant for local-build / partner narrative. I tagged them P2 with `working_harbour` type — but they're really "partner-yard" class with no passenger purpose. Suggest schema add: `shipyard_partner` type, or a `category=partner` tag orthogonal to type. Or: explicitly hide them with `hide-partner-yard`. Currently muddles the P2 set.
  - schema-gap: The MPA Maritime Drone Estate is sandbox-water (a polygon, not a pier) — I forced it into `floating_pontoon` for lack of a better type. Suggest add `sandbox_water` or `test_water` type for cert-anchor instances (MPA SG, MASS Dubai, MPC NEOM, etc.) — distinct from a passenger boarding point but strategically the most important.
  - schema-gap: Singapore River Cruise jetties are spread across 3+ stops (Clarke Quay, Boat Quay, Robertson Quay, MB loop) but I collapsed to one row. Same one-entry-per-property-rule precedent from Dubai/AUH learnings but this is a *network* not a property. Suggest schema add: explicit treatment for "scheduled-route network" — either one row per stop, or one row tagged `network=N stops` with representative coord. Currently inconsistent across files (Dubai enumerated RTA stops; AUH had none; SG collapsed bumboat).
  - schema-gap: Used `hide-commercial-only` × 3 (Pulau Tekong, Semakau, Tuas Mega Port, Changi Naval Base — 4 actually). Sub-vocab from Dubai/AUH learnings holds 3-of-3 cities. Promote.
  - efficiency: Single-write pattern held for SG (no web search needed). One JSON write + one validation script (coord-range check + type/relevance histogram). 51 POIs in one turn.
  - efficiency: Unpromoted-entry watermark below most recent PROMOTION NOTE: 11 entries (Likupang+Brunei+5 dossiers+RSG+Dubai-coastal-poi+AUH-coastal-poi+this SG-coastal-poi). **>3.5× threshold.** Parent has now been flagged EIGHT consecutive times. Parent consolidation severely overdue.

task: coastal-poi bali — 65 boarding points (Bali + Nusa + Gilis/Lombok + Sumba + Komodo arc)
learnings:
  - methodology: Bali "city" file is actually a 5-cluster archipelago arc (Bali mainland + Nusa Penida/Lembongan + Gilis/Lombok + Sumba + Komodo/LBJ). Forced expansion beyond a single-city scope — 65 points authored vs. Dubai 71 / SG 51 / AUH 43. **Pattern: when city file scopes a multi-island arc with Quanta-LR endpoints (Sumba ~210 nm, LBJ ~250 nm), boarding-points file must follow the same scope and enumerate distant endpoints (NIHI, Cap Karoso, AYANA Komodo, Amanwana Moyo).** Promote to subagent spec: "If the linked city file scopes a multi-cluster arc, boarding-points scope matches the city-file scope, not the city anchor radius."
  - methodology: Relevance split P0:20 / P1:22 / P2:21 / hide:2 — ~31% P0, slightly above proposed 15–25% P0 benchmark band. Reason: multi-cluster arc has multiple anchor classes (Bali hospitality flagships + Sanur ferry terminal + NIHI + LBJ marina + Aman properties scattered across the arc) — each cluster contributes its own P0 set. **Refine benchmark: multi-cluster arcs yield ~25–35% P0; single-anchor cities yield 15–25%.**
  - methodology: Type-vocabulary coverage 9 of 14 types used (hotel_jetty + ferry_terminal + public_pier + dive_centre + marina + working_harbour + beach_club_jetty + cruise_terminal + yacht_club). Absent: seaplane_base (no Bali seaplane), water_taxi_stop / abra_station / water_bus_terminal (no Bali public-water-mobility network — structural gap vs. Dubai), floating_pontoon / floating_helipad. Confirms `dive_centre` is the SEA-archetype that AUH/Dubai/SG don't trigger (Amed, Tulamben, Pemuteran, Crystal Bay).
  - source-discovered: **Bali has no public-water-mobility network whatsoever** (no RTA-style water bus, no abra, no scheduled water taxi). Every existing water leg is privately-operated fast-boat or resort-tender. Greenfield wedge for any Pioneer II MPA-style scheduled service in Sanur Bay or Nusa Dua arc — comparable to AUH's pre-RTA state but smaller. Worth surfacing in `bali-indonesia.md` charging-architecture section as "public-water-mobility = clean greenfield, no incumbent to displace, regulatory pathway via Dinas Pariwisata Bali + KSOP."
  - source-discovered: **AYANA group operates BOTH AYANA Resort Bali (Jimbaran/Rock Bar) AND AYANA Komodo (Waecicu).** Single brand-relationship = two-anchor wedge spanning Bali Pioneer II + LBJ Quanta-LR programs. Currently bali-indonesia.md mentions AYANA Komodo in the Komodo cluster row only; the AYANA-Bali↔AYANA-Komodo brand-anchor linkage is the single cleanest dual-cluster hospitality wedge in the file and deserves explicit promotion. Worth feeding back to `bali-indonesia.md` wedge hypotheses as a new dedicated wedge (#9) — "AYANA dual-anchor (Jimbaran Rock Bar + AYANA Komodo Waecicu): single GM-level relationship spanning Phase-1 Pioneer II + Phase-2 Quanta-LR (~250 nm) wedge."
  - source-discovered: **Marina Labuan Bajo (Waterfront)** + **AYANA Komodo Waecicu floating jetty** + **Plataran Komodo jetty** are the 3 operational LBJ luxury-marine landing points today; together they bracket the Quanta-LR Phase-2 LBJ-side fleet-base candidate set. Worth a dedicated "LBJ landing-point triad" line in the bali-indonesia.md Komodo sub-cluster row.
  - source-discovered: **Sampalan and Banjar Nyuh both operational on Nusa Penida** — Banjar Nyuh is primary post-2019, Sampalan is the Padang Bai-side endpoint. City file collapses Penida to single locale; the 2-pier structural reality matters for fleet operational planning (which side launches when based on Badung Strait sea state).
  - source-discovered: **Bangsal Harbour is cooperative-locked** (Koperasi Karya Bahari monopoly on Gili transfers from Lombok). This is a hard counterparty constraint distinct from typical KSOP/Pelindo dynamics — for any Lombok-side Gili pilot, **Teluk Nara/Teluk Kodek is the operationally cleaner alternative** despite Bangsal being the named pier. Worth adding to bali-indonesia.md holds.
  - source-discovered: **Mandalika beach + ITDC pre-spec greenfield design-in window** is uncaptured at boarding-point granularity in bali-indonesia.md (mentioned in sub-clusters as MotoGP/Pullman pipeline but no specific landing-point spec). Mandalika beach coord (-8.8983, 116.2728) is the Phase-2 Quanta-LR purpose-built terminal candidate site.
  - schema-gap: Multi-cluster Aman chain (Amankila + Amandari + Amanusa + Amanwana Moyo + Amanjiwo Java) spans 4 distinct geographies in the Bali arc. I enumerated 3 of 4 with boarding points (Amankila, Amanusa, Amanwana); Amandari is Ubud-inland and skipped per "must be within 50m of open water" rule. Worth promoting: **inland luxury-chain anchors that brand-link to coastal anchors should be referenced inline in coastal entries' notes field** (e.g. Amankila notes reference Amandari as Pioneer II road-feeder to coastal Aman); currently the linkage is implicit only.
  - schema-gap: 2 `hide` entries (Tanah Lot + Uluwatu temple) used hide-protected and hide-no-jetty sub-vocabulary respectively — confirms 4-of-4 cities use this sub-vocab. Promote.
  - efficiency: Single-write pattern held; 65 points in one JSON write + one validation script. No web search needed — Bali coastal knowledge dense in training. 3-turn run (read-phase / write+validate / learnings-append).
  - efficiency: Unpromoted-entry watermark below most recent PROMOTION NOTE: 12 entries (Likupang+Brunei+5 dossiers+RSG+Dubai-coastal-poi+AUH-coastal-poi+SG-coastal-poi+this Bali-coastal-poi). **4× threshold.** **Parent has now been flagged NINE consecutive times.** Parent consolidation severely overdue.

learnings:
  - methodology: Phuket = 54 POIs in a multi-cluster arc (Phuket island spine + west coast luxury belt + Phang Nga Bay + Yao Yai/Noi + Phi Phi/Krabi/Railay + Lanta edge + Similan/Surin DNP eco + Natai/Khao Lak northern extension). Below Dubai 71 / Bali 65 but above SG 51 / AUH 43. **Hospitality-density inflates hotel_jetty share to 29 of 54 (~54%) — highest in the dataset by far** vs. Bali ~30% / Dubai ~15%. Confirms Phuket's "highest-density luxury hospitality concentration in SEA" framing structurally at the boarding-point layer.
  - methodology: Relevance split P0:18 / P1:19 / P2:15 / hide:2 — 33% P0, top end of multi-cluster-arc benchmark (25–35% Bali precedent). Marina-spine (RPM, Boat Lagoon, Ao Po Grand) + flagship hospitality (Amanpuri, Trisara, Anantara Layan/Mai Khao, Six Senses Yao Noi, COMO Point Yamu, Rosewood, Banyan Tree, Rayavadee, Phulay Bay) + critical public infra (Chalong, Bang Rong, Ao Nang, Phi Phi Tonsai) = 18 P0 cleanly.
  - source-discovered: **Natai Beach / Khao Lak (Phang Nga south)** is materially under-flagged in phuket-phang-nga-thailand.md — Aleenta + Iniala are the existing luxury anchors on Natai (just N of Sarasin Bridge from Phuket airport), and Khao Lak proper has Sarojin / Pullman / The Sands as the Similan-gateway luxury cluster. The city file mentions Khao Lak only implicitly via Thap Lamu pier. Worth promoting as a distinct "Natai/Khao Lak northern extension sub-cluster" — Phuket-adjacent but Phang Nga-province; pre-spec greenfield-style hospitality density emerging.
  - source-discovered: **Maya Bay reopened 2022 with permanent zoned floating-pontoon arrival at rear of island + 4000 visitor/day cap** — DNP-permit-gated; this is the most operationally instructive DNP precedent for Quanta-LR quiet-mode + zoned-anchorage SOPs. Worth feeding back to phuket-phang-nga-thailand.md DNP section with explicit Maya Bay zoned-pontoon precedent.
  - source-discovered: **Phi Phi Laem Tong (north Phi Phi)** is the quiet-luxury escape from Tonsai chaos — Zeavola + SAii Phi Phi Island Village anchor a north-end resort cluster that the city file collapses into generic Phi Phi. Worth surfacing as distinct: Tonsai (commodity-volume) vs. Laem Tong (luxury) on Phi Phi Don.
  - source-discovered: **Anantara group chain wedge** is unusually concentrated in Phuket — Anantara Layan + Anantara Mai Khao + Naka Island (Minor-operated) + Andaman Cruises (Minor-affiliated charter operator) = **4 boarding-point footprints on a single Minor group MOU.** Worth promoting as the densest single-group leverage point in any city file so far.
  - source-discovered: **Marina-counterparty stack at Phuket east coast is 4-deep**: Royal Phuket Marina (Gulu Lalvani / family-owned) + Phuket Boat Lagoon (PCL listed) + Ao Po Grand (private) + Yacht Haven (private). All 4 mainland-Phuket-east-coast within 20 km. Mirrors SG's 4-counterparty marina stack (SUTL/Keppel/Raffles/SDC) — both are unusually optionality-rich vs. AUH (Aldar+Modon dominant) or Dubai (DMYC dominant).
  - schema-gap: `floating_pontoon` would have been correct for Maya Bay's zoned floating arrival but I used `public_pier` (DNP-operated). For consistency with DNP marine-park instances, `public_pier` + DNP operator field carries the regulatory framing better than `floating_pontoon`. Suggest schema clarification: when DNP/NP-zoned anchorage/mooring is the boarding mode, prefer `public_pier` with operator=DNP + notes capturing the anchorage-vs-jetty reality.
  - schema-gap: Two `hide` entries used hide-no-jetty (Patong main beach commodity layer + Keemala inland forest-resort). 5-of-5 cities now use the `hide-{commercial-only,protected,no-jetty}` sub-vocab. Long overdue for promotion to schema.
  - efficiency: Single-write pattern held; 54 POIs in one JSON write + one validation script (coord bbox + type/relevance histogram + id uniqueness). No web search needed — Phuket dense in training. 3-turn run.
  - efficiency: Unpromoted-entry watermark below most recent PROMOTION NOTE: 13 entries (Likupang+Brunei+5 dossiers+RSG+Dubai-cpoi+AUH-cpoi+SG-cpoi+Bali-cpoi+this Phuket-cpoi). **>4× threshold.** Parent consolidation escalation #10. Severely overdue.

subagent: navier-coastal-poi.md
task: coastal-poi red-sea-global — 41 boarding points (RSG Shura + Sheybarah + Ummahat + Turtle Bay + RSI + AMAALA Triple Bay/Coastal/Island + gateway towns Umluj/Al Wajh/Yanbu)
learnings:
  - methodology: RSG is the first **closed-destination greenfield** boarding-points file — almost every point is greenfield (under build or planned), with only ~5 OPEN today (St Regis Ummahat, Nujuma, Shebara Sheybarah, AMAALA Yacht Club, Fly Red Sea seaplane + RSI). Density therefore reads as "potential" rather than "operational." 41 POIs lands below Dubai 71 / Bali 65 / Phuket 54 / SG 51 / AUH 43; on par with AUH. **Pattern: closed-destination greenfield cities yield ~30–50 POIs (vs. mature-marina cities 50–70+) because the entire portfolio is one master-counterparty (RSG) and POI granularity is limited by what RSG publicly discloses.** Promote to subagent spec as a typology row.
  - methodology: Relevance split P0:10 / P1:15 / P2:14 / hide:2 — 24% P0, within single-anchor benchmark (15–25%) despite being a multi-cluster arc (Shura + Sheybarah + Ummahat + AMAALA + RSI + gateway towns). Reason: AMAALA Yacht Club + Ocean Race 2027 event pontoon + Triple Bay Marina + Four Seasons AMAALA + 3 open RSG-side resort jetties + Shura Marina + Turtle Bay + RSI = exactly 10 P0; remaining Shura hotel-jetties default to P1 (planned not yet open) and outer Al Wajh / wellness brands default to P2. **The "open today" filter is doing most of the P0 lift here, not the city-file marquee list — confirms a closed-destination heuristic distinct from open-market hospitality cities.**
  - methodology: Used 8 of 14 types — hotel_jetty (23), marina (5), working_harbour (4), floating_pontoon (4), ferry_terminal (2), seaplane_base (2), floating_helipad (1). Hotel_jetty share at 56% — HIGHEST of dataset, beating Phuket's 54% — confirms RSG/AMAALA is the most hospitality-anchored city file in Navier dataset (per the Phuket "hospitality-density >40% → hotel-group-MOU-led pitch" learning, RSG is the canonical instance — except the MOU consolidates into a SINGLE counterparty (RSG corporate / John Pagano)). Absent types: yacht_club (AMAALA YC is structurally a marina), public_pier, water_taxi_stop, water_bus_terminal, abra_station, dive_centre, beach_club_jetty, cruise_terminal. Confirms RSG has zero scheduled-water-mobility incumbent layer — greenfield wedge.
  - source-discovered: **RSG/AMAALA single-counterparty consolidation is structurally distinct from every other dataset city.** Where Phuket has 4-deep marina counterparty stack and SG has 4-counterparty marina stack and Dubai has Meraas/Nakheel/Dubai Holding/Aldar etc., RSG/AMAALA has ONE counterparty (RSG corporate) that owns the marinas, the resort jetties (through brand MOUs), the ops base, the airport coast jetty, and the seaplane JV. **Single-MOU-unlocks-all is the operating reality** — Navier pitch is 1-anchor not 4-anchor. Worth promoting to `red-sea-global-ksa.md` charging-architecture section as the single sharpest counterparty-collapse instance in the dataset.
  - source-discovered: **Brand-pair dual-anchor wedges across RSG↔AMAALA portfolio** — Four Seasons Shura ↔ Four Seasons AMAALA; Rosewood Shura ↔ Rosewood AMAALA; Six Senses Southern Dunes ↔ Six Senses AMAALA. Three brand-groups have dual properties across the two RSG sub-portfolios → single brand-HQ MOU unlocks both clusters. Mirrors AYANA Bali↔AYANA Komodo single-MOU-dual-cluster pattern from Bali learnings, but at 3× density. Worth adding to red-sea-global-ksa.md wedge hypotheses as "brand-pair-dual-anchor" — distinct from #3 (single-property signature arrival) and #1 (RSG corporate framework MOU).
  - source-discovered: **Ocean Race 2027 race village pontoons** belong in the boarding-points file as a P0 named event-pontoon (not just dossier deliverable content). Single most consequential time-bound boarding point in the entire dataset — analogous to NEOM MPC sandbox-water (SG learning) but event-bound rather than cert-bound. Confirms `floating_pontoon` type covers event pontoons in absence of dedicated `event_pontoon` type proposed in earlier Dubai learning.
  - source-discovered: **Umluj is materially a "Saudi Maldives" domestic-tourism gateway today** — outside the RSG perimeter but with active Saudi-domestic boat-tour operators. Worth elevation in red-sea-global-ksa.md as an "outside-perimeter domestic-tourism wedge" distinct from the closed-perimeter UHNW play — Pioneer II domestic-tourism positioning at Umluj could be a soft-launch parallel track ahead of RSG framework signature, especially during Ramadan/Hajj RSG blackouts.
  - schema-gap: For closed-destination greenfield cities, many resort jetties are "planned but not yet built" — I assigned coords by extrapolating from Shura masterplan + AMAALA Triple Bay layout but these are estimates. Suggest schema add explicit `status` field: `open|under_construction|planned|hide` distinct from `relevance`. Currently I'm conflating status into notes; would be cleaner as structured. Affects Maldives + NEOM + future Wakanda-class closed-destination files heavily.
  - schema-gap: `seaplane_base` used 2× confirms type-vocab fit for RSG (matches Maldives expectation + AUH Rotana Jet learning). Promote stability 3-of-3.

  - schema-gap: Used `hide` × 2 (Southern Dunes inland + Desert Rock inland — both hide-no-jetty). 6-of-6 cities now use hide sub-vocab. **STRONG PROMOTE: `hide-{commercial-only,protected,no-jetty}` sub-vocabulary is dataset-stable. Should be hard-coded into subagent spec.**

date: 2026-05-30
subagent: navier-atlas-visual-audit.md
task: atlas v7 visual audit — 6 cities (Dubai/AUH/SG/Bali/Phuket/RSG)
learnings:
  - bugs-found:
      P0-cross-border-route-stubs:
        - Cross-border / inter-city routes resolve destination coord to the ORIGIN-city anchor (or a near-origin POI), not the actual destination city coord.
        - Confirmed in 4 of 6 cities (Dubai→Muscat/Doha/Manama all stub in Dubai metro; AUH→Doha/Manama/Muscat all stub in Dubai metro; Bali→Sumba/LBJ stub at Bali anchor; RSG→NEOM/Jeddah stub at RSG anchor; SG→Batam stub at Jakarta).
        - Phuket cross-border endpoints (Penang/Samui/Phangan/Similan/Surin) are CORRECT — implies the lookup works when the destination city has been authored with a separate node id and coord that the builder reaches; fails when destination city id doesn't resolve and builder falls back to a same-city POI with a matching name suffix.
      P0-no-land-avoidance:
        - All 264 routes are straight great-circle interpolations (~25 coords/15 nm). Zero land-avoidance routing across the entire atlas.
        - Visible land-crossings: Phuket→Penang/Samui through Malay peninsula (~120 km land); Bali Quanta-LR N across Bali central volcanic ridge; SG Marina Bay→Pulau Ubin across Bishan/Toa Payoh; AUH dashed E-W across Abu Dhabi island; Dubai diagonals across UAE desert; RSG N across Tabuk inland.
      P1-icon-type-collapse:
        - All POI features have `properties.type === "poi"` — the rich coastal-poi v2026-05-30 type vocabulary (marina/ferry/hotel_jetty/seaplane_base/abra_station/yacht_club/working_harbour/sandbox_water/event_pontoon/shipyard_partner/etc.) is FLATTENED in the GeoJSON build. Legend colors must derive from a different field (or are not actually differentiated and colors in screenshots are label colors only — needs verification).
      P1-RSG-anchor-inland:
        - RSG city anchor at [37.05, 26.45] is ~25–40 km inland on Saudi mainland near Umluj — should be over the Lagoon midpoint of the RSG+AMAALA arc (e.g. [36.85, 26.0]).
      P1-RSI-zero-length-route:
        - `edge__red-sea-global-ksa__red-sea-international-airport-rsi-coast` has identical start/end (distance metadata 22.5 nm) — likely missing POI coord lookup, pipeline collapses to anchor.
      P2-URL-deeplink-one-way:
        - `?city={slug}` reads on load and flies camera, but clicking city pills updates only the `#camera=` hash, not the `?city=` query. Shared URLs after navigation don't preserve city focus.
  - pattern-recognized:
      - **Universal: every cross-border / inter-cluster route is a stub when the destination city node id has a different naming convention than the route's `to` field expects.** Phuket is the canonical counter-example where it works — investigating Phuket's nodes vs. others' would isolate the resolver bug. Likely a slug-mismatch (e.g. `muscat-oman` city node vs `muscat` referenced in the route `to`).
      - **Universal: zero land-avoidance.** Affects every multi-cluster city. Needs a routing layer (sea-only navmesh via natural-earth coastlines + Dijkstra, or hand-authored waypoints from `route-waypoints.json` which exists in atlas-external/ but appears unused).
      - **Universal: icon-type collapse.** Single-line build-pipeline fix.
      - **Cluster: stub-failure density correlates with multi-cluster arc geometry.** RSG (5 stubs/12 edges) > Bali (3 stubs/6 east-arc edges) > Dubai/AUH (3 stubs each on cross-border) > Phuket (0). RSG is worst because the entire portfolio is multi-cluster and route names use sub-cluster suffixes.
      - **Worst city: RSG** — anchor inland + 5 stubs + 1 zero-length route + N-S long-hauls through Saudi desert.
  - audit-protocol-improvements:
      - **Browser canvas-based maps don't expose `[data-poi]` DOM selectors** — must query `window.map.getSource(<id>)._data.features` for POI/route counts. Update subagent spec to use the maplibre source API not DOM selectors.
      - **Cross-checking route endpoints via `evaluate` is 100× more efficient than visual inspection** — pulling `{id, distance_nm, start, end}` for every edge filtered by city slug in ONE evaluate call reveals stub-failures that screenshots can't (because stub-routes render as a tiny dot near the origin and look "fine"). Promote: every city audit should include a route-endpoint sanity check (distance_nm vs. great-circle(start,end) ratio — flag >5x mismatch as stub).
      - **POI type-flatness only visible via `evaluate` on `Set(features.map(f=>f.properties.type))`** — if the set is `{"poi"}` then type-collapse is happening regardless of how legend appears.
      - **URL-state check**: navigate to `?city=dubai`, click another pill, then read `window.location.search` — if it doesn't include the new city slug, deep-link is one-way.
      - **Zoom strategy**: city-anchor zoom (~11) is right for Dubai/AUH/SG; ~9 for Bali; ~10 for Phuket; ~7.5 for RSG. At z<8 POIs cluster — individual placement verification needs second pass at z>=11 over each sub-cluster centroid. This audit did NOT do the second pass — future runs should.
suggested-update:
  target-file: /agent/subagents/navier-atlas-visual-audit.md
  change: |
    (1) Replace DOM-selector POI/route counting with maplibre source-data API: `window.map.getSource('pois')._data.features.length`, same for `routes`, `cities`, `locales`, `rings`.
    (2) Add mandatory route-endpoint stub check: for each city, evaluate `routes filter by from/to includes city-slug → map to (id, distance_nm, start, end) → flag any edge where haversine(start,end) < distance_nm × 0.2 as STUB. This caught 5 of 6 cities' biggest defect class in this audit.
    (3) Add icon-type-collapse check: `new Set(pois.features.map(f=>f.properties.type)).size === 1` → flag as type-flatness P0.
    (4) Add second-pass zoom: after the city-anchor screenshot, zoom to each sub-cluster centroid (multi-cluster cities) at z≥12 and verify POI placement individually. Bali (Nusa/Gilis/LBJ/Sumba), RSG (Shura/Sheybarah/Triple Bay), Phuket (Phi Phi/Yao/Similan), AUH (Sir Bani Yas), Dubai (World Islands) all need sub-cluster passes.
    (5) Add URL deep-link bi-directional test: navigate `?city=A`, click pill B, read location.search, expect `city=B`.
    (6) Default screenshot save path: `/agent/home/navier/atlas-external/audit-reports/v{v}-screenshots/{city}.png` (this run established that convention).
suggested-update:
  target-file: /agent/home/navier/atlas-external/build.py
  change: |
    Likely fixes (parent / build owner to confirm):
    (1) Route resolver: when `route.to` references a city slug, look up that slug in the `cities` source FIRST; only fall back to intra-city POI suffix lookup if the city node doesn't exist. Currently the order seems reversed (or city lookup is missing). Phuket works because Penang/Samui/etc. are authored as their own city nodes with matching slugs; Dubai's Muscat/Doha/Manama, Bali's Sumba/LBJ/Komodo, RSG's NEOM/Jeddah likely either aren't authored as separate city nodes or have slug mismatches.
    (2) Land-avoidance routing: integrate `route-waypoints.json` (already present in atlas-external/) into the route builder. If a route has authored waypoints, use them; otherwise interpolate. Eventually add coastline-aware Dijkstra over a sea navmesh.
    (3) POI type propagation: pass through `feature.properties.type` to the rendered GeoJSON. Currently all features land as `type:"poi"` — the rich subtype field (`hotel_jetty`/`marina`/`ferry_terminal`/etc.) is being dropped in the build pipeline. Add to `nodes.json → pois.geojson` mapper.
    (4) RSG anchor coord: move from [37.05, 26.45] (inland) to maritime midpoint e.g. [36.85, 26.0]. Also gives stub-failure routes a more sensible fallback location.
    (5) RSI zero-length route: investigate `red-sea-international-airport-rsi-coast` — start=end indicates POI coord lookup returned anchor. Fix POI authoring or fallback path.
    (6) URL deep-link sync: emit `?city={slug}` in URL when pills clicked (currently only camera hash updates).
unpromoted-watermark: 1

date: 2026-05-28
subagent: navier-coastal-poi.md
task: coastal-poi bangkok (Chao Phraya + Upper Gulf arc)
learnings:
  - typology: **Hybrid hospitality-anchor + intermediate-scheduled-network** archetype. 68 POIs total — 18 P0 / 28 P1 / 16 P2 / 6 hide. hotel_jetty share 38% (just under the 40% hospitality-density flag); public_pier share 43% (river-density-driven). Sits between SG (intermediate scheduled-network) and Phuket (hospitality-anchor) archetypes; matches the 50–70 multi-cluster arc benchmark.
  - multi-cluster scope applied: 4 clusters enumerated end-to-end (Chao Phraya urban — Pattaya — Hua Hin — Samet/Chang/Kood). Cross-Gulf Koh Samui retained as single counterparty-pointer row per cross-file rule (`koh-samui-thailand.md` owns endpoint enumeration).
  - scheduled-network rule applied twice: (a) **CPEB ~30+ stops collapsed to single summary row** (Supapanich incumbent, diesel, fleet-renewal upgrade target). (b) **BMA electric water-taxi 2026 pilot collapsed to single under_construction row** — flagged as the **single most time-sensitive pedestal-spec window in Bangkok file** (4 new piers commissioning in 2026, Candela lock-in risk acute). The under_construction status field carries the time-decay signal cleanly.
  - hide sub-vocab heavy usage (6 entries — 2× more than typical hospitality city): 3× hide-protected (Klai Kangwon, Grand Palace frontage, Bang Pa-In summer palace — **royal-overlook is unique to Bangkok/Hua Hin**, no analog in Dubai/SG/Bali files), 2× hide-commercial-only (Klong Toey Port, Sattahip Naval Base), 1× hide-no-jetty pitch-trap (Saen Saep — retained explicitly to flag the canal as not a Navier route). **Suggested promotion: `hide-royal-overlook` as a new sub-class** — distinct from hide-protected (ecological) — applies to Thailand + Saudi diwan-adjacent + Brunei royal-bay; Bangkok has 3 instances.
  - charging-spec window classes (Dubai-canonical 5-class set extended): Bangkok exhibits all 5 within-city + a 6th — (1) open-greenfield (Bang Na intermodal, BMA 4-new-piers, Ocean Marina Pattaya); (2) closed-retrofit (~all hospitality piers); (3) HOLD-gated public (CPEB upgrade); (4) royal-overlook-restricted operational-not-spec (Klai Kangwon stretch — operationally usable but marketing-restricted); (5) Quanta-LR-diesel-bypass (Hua Hin/Samet/Chang/Kood); (6) **DNP-NP-zoned anchorage** (Na Dan / Mu Ko Chang NP — DNP-as-operator convention applied). #4 and #6 are Bangkok-specific extensions to the canonical 5-class.
  - counterparty-stack: **N-deep stack** (Bangkok central) — Minor (Anantara/Avani) + Marriott-via-AWC + Hilton + IHG (InterCon, Six Senses) + Hyatt + Accor (SO/Sofitel) + KEHG (Shangri-La) + MOHG + Peninsula + FS + Capella + Soneva + Centara/Central + Banyan Tree + Cape & Kantary + Royal Cliff family + Chatrium + Wyndham + AWC + Siam Piwat + CP + Magnolia + BMA + Marine Dept + 4 provincial Marine Offices + DNP + PAT. **Single MOU does not unlock the city** — opposite of RSG; closest analog Dubai N-deep but with royal-overlook layer Dubai lacks.
  - one-entry-per-property rule split: FS Chao Phraya + Capella Bangkok retained as distinct entries despite sharing pier infrastructure (distinct GMs, distinct brand wedges); Anantara Riverside + Avani+ Riverside retained as distinct (Minor portfolio but distinct sub-brand demand surfaces).
  - hospitality-chain wedge highest-leverage: **Anantara Riverside Bangkok entry annotated as the chain-wedge anchor** — single MOU spans Bangkok + Hua Hin + Phuket Layan + Mai Khao + Banana Island Doha + Sir Bani Yas AUH + future Kihavah Maldives (4-country portfolio).
  - Quanta-LR endpoint highest-conviction: **Soneva Kiri (Koh Kood)** — water-only-arrival ultra-luxe; coordinate-with-Dotan-Maldives-thread blocker noted.
  - DNP convention applied at Na Dan (Koh Samet) + Ao Sapparot (Koh Chang) — operator=DNP/Mu Ko Samet NP + Mu Ko Chang NP overlay; pairs with Phuket Similan/Surin DNP precedent.

task: coastal-poi hong-kong (Victoria Harbour + Lantau + outlying + Sai Kung + South-side)
learnings:
  - typology: **Intermediate scheduled-network city** archetype but with strong yacht-club / hospitality overlay → 72 POIs (11 P0 / 32 P1 / 18 P2 / 11 hide). hotel_jetty share 17% (below 40% density flag — confirms NOT a hospitality-anchor archetype despite waterfront luxury cluster); ferry_terminal share 32% + public_pier 25% = TD-managed-pier dominance is the structural signature. Closest analog Singapore (intermediate scheduled-network), but HK is **TD-pier-concentrated** where SG is marina/scheduled mix.
  - Central Piers 1–10 enumerated per-pier (not collapsed) — 8 of 10 used; Piers 7+10 + 8 each get distinct entries because they have structurally distinct counterparty stories (Star Ferry concession, public landing, Maritime Museum tenant). This is the **first city where a single block of consecutive numbered public piers carries 8 distinct counterparty stacks** — promote as scheduled-network enumeration sub-rule: "numbered-pier blocks with distinct concession-per-pier are per-pier-enumerated even when consecutive."
  - counterparty-stack: **N-deep stack with sovereign-state HOLD overlay** — Transport Dept HKSAR + Marine Dept HKSAR + AAHK + LCSD + AFCD as the public layer; HKR International (DB single-shareholder) + Shun Tak (Macau corridor) + Sino Group (3-pier portfolio: Marina Cove + Gold Coast + Fullerton) + CKH (Hutchison Ports + HUD) + Swire (Pacific Place inland + HUD JV) + Jardine/Hongkong Land/MOHG + HSH (Peninsula) + New World/Rosewood + Kerry/Shangri-La as private layer. **Single MOU does not unlock the city.** Sino 3-pier portfolio is the highest single-counterparty leverage point (Marina Cove + Gold Coast YC + Fullerton Ocean Park).
  - charging-spec window classes: HK exhibits a **unique 5-class set distinct from Dubai-canonical**: (1) HOLD-gated TD public piers — all 10 Central piers + Wan Chai + TST Star + cross-harbour grid (~12 piers); (2) club-microgrid (RHKYC ×3 + Aberdeen Marina Club + Aberdeen Boat Club + Hebe Haven + DB Marina Club + Gold Coast YC); (3) HKR single-shareholder open-spec (DB pier + DB Marina Club — **strongest pedestal-installation pathway in the city**); (4) AAHK concession (SkyPier — open question on concession structure); (5) cruise-terminal greenfield (Kai Tak event pontoon + Ocean Terminal apron). **No abra/water-taxi analog** despite kaito cultural pattern — kaito are private sampan operators on TD piers, no separate licensing layer (unlike Dubai RTA Marine).
  - hide sub-vocab heavy usage (11 entries — heaviest of any city to date, tied with Bangkok at 6 then exceeding): 5× hide-protected (Mai Po Ramsar + Hoi Ha Wan + Sha Chau/Lung Kwu Chau + HK Wetland Park + dolphin-habitat); 2× hide-commercial-only (Kwai Tsing container + Stonecutters PLA Navy — **PLA-Navy military-base hide is unique to HK in the 8-city sweep**, suggests promotion of `hide-military` sub-class distinct from hide-commercial-only); 4× hide-no-jetty (Repulse Bay beach + Deep Water Bay beach + Pacific Place inland-hotels ×2 + Disneyland Penny's Bay).
  - **inland-anchor-referencing applied**: Upper House + Island Shangri-La authored as `hide-no-jetty` entries but with brand-link notes pointing to coastal sister-properties (Kerry Hotel Hung Hom = Shangri-La sister; Pacific Place tenant-amenity commute = Swire wedge). This is the **first dense urban Pacific-Place-style inland-anchor hide pattern** — distinct from Bali Amandari/Keemala convention (resort vs. urban).
  - Macau-corridor cross-border: HK-Macau Ferry Terminal (Shun Tak) + China Ferry Terminal (TST) authored as P0/P1 but flagged partnership-or-pass; Macau-side counterparty stack lives in a separate (unauthored) Macau city file — cross-border-to-unauthored discipline applied per shared §X handling table.
  - **PLA-Navy military overhead is unique counterparty-stack signal** — affects nothing operationally but Stonecutters proximity to Kwai Tsing container shipping + Western Harbour Fairway requires sensitivity in route corridors (analog: ADNOC offshore terminals in AUH but politically far heavier).
  - Quanta-LR fit: HK is comfortably in-range for Pioneer II on ALL intra-HK legs (range non-binding per city file). Quanta-LR's primary HK value = HK↔Macau ~37 nm one-way which removes Macau-side pedestal dependency; secondary value = remote-end Sai Kung (Tap Mun, Wong Shek) and west-Lantau (Tai O) diesel-bypass eliminates remote pedestal capex.

date: 2026-05-28
subagent: navier-coastal-poi.md
task: coastal-poi male-maldives (national archipelago — gateway + 8 atoll-radials Malé/N-Malé/S-Malé/Ari/Baa/Noonu/Raa/Lhaviyani/Laamu/Dhaalu-Thaa-Meemu-Faafu/Gaafu/Addu/Shaviyani-HaaAlifu-HaaDhaalu)
learnings:
  - typology: **Hospitality-anchor + single-gateway archipelago** archetype — extreme variant. 86 POIs (22 P0 / 29 P1 / 30 P2 / 5 hide). **hotel_jetty share 79% (68/86) — highest in the 9-city sweep, nearly 2× the 40% hospitality-density flag and 1.4× Phuket's 54%.** This is the canonical instance of the archetype: country IS its hospitality stack. Single MOU with TMA/Blackstone OR with top-3 hospitality groups (Soneva + LVMH-Cheval-Blanc + Six Senses-IHG) effectively unlocks national fleet.
  - multi-cluster-arc scope rule strongly applied: 8 atoll radials enumerated end-to-end from MLE single gateway, including deep-south Gaafu/Addu ~250–300 nm endpoints (Park Hyatt Hadahaa, Ayada, Equator Village Addu). Per platform-specs reframe, all distance-banded as Pioneer II in-range / Pioneer-edge / Pioneer-out-Quanta-LR-in / Quanta-LR-mid-cruise — not stamped out of scope.
  - counterparty-stack: **N-deep but with single-gateway concentration overlay** — distinct from Dubai N-deep (geographically dispersed). All 65+ resort jetties funnel through MLE Velana resort speedboat lounges + TMA/Manta seaplane terminals. Highest single-counterparty leverage: TMA/Blackstone (asset-holder template AND incumbent). Secondary high-leverage: Soneva (Dotan-warm, 3-property), Marriott (5 properties: Ritz-Carlton Fari, W Fesdu, Sheraton, St. Regis Vommuli, JW Vagaru, Westin Miriandhoo), Minor International/Anantara (4 properties: Dhigu+Veli+Naladhu+Kihavah+Niyama), Aitken Spence (3 properties — also Sri Lanka MRO triangulation linkage).
  - **Velana airport resort-boat-lounge complex (mv-velana-resort-boat-lounges) = the single most concentrated displacement target globally** — every short-hop transfer in country departs here. No equivalent single-airport-marine-gateway in the 9-city sweep. Should be elevated to a city-file Open Question: AAM concession structure for resort-leased berths?
  - charging-spec window classes — **Maldives-canonical 4-class set distinct from Dubai's 5-class**: (1) resort-microgrid closed-retrofit (~90% of POIs — mature resorts); (2) resort-microgrid open during renovation/expansion cycle (Soneva expansion, Cheval Blanc renovation, Joali expansion); (3) **resort-microgrid solar-flagship** (Kudadoo 100% solar, Soneva Fushi/Jani solar-heavy — strongest pedestal-install fits); (4) **greenfield public-terminal-anchored Hulhumalé Phase II** (only credible shared multi-resort hub). Quanta-LR diesel-bypass collapses 1+2 into a non-issue for ~90% of country. **The solar-flagship sub-class (#3) is unique to Maldives in the 9-city sweep** — promote as 7th canonical charging-spec window class.
  - **One-entry-per-property rule split at Anantara Dhigu/Veli/Naladhu cluster** (shared pier, 3 distinct brand SKUs) and **Taj Exotica + Taj Coral Reef** (2 properties, single Sampriti-Taj MOU). Soneva Fushi/Jani/Secret kept distinct (3 atolls).
  - **`status` field carries P0 narrative weight here uniquely**: 1 under_construction (Capella Fari — pedestal-spec time-bound window) + 1 planned (Hulhumalé Phase II — concession-availability window). Both are time-decay items the relevance field alone cannot capture.
  - hide sub-vocab: 5 entries — 2× hide-protected (Hanifaru Bay UNESCO, S Ari Whale Shark MPA), 2× hide-commercial-only (Malé Commercial Harbour, Thilafushi Industrial), 1× **hide-military** (MNDF Coast Guard Base — applying the HK-promoted sub-class).
  - **Candela on-record competitor**: 12 Ego Shuttle vessels deployed (per city file). Not authored as POIs (operator-mobile, not boarding-point-fixed) but every short-hop hotel_jetty entry in N Malé / S Malé / inner Ari is head-to-head zone. Time-bound trigger: any incumbent atoll's Candela contract renewal → Navier displacement window.
  - **Quanta-LR-bypass-of-pedestal-civil-works** is the single dominant Series-B narrative for Maldives. ~65 of 68 hotel_jetty entries note this. Diesel-bunkering co-locates with existing resort genset supply chain → collapses 2–5 year per-resort pedestal timeline to "fuel already exists."
  - **Inland-anchor referencing not applicable** (no inland luxury chain in MV — country is 99% water). Confirms inland-chain-anchor rule is genuinely SEA-mainland-specific.
  - **DNP/NP convention not applied** — Maldives EPA + UNESCO Biosphere overlay differs from Thai DNP regime; no permit-gated NP boarding pontoon. Hanifaru is permit-gated anchorage (no jetty), tagged hide-protected.

date: 2026-05-30
subagent: navier-coastal-poi.md
task: coastal-poi langkawi (Andaman MY archipelago + cross-border-immigration-gated MY↔TH micro-corridor cluster)
learnings:
  - typology: **Hospitality-anchor + single-gateway archipelago** archetype with cross-border-immigration-gated overlay. 43 POIs (10 P0 / 16 P1 / 13 P2 / 4 hide). hotel_jetty share 35% (under 40% density flag — sits just below Bangkok 38% and well under Phuket 54% / Maldives 79%), but YTL's locally-operated 2-property concentration (Datai + Andaman) + Marriott 4-property single-island concentration (Ritz/St Regis/Westin/Andaman-Autograph) compress the hospitality leverage onto fewer counterparties than the share suggests. **Effectively a 4-deep counterparty stack** (YTL + Marriott + Berjaya + LADA) — not N-deep despite multiple chains. Sits between Phuket east-coast (4-deep stack canonical) and RSG (1-counterparty consolidation) in counterparty concentration.
  - cross-border-immigration-gated pier-pair archetype materially shapes POI scope: Bundhaya Pier (Koh Lipe TH) + Tarutao Ao Talo Wao + Koh Adang/Rawi enumerated as P0/P1/P2 per multi-cluster-arc scope rule — city-file scope includes 12-17 nm cross-border micro-corridor cluster. Pak Bara TH-mainland gateway also retained as tertiary. **First instance of cross-border POIs added under a MY-side coastal-poi file by virtue of immigration-gated pier-pair archetype** (HK Macau-side handled differently — partnership pointer, Macau file unauthored).
  - Phuket-side cross-border endpoints (Royal Phuket Marina, Ao Po Grand, etc.) NOT enumerated — cross-file rule per phuket-phang-nga-thailand.md ownership of TH-side at 110-nm distance band. Confirms multi-cluster-arc scope rule has an **upper-distance natural break**: micro-corridor (12-17 nm) = include cross-border; routine cross-border (>100 nm) = cross-file pointer only.
  - charging-spec window classes — Langkawi exhibits a **5-class set with mainland-tethered-grid-SPOF overlay**: (1) open-spec design-in (Telaga Harbour — highest-leverage MY pedestal-design-in window); (2) club-microgrid (RLYC, Rebak Marina); (3) closed-retrofit (Kuah Jetty Point Complex, Cruise Terminal, Pelangi/Casa del Mar/per-resort piers); (4) DNP/LADA Geopark-Unit-zoned anchorage (Kilim Karst, Dayang Bunting, Pulau Payar — using DNP convention but with LADA-side analog: LADA Geopark Unit + DOF Marine Park); (5) Quanta-LR diesel-bypass class spanning ALL grid-SPOF nodes — sharpest single positioning lever (cf. mainland-tethered-island SPOF promotion). **No abra/water-taxi/scheduled-network analog** (LADA + Langkawi Ferry Services = commodity-ferry only; no premium PTA layer).
  - **gateway-concentration POI pattern (Maldives Velana analog) DOES NOT apply** — LGK airport has NO seaplane terminal and NO resort-boat-lounge complex. Authored `lgk-langkawi-airport-marine-link` as P2 planned-greenfield with explicit "no incumbent" note. This is structurally a wedge: Langkawi has NO Maldives-Velana-style consolidated air-marine handoff, so any concession openin MAHB pursues becomes uncontested.
  - **hide-royal-overlook** (Bangkok-promoted) applied to Istana Anak Bukit as a NEGATIVE confirmation entry — palace is inland mainland Alor Setar, no Langkawi water-frontage. Useful to surface the absence explicitly (matches HH-verification clear). Suggests hide-royal-overlook entries should ALWAYS be included even when negative, to document HH-verification result structurally.
  - **hide-military** (HK-promoted) applied to MMEA/RMN — clean usage; no domestic naval base on Langkawi itself, but maritime zone overlay sufficient to warrant hide entry.
  - LIMA (Langkawi International Maritime & Aerospace Exhibition) biennial = event_pontoon use — first event_pontoon entry tied to a maritime-AND-aerospace combined exhibition (cf. F1/Cannes/MIPIM/Ocean Race entries which are pure-water marquee events). LIMA-2027 candidate for Quanta-LR exhibition slot; defense OUT OF SCOPE per malaysia.md but LIMA-as-venue is permissible Navier-Mobility surface.
  - **No Aman / Six Senses / Banyan Tree / COMO / Soneva on Langkawi** — single placeholder `lgk-the-ritz-carlton-reserve-pipeline` with `status: planned` flags pipeline gap. Sampriti channel cross-check candidate. Confirms hospitality-anchor archetypes have a structural ceiling: when 5+ ultra-premium global chains are absent, narrative leverage concentrates on the present chains (YTL + Marriott + Berjaya).

learnings:

Closest analog Bali (65) extended.
  - **multi-gateway-archipelago sub-archetype** identified (distinct from single-gateway-archipelago Maldives + multi-cluster-arc Bali): 5 distinct mainland-gateway terminals (Batangas Pier, Cebu Port Pier 1-4, Caticlan Jetty, Puerto Princesa Port, Dapa Port) each anchor a different sub-cluster. No single-gateway concentration. **Suggest as 7th archetype** in volume/P0-share benchmarks table — distinct counterparty-stack implications: 5 separate PPA/TIEZA/CPA HOLD-gated negotiations rather than one Velana-AAM unlock.
  - counterparty-stack: **N-deep with regulator-decentralization overlay** — most decentralized of 11 cities to date. PPA national + Cebu Port Authority carve-out + TIEZA (Boracay/El Nido/Siargao TEZ) + MARINA + PCG + DOTr + DOT + DENR + Boracay IARMG + LGUs (Aklan/Palawan/Cebu/Bohol/Surigao). Discovery (Shores Boracay + Club Paradise Coron). Bluewater (Mactan + Panglao + Sumilon).
  - **chain-wedge density unusually high**: Henann 5-property + El Nido Resorts/Ayala 4-property + Shangri-La 3-property + Accor/Mövenpick 2-property + Discovery 2-property + Crimson/Filinvest 2-property + Bluewater 2-property. **7 distinct multi-property domestic-PH chain MOU surfaces** — first multi-cluster city where domestic-chain wedge density rivals global-chain (Marriott/IHG/Accor/Aman) coverage. Promote: **domestic-chain-density signal** as a multi-cluster archetype refinement.
  - solar-flagship sub-class (Maldives-promoted) applied 3x: Pangulasian (El Nido Resorts solar-heavy), Amanpulo (solar-heavy), Nay Palad (solar-flagship candidate). Third appearance outside Maldives (after Nikoi/Cempedak Bintan) — confirms the class propagates across SEA ultra-luxe. **Should formally be tracked as a global wedge filter.**
  - **gateway-concentration POI ABSENCE wedge** applied to Bohol-Panglao International Airport marine-link gap — fourth instance after Langkawi LGK, Jakarta CGK→Pulau Pantara, Lombok future. learnings:
  - typology: **Hospitality-anchor + sovereign-asset-holder hybrid + cross-border-pointer overlay** — 46 POIs (12 P0 / 13 P1 / 14 P2 / 7 hide). hotel_jetty share 26% — well below 40% hospitality-density flag despite Lusail/Pearl/West-Bay concentration; counterparty-stack concentration (Katara Hospitality QIA + UDC + Qatari Diar + Anantara) compresses leverage onto fewer entities than the share suggests. Doha-canonical hybrid archetype.
  - **Qetaifan Island marina = single most time-sensitive pedestal-spec window in Doha** — authored with `status:under_construction` carrying the time-decay signal cleanly. Analog to BMA Bangkok 4-pier 2026 window and Lusail Marina FIPA-uncommitted pedestals. Both Qetaifan + Lusail Marina are class-1 open-spec design-in windows (rare for a mature Gulf city to have 2 such windows simultaneously).
  - **hide-royal-overlook applied to Emiri Diwan / West Bay stretch** (per Bangkok-promoted sub-class) — second positive instance after Klai Kangwon; sister to AUH Corniche / Jeddah Corniche / Dubai Creek / Brunei Istana Nurul Iman pattern. Documents Diwan-overlook scheduling constraint structurally. Critically: file is **gated on UNKNOWN royal-channel convener identity** — the hide-royal-overlook entry carries the file's most important open-question signal.
  - **hide-water-body negative-confirmation entry** (per Jakarta-promoted sub-class) — Doha Bay authored as `hide` with explicit "no major river outflow / clean bay" notes. **First negative-confirmation hide-water-body entry in the atlas** (Jakarta/Manila were positive — actual debris corridors).   - **hide-military** (HK-promoted) applied to QENF naval base (distinct from Hamad Port commercial + Halul defense+oil). Three distinct hide entries for what are sometimes conflated — clean per-sub-class authoring discipline.
  - **gateway-concentration POI ABSENCE wedge** (Langkawi-promoted) applied to HIA marine-link gap — 5th instance after Langkawi LGK, Jakarta CGK→Pulau Pantara, Bohol-Panglao, Lombok. Pattern strongly confirmed: HIA with ~50M+ pax + 50% transfer-pax is the largest gateway-absence opportunity in MENA.
