### 2026-05-28 — coastal-poi penang
subagent: navier-coastal-poi.md
task: Curated 41 boarding points across Penang (George Town UNESCO + Batu Ferringhi luxury strip + Tanjung Tokong Straits Quay + Butterworth/Penang Sentral RoRo + Rapid Ferry incumbent-dense + south-island Bayan Lepas/Jerejak + cross-cluster Langkawi/Pangkor Laut/Phuket pointers + cross-border MY↔ID Belawan long-haul candidate #17 + seasonal-complementarity-pair MY-internal first instance).
learnings:
  - **Penang is FIRST MY-side instance of PUBLIC-WATER-MOBILITY INCUMBENT-DENSE counterpoint to MENA/SEA absence-wedge pattern.** Rapid Ferry Butterworth↔George Town = oldest scheduled ferry in MY (1894), continuous operation, ~10–25k pax/day. Channel-partner-not-compete framing per Setouchi/HK/Dubai-RTA discipline. Distinct from Bali/Lombok/Jakarta/Doha/AUH ABSENCE-wedge. Penang Port + Prasarana/Rapid Penang JV (post-2020) = 2-counterparty incumbent-upgrade conversation. Public-water-mobility wedge inverts here — Pioneer II foiling-upgrade narrative for existing 130-year-old route rather than greenfield concession-RFP collapse.
  - **GATEWAY-CONCENTRATION POI ABSENCE confirmed at PEN Bayan Lepas (light-magnitude).** Penang International Airport ~8M pax/yr (2019); ~20 km road to George Town, ~30 km to Batu Ferringhi via Lim-Chong-Eu coastal road. Light-magnitude variant (Bangkok-Airways/USM-Samui-precedent + RAK road-feeder analog) — wedge real but concession-channel private-operator-friendly rather than sovereign-bundled. MAHB/Khazanah single-MOU collapse cross-link to DCFT Desaru TAR&H/Khazanah pattern (sovereign-fund cross-portfolio lever).
  - **E&O Berhad single-family-office 3-asset MOU = highest-leverage Penang-side single-counterparty** after YTL national chain. Straits Quay Marina (class-1 OPEN-spec) + E&O Hotel George Town (UNESCO heritage, class-3 HOLD-gated) + Lone Pine Batu Ferringhi (class-2 open) single-MOU. Penang counterparty-stack is 5-deep optionality-rich: E&O + YTL (Datai/Pangkor-Laut cross-cluster) + Penang Port/MMC (Syed Mokhtar — Swettenham cruise + RoRo ferry + NBCT cargo) + Shangri-La/Kuok (Rasa Sayang + Golden Sands) + MAHB/Khazanah (PEN airport handoff). Closest archetype: SG 4-deep + Phuket east-coast 4-deep. Higher counterparty-density than Antalya (D-Marin Doğuş + Setur Koç 2-chain duopoly).
  - **MY-INTERNAL seasonal-complementarity-pair FIRST INSTANCE authored** — Penang/Langkawi/Pangkor-Laut west-coast (Malacca-Strait Sumatra-sheltered) Apr-Oct prime ↔ Tioman/Redang/Perhentian east-coast (S China Sea Nov-Mar NE-monsoon-shut) prime. **YTL is candidate first MY same-counterparty seasonal-pair consolidation** (Datai Langkawi west + Tanjong Jara Terengganu east + Pangkor Laut west) — single Francis-Yeoh MOU spans both sides of MY peninsular monsoon-pair, paralleling Antalya↔Bodrum D-Marin Doğuş first-global-instance (pass-#12 Antalya entry). 7th SEA-internal complementarity instance globally.
  - **Cross-border catalogue #17 candidate: Penang↔Belawan (Medan, North Sumatra) ~180 nm Quanta-LR long-haul MY↔ID Strait-of-Malacca.** Historic Eka Express Langkawi-Penang-Belawan ferry (defunct ~2012) — REVIVAL as Quanta-LR cross-border wedge-flip. 2-sovereign-counterparty stack: Penang Port (MMC/Syed Mokhtar) + Pelindo (ID SOE). Cross-references jakarta-side BP Batam discipline. Distinct from MY↔SG DCFT/Tanah Merah micro-corridor (#14, 25 nm ICQ-gated) and MY↔TH Telaga/Bundhaya (#3, 30 nm ICQ-gated) — Penang↔Belawan is full-distance routine-cross-border pointer-only per pass-#9 rule 1, not micro-corridor. Highest-priority candidate for next inclusion in `reference/quanta-lr-cross-border-wedge-flips.md`.
  - **Hide-royal-overlook LIGHT-MAGNITUDE / NON-ROYAL-STATE variant** — Penang is one of 4 non-royal MY states (no Sultan; constitutional appointed Governor / Yang di-Pertua Negeri / TYT instead). Seri Mutiara residence at Northam Road = constitutional-tier protocol overlay, not hereditary-Sultan tier. Distinct from Desaru (Sultan-of-Johor + Agong-XVII DUAL highest-tenure overlay) and Langkawi (Sultan-of-Kedah hereditary). New sub-variant beyond pass-#12 republican-executive-residence Malacañang/Okluk-Cumhurbaşkanlığı/Johor-Sultan-Agong — call it "appointed-Governor / non-royal-state constitutional-tier" sub-variant. Propagates to Melaka, Sabah, Sarawak (the other 3 non-royal MY states).
  - **MY 3-tier shipyard-partner topology partial — Penang Strait composite-commercial tier only.** PSCSB (Boustead-linked) + Muhibbah Marine Engineering + Marine Crest Penang = composite-commercial (tier 2). Sovereign-naval (tier 1) is Boustead Naval Shipyard Lumut (Perak, ~95 nm S, RMN affiliate). Cultural-heritage tier 3 ABSENT (parallel to Ha Long N-Vietnam absence — N-Malay-Strait wooden-boat tradition industrialized into PSCSB/Muhibbah pipeline). Distinct from Bira-Bugis ID + Bodrum İçmeler + Aswan-felucca + Sur-sambuk 4-instance cultural-heritage tier. Regional MRO triangulation network: Penang ↔ Phuket Boat Lagoon ↔ Lumut ↔ Damen Song Cam VN ↔ Tsuneishi Cebu PH.
  - **UNESCO George Town heritage core (inscribed 2008, joint with Melaka)** authored as hide-protected-heritage + symbolic-arrival pier-class for Clan Jetties (Chew/Lee/Lim/Tan/Yeoh/Koay) — customary clan-association tenure + GTWHI heritage-stewardship dual-gate. Parallel to Lombok Bali customary + Komodo Ata Modo customary + Sharjah Heart-of-Sharjah heritage. Pioneer II UNESCO arrival-experience candidate via Swettenham cruise tender + Tanjung City Marina + Esplanade E&O — 3-pier UNESCO-buffer-zone arrival options.
  - **Aman chain-gap-as-wedge — 7th instance globally** after Komodo + RA + Phu Quoc + Ha Long + Desaru + Antalya. Closest Aman is Amanpuri Phuket ~250 nm. UNESCO + heritage-luxury archetype-fit makes Penang strong candidate for Aman-pattern Quanta-LR quiet-mode answer.
  - **Hotel_jetty share 8/41 (~20%); ferry_terminal 3 (7%); marina 6 (15%); hide 7 (17%); pointer 4 (10%); shipyard_partner 1; seaplane_base 1; event_pontoon 1.** Archetype mix sits closer to intermediate scheduled-network archetype #3 + UNESCO-heritage overlay than to pure hospitality-anchor #2 — because dense-incumbent Rapid Ferry + UNESCO public-pier layer + cross-cluster pointer density dominate over the 6-hotel Batu Ferringhi cluster.
  - **Pulau Payar Marine Park (DMPM Federal NRES) authored as DNP/NP-zoned anchorage (class-6 charging-spec window per pass-#10)** between Penang and Langkawi (~30 nm N Penang) — mid-arc conservation-staging candidate for Pioneer-edge / Quanta-LR routine Penang↔Langkawi arc. First MY-west-coast DMPM-administered instance in atlas (parallel to Pulau Tioman + Pulau Redang + Pulau Tenggol east-coast).

date: 2026-05-29
subagent: navier-city-stub.md (executed: data-spine validator build, not a city stub)
task: Build snap-to-coast POI validator + audit report + wire into build.sh
learnings:
  - methodology: |
      Distance-to-coastline is the right shape for a quality gate, but the **basemap
      resolution dominates the signal at small thresholds**. Natural Earth 10m
      generalises to ~1 km, so the literal payload thresholds (50/200 m PASS/WARN/FAIL)
      produced 90% FAIL even on POIs that are clearly correctly snapped (Tsim Sha Tsui
      Star Ferry Pier reported 55 m). The validator now ships with a **distribution
      caveat in the report**: long-tail (>10 km) is the actual signal; sub-2 km is
      basemap noise. Recommend treating the thresholds as **distribution bins for
      prioritisation**, not pass/fail gates, until OSM water polygons replace NE 10m.
  - source-discovered: |
      Distinct atlas-quality clusters surfaced by the >10 km long-tail (302 records):
      (1) **Lake Toba shell** — every POI is legitimately inland on a freshwater lake;
          the entire cluster needs `inland_authorized: true` at the city-shell level
          (proposed schema convention: shell-level `water_body_class: inland-lake`
          auto-bypasses coast audit for all children).
      (2) **Cambodia Tonle Sap / Phnom Penh river confluence** — Mekong/Tonle Sap
          riverine POIs; same fix.
      (3) **Cross-region pointer locales** ("Port Sudan" under Jeddah, "Sharm El
          Sheikh" under NEOM, "Antalya↔Cyprus", "Abu Dhabi cross-border" under
          Muscat, "DMZ/NLL islands" under Korea, "Derawan↔Sandakan") — these are
          intentional cross-cluster strategic references, not deployable POIs.
          They need a schema flag (`cross_reference_only: true` or
          `role: pointer-only`) to be skipped by coord-quality checks AND by any
          future routing/range computation.
      (4) **Placeholder locales** ("Coastal Village", "Future Edition / future Faena
          / future flagship brands") under RSG — these are unbuilt brand pipeline
          slots and shouldn't be in the node graph at all OR need a
          `status: planned-unbuilt` flag.
      (5) **Inland-adjacency locales** ("Liwa / inland desert", "Mleiha inland
          archaeology", "Six Senses Southern Dunes mainland", "Desert Rock mainland")
          — these are legitimate cluster context but explicitly mainland; need
          `inland_authorized: true` at the locale level. Common pattern: any locale
          whose name contains "mainland" / "inland" / "desert".
  - schema-gap: |
      Five missing schema flags surfaced by this run, in declining urgency:
        1. `inland_authorized: true` at sub-cluster/locale level (covers desert-
           adjacency, inland-archaeology, mainland-back-of-house);
        2. `water_body_class: inland-lake | river | sea | ocean` at city-shell level
           (auto-derives coast-audit exemption for lake/river clusters);
        3. `cross_reference_only: true` (or `role: pointer-only`) for strategic
           pointers to neighbouring clusters — prevents them being counted as
           operational POIs;
        4. `status: planned-unbuilt | operational | mothballed` for future-brand
           placeholders (RSG Faena, etc.);
        5. Per-POI `city_id` / `parent_city` in nodes.json — currently city must be
           reconstructed from `id` prefix (before `__`) or `anchor_node_id` because
           the spine has no explicit foreign key. Worth making explicit at parse time.
      Recommend folding (1)–(4) into CITY_TEMPLATE addendum and (5) into the
      data-spine parse_city_files.py extractor.
  - efficiency: |
      Failure-rate-by-city is the highest-signal table for re-curation
      prioritisation. Sorted by FAIL count, the top quality-risk shells are:
      Hong Kong (134 POIs, 75%), Malé/Maldives (95, 98%), Dubai (89, 87%),
      Philippines (84, 87%), Komodo (67, 96%), Bangkok (62, 98%), Bali (63, 92%),
      Doha (61, 92%), Lombok (59, 95%), Abu Dhabi (58, 97%). High-volume + high-rate
      = best ROI for re-curation passes. Cities with **0 PASS** (Doha, Abu Dhabi,
      Muscat, RSG, Lombok, Desaru, NEOM, Salalah) suggest **systemic coord-source
      issues** rather than per-POI errors — likely a single bad anchor or override
      is propagating. Recommend a separate per-city anchor-validation pass before
      individual POI re-curation.
  - efficiency: |
      Validator runtime ~22s for 2,244 POIs against 4,133 coastline segments using
      shapely STRtree + local-equirectangular projection. Negligible cost; safe to
      keep as default build step. Coastline cache is ~10 MB and persists in
      manual-coords/coastline-cache.geojson.

### 2026-05-28 — sub-cluster intra-file geocoder (efficiency + schema-gap)

**Context.** Built/extended `app/data-spine/geocode_sub_clusters.py` to resolve POIs parsed from sub-cluster tables that were previously dumped into `fallback-jitter-around-parent` and surfaced as atlas stubs.

**Run result.** Cache: 259 entries, 234 with coords (90 % hit-rate on first pass; 19 nulls). `apply_coords.py` stats: `osm-sub-cluster=181`, `fallback-jitter=19` (down from 200+ pre-pass). 74 hits flagged >50 km from parent anchor for review.

**Findings.**
1. **Efficiency / methodology — viewbox + country-tail country routing.** Suffix-driven `country-for-anchor` map keyed on the terminal token of the anchor id (`*-uae`, `*-oman`, `*-indonesia`, …) covers all 60 city anchors with zero per-city config. Country-shell anchors (japan / korea / taiwan / turkey / malaysia / cambodia / vietnam / brunei-darussalam) need bbox-disabled queries because their POIs span the whole country — special-case set in script.
2. **Source-discovered — 50 km flag is too aggressive for archipelago / cross-border sub-clusters.** Of 74 far-flagged hits, the long tail is largely correct: "Aden, Yemen" from Salalah (870 km), "Jakarta direct" from LBJ (1462 km), Socotra from Salalah (865 km), Wakatobi-adjacency from Banda (912 km). These are *legitimate* cross-border or remote-island pointers, not OSM errors. **Real** false positives clustered around name-collision in Indonesia: "Karimun Besar" geocoded to Karimun in Riau Islands instead of Karimunjawa (Central Java). Recommend per-archetype thresholds: 30 km for `core-tourism-protocol-city`, 200 km for `archipelago-cluster`, disable for cross-border-corridor rows. Schema-gap: sub-cluster rows could carry an explicit `expected_radius_km` hint.
3. **Schema-gap — `_OUT OF SCOPE — pitch trap_` rows are still being emitted as POIs.** `clean_query` filters them at geocode time, but `parse_city_files.py` still creates the node (4 instances: japan / korea / taiwan / turkey country shells). Parent action: filter at parser level, or carry a `meta_row: true` flag so atlas can hide them.
4. **Methodology — provenance.** `apply_coords.py` tags these `coords_source = "osm-nominatim-sub-cluster"` (not the payload's `sub_cluster_geocoded`). Distinct from `osm-nominatim` (priority-story-city pass) so downstream consumers can weight confidence. Keeping the two caches separate (`osm-cache.json` vs `sub-cluster-osm-cache.json`) matters because the sub-cluster pass uses parent-anchor viewboxes — re-running the priority pass would otherwise widen its viewbox model.
5. **Recurring failure pattern in nulls.** Of the 19 no-results: 4 are `_OUT OF SCOPE — pitch trap_` rows (see #3); rest are descriptive composites OSM doesn't know ("Phinisi-companion vessel circuit", "TR↔GR Aegean cross-border catalogue", "Future Edition / future Faena", "Eastern Anatolia inland waters (Van Gölü etc.)"). These are inherently un-geocodable thematic rows — they belong in `poi-overrides.json` with a `meta_row` flag rather than a coord. Schema proposal: add a `non-geocodable thematic row` provenance bucket so apply_coords stops jittering them.

**Proposed schema/reference updates** (for next promotion pass):
- CITY_TEMPLATE addendum: sub-cluster table rows that are thematic/composite (not real places) carry a `thematic: true` column or a leading `🏷️` marker so the parser skips POI emission.
- `parse_city_files.py`: filter `_OUT OF SCOPE — pitch trap_` rows at source.
- `geocode_sub_clusters.py`: optional `expected_radius_km` per archetype; the current uniform 50 km threshold over-flags archipelago and cross-border rows.

## coastal-poi infrastructure — POI Precision Conveyor Belt v1 (2026-05-28, pass-#14)

Built `/agent/home/navier/app/poi-precision/` — 7-stage pre-write validator now MANDATORY before any city's `boarding-points/{slug}.json` is considered done. Pipeline rewrites the JSON in place adding a per-POI `precision` field + city-level `precision_pipeline` block + sidecar `_audit/{slug}.audit.json` + per-city `rejects/{slug}-rejects.json`. Subagent prompt updated.

**Stages** (per-stage CLI wrapper + shared `conveyor.py` lib): (1) Overpass strict marine — `leisure=marina | harbour=* | man_made=pier|quay|breakwater|wharf | amenity=ferry_terminal | waterway=dock`, 200m match. (2) Snap-to-coast — per-city OSM `natural=water | natural=coastline | waterway=riverbank` (cached), with NE 10m fallback; ≤50m `on_coast`, 50–300m `snapped_to_coast`, >300m `rejected_inland`. Inside water-polygon → distance 0. (3) OpenSeaMap seamark:* (harbour/mooring/pier/anchorage/berth). (4) Mapbox Search Box — skipped (no key in `.secrets/`). (5) Google Places validator — skipped. (6) Hotel-jetty scrape — best-effort, capped at 6 fetches/city. (7) Pre-write gate — pass if any of marine/seamark/mapbox match, OR `manual_override`, OR coast-snap medium-confidence.

**v1 reprocess result (37 cities, 1672 eligible POIs)**: pass 476 (28.5%) / reject 1196 (71.5%). Histogram: `needs_escalation` 1520, `rejected_inland` 1134, `snapped_to_coast` 349, `on_coast` 189, `osm_marine_match` 152, `openseamap_match` 47. Dubai 64% pass (best-mapped). The 71% reject rate is the headline finding: **a majority of authored POIs do not have an OSM marine feature within 200m AND are >300m from the nearest water polygon**. Two root causes:

- (a) **Coord drift**: many POIs were placed off-water (administration entrance / parking / hotel reception centroid) rather than the actual jetty/marina point. These need either re-snap (the audit gives `_audit.stage1.snap_lng/lat` or `_audit.stage2.snap_lng/lat` candidates) or `manual_override: true`.
- (b) **OSM marine-tag sparsity in fast-developing coasts** (Sharjah/Doha/Manama new lagoons, NEOM/RSG greenfield, Maldives outer-atolls, Phu Quoc, Komodo/Flores village piers, Raja Ampat) — legitimate marine POIs without OSM mapping. Use `manual_override` with `override_reason` in notes.

**Operational rule going forward**: when re-authoring or extending any city file, run the conveyor, triage the rejects list (it's per-city), and either snap or override. Do not ship a file with unresolved rejects unless documented.

**Caveats**: Overpass per-query cap (`out center tags 800`) loses tail in dense-marina cities (Dubai/Bangkok/Singapore — observed). Bumping to `5000` is a v2 candidate. Sandbox sessions cycle ~20 min so end-to-end batch needs `nohup` + caching (cache directory survives across sessions, so reruns are fast).

**Suggested-update-to-conventions**: add a `precision: osm_marine_match | openseamap_match | mapbox_match | on_coast | snapped_to_coast | manual_override | rejected` enum to the boarding-point schema authoring convention. Atlas-side rendering should style `osm_marine_match` and `on_coast` as full-strength glyphs and dim/cross-hatch anything `manual_override` or unaudited.

date: 2026-05-29
subagent: navier-poi-conveyor.md
task: v2 conveyor build + full 38-city backfill
learnings:
  - methodology: v2 conveyor at atlas-external/conveyor/ wraps existing v1 lib (app/poi-precision/conveyor.py) — no code duplication, only adds confidence/source_chain/validation_log layer + diff reports + scoreboard.
  - source-discovered: Mapbox Search Box category filter [marina/harbor/ferry/pier] is too strict — 0% match rate on Dubai's 69 POIs; real marinas often classified as `business`. Fix one-line in stage4.
  - source-discovered: Google Places Text Search returns parent-POI centroid not jetty point — 100% disagreement on Dubai. Switch to Nearby Search or raise threshold to 250m.
  - source-discovered: OSM marine-tag coverage is only ~8% globally across our 2044 POIs; greenfield coasts (NEOM, RSG, Phu Quoc, Komodo village piers, Raja Ampat outer) are systematically under-mapped — legitimate manual_override territory, not pipeline failure.
  - schema-gap: Add `confidence_override: true` + `override_reason` to boarding-point authoring convention; many off-OSM-coast POIs are correct-but-unverifiable.
  - efficiency: Two-stage backfill — full cached pipeline first (3s/city), API escalation only on POIs that need it — keeps the loop cheap.

date: 2026-05-28
subagent: navier-atlas-v10-fixes.md
task: Applied 8 UX/data fixes to external atlas (panel-collapse, label visibility, KL/country-shell hide, country-prefix strip, glyph legend, land-crossing Quanta routes, terminology unification, route hover tooltip) + deploy v10.
learnings:
  - methodology: The build.py template embeds CSS+JS+HTML in a single triple-quoted block; for compound v10-class fix bundles, edit build.py only (never edit the rebuilt index.html in place). 8 distinct fixes shipped in a single rebuild without index.html touch.
  - source-discovered: Country-shell anchors (`malaysia`, `vietnam`, `japan`, `korea`, `taiwan`, `turkey`, `cambodia`) all use inland-capital coords (KL, HCMC, Tokyo, Seoul, Taipei, Istanbul, Phnom Penh) that mis-anchor coastal-network rendering. Pattern: 7-of-7 country-shells use national-capital coord regardless of whether the capital is coastal. Mitigation: HIDE_ON_MAP set + harbour-overrides → coastal-port coords (Port Klang, Ha Long, Tokyo Bay anchorage, Busan, Keelung, Bosphorus, Sihanoukville). Route resolution preserved via origin_coords(node) harbour-overrides lookup; node remains in node_by_id for backlinks.
  - source-discovered: Bodrum (`turkey-bodrum`) and Setouchi (`japan-setouchi`) sub-cluster nodes have `coords: None` upstream — they don't render at any zoom level because the build's `coords_of` returns None. Both were named in v10 audit screenshot list. Data gap, not v10 regression. Owner: data-spine sub-cluster geocoder retrofit pass.
  - source-discovered: No Langkawi↔Koh-Samui edge exists in current edges.json (Fix 6 land-crossing concern was already pruned upstream). Phuket↔Penang chokepoint rule added defensively as new auto_waypoints rule (Malacca-strait routing 98.4→99.4→100.0). Hard land-intersection validator deferred (parent-owned, requires basemap polygon overlay).
  - schema-gap: `hide_on_map: true` and `is_country_shell: true` are now recognized at atlas build-time but not authored upstream in nodes.json. Either parse_city_files.py should emit these flags on country-shell nodes, or the HIDE_ON_MAP set in build.py is the canonical source — currently the latter. Propose: country-shell flag at parse time.
  - efficiency: MapLibre `text-allow-overlap: true` + `text-ignore-placement: true` + `symbol-sort-key` per-tier is the canonical fix for "market label suppressed by adjacent POI glyph" pattern. Used tier_sort_key {city:1, locale:3, poi:5} so lowest-key (markets) paint first. Applied to city-labels layer only — Singapore now visible at z11 alongside MPA POI ferry glyph.
  - efficiency: Panel-collapse-full-width fix requires THREE CSS changes, not one: `body.panel-hidden #map { right:0 }` + `body.panel-hidden #panel { width:0 }` + `map.resize()` post-transition. The transform:translateX(100%) alone is insufficient because `#map { right:420px }` was hard-coded; panel still reserved column width.
suggested-update:
  target-file: /agent/home/navier/app/data-spine/parse_city_files.py
  change: Emit `is_country_shell: true` + `hide_on_map: true` flags on top-level country-shell nodes (id matches /^(malaysia|vietnam|japan|korea|south-korea|taiwan|turkey|cambodia|indonesia|philippines|thailand)$/). Promotes HIDE_ON_MAP from build.py-local set to schema-level flag.

date: 2026-05-28
subagent: navier-coastal-poi.md (sub-cluster geocoder build mode)
task: Added resolve_subclusters.py pipeline stage, wired into build.sh. Walks every city .md for tables under headers Sub-clusters / Locales / Areas / Boarding clusters, geocodes via OSM Nominatim with Google Places fallback (key at /agent/home/navier/.secrets/google-places.key). Persists into manual-coords/sub-cluster-osm-cache.json; apply_coords.py picks up coords. Emits new POI nodes for table rows the v0.1 parser misses.
learnings:
  - methodology: Existing geocode_sub_clusters.py + extract_locales.py overlap with the requested scope. Built resolve_subclusters.py as a layered AUGMENT (Google fallback for OSM misses + wider header vocabulary), not a replacement — mutates the same sub-cluster-osm-cache.json so apply_coords.py change-set is zero. Sub-cluster cache went from 259→484 entries, with-coords 234→434 (+200 newly-resolved POIs); 52 of those net-new hits came from Google Places fallback (OSM had no answer).
  - source-discovered: Of 60 city .md files, only `## Sub-clusters` header appears (41 instances). No `## Locales`, `## Areas`, or `## Boarding clusters` headers exist anywhere in the corpus despite the payload listing them. Net result: only 3 net-new POI nodes were emitted by my walker that weren't already extracted by parse_city_files.py (all Sub-cluster table rows the v0.1 parser dedup-collided on). Future-proofs against new authoring vocabularies but doesn't shift today's count materially.
  - source-discovered: OSM Nominatim public endpoint is currently aggressively rate-limiting our Tasklet egress IP — HTTP 429 on every request even at the documented 1.1s sleep. Toggled SKIP_OSM=True so the resolver goes straight to Google Places (which works reliably). Re-evaluate when the 429 block lifts; until then OSM-only geocoders (geocode_sub_clusters.py, geocode_osm.py, geocode_locales.py) are de-facto no-ops on new candidates.
  - source-discovered: **Stub count did NOT drop (86 unresolved → 86 unresolved in resolve_cross_file_edges.py audit).** Headline stubs are dominated by route-prose endpoints in `## Waterways & Routes` (e.g. `Hinode ↔ Asakusa`, `Sanur ↔ Amed`, `Takamatsu ↔ Naoshima`, `Banda Neira ↔ Run`), which are pier/locale names mentioned in route bullets — NOT sub-cluster table rows. entry: waterways-prose pier extractor (data-spine stage 1d)
date: 2026-05-28
findings:
  - New stage `/agent/home/navier/app/data-spine/extract_waterways_piers.py` walks every city .md `## Waterways & Routes` section, splits route prose on ↔ / → / " to ", further subsplits chunks on " / " + " + ", drops commentary after `:` `;` ` — ` ` – `, and clean-trims markdown emphasis + trailing distance/nm noise + trailing modifier words (`direct`, `coastal`, `intra-cluster shuttle`, …).
  - Hard discipline: process only bullet/pipe-table rows; reject prose lines with backtick spans (path/code refs like `/agent/home/navier/...`) — without this, narrative cross-references explode into path-token noise ("agent", "home", "navier").
  - Pipeline-side: 857 raw route-endpoint extractions across 60 city files → 391 unique unresolved → 381 geocoded via Google Places Text Search (Google fallback; OSM Nominatim still 429-locked per resolve_subclusters.py pattern). Cache at `manual-coords/waterways-pier-cache.json`; geocoded endpoints also appended to nodes.json as `_ww__{slug}` POIs so `resolve_cross_file_edges.py` resolves them by exact-name match.
  - Stub delta: **86 → 24** (target <40). Remaining 24 are legitimate edge cases — cross-border flag-and-exclude rows (Ishigaki↔Hualien JP↔TW, Kinmen↔Xiamen, Matsu↔Fuzhou, Hokkaido↔Sakhalin), region-meta references (Java↔Sumatra, Jeollanam↔Gyeongsangnam, MY↔TH), and compound-qualifier endpoints (`Niseko-adjacency Higashidate/Iwanai`).
  - Wedge surface for next iteration: existing parser folds compound POIs like "Setouchi (Naoshima / Teshima / Inujima / …)" into a single node whose poi_tokens index drops everything inside parens — so individual island tokens (Naoshima, Teshima) only resolve via the new `_ww__` POIs. Worth promoting the parens-aware sub-token indexing into parse_city_files.py directly.

entry: land-intersection route validator (data-spine stage 8)
date: 2026-05-28
subagent: navier-coastal-poi.md (pipeline payload)
findings:
  - New `/agent/home/navier/app/data-spine/validate_route_land_crossings.py`: samples 50 great-circle pts/edge, cumulative land-crossing >2km → auto-inject `auto_waypoints.py` deflection (known chokepoint) else flag to `atlas-external/route-review-queue.json`. Wired into build.sh as non-blocking stage 8/8 (runs last so its edge annotations aren't overwritten). Uses `uv run --with global-land-mask`.
  - DATA-SOURCE REALITY: conveyor `coastline-cache/*.geojson` are OSM `natural=coastline` **LineStrings** (per-country, osm_id-tagged), NOT closed land polygons → point-in-polygon containment unavailable from them. Fell back (as spec allows) to Natural-Earth/GSHHG global land mask (`global_land_mask`, ~1km, whole-globe). Per-country LineStrings only span ~19 countries; mask covers all edges. Future: polygonise coastline LineStrings for a higher-res refinement.
  - RUN-ONCE RESULT (932 edges evaluated, 437 skipped no-coords, 7 flag_and_exclude): 791 cross >2km land pre-fix. Classified: 643 `inland-or-coarse-endpoint` (city anchors are inland centroids — pre-existing, NOT routing), 115 `coord-suspect` (gc >> curated distance_nm; dominated by `_ww__` waterways-prose mis-geocodes e.g. `_ww__west-bay`→Singapore 103.86,1.27), 28 genuinely-actionable `needs-waypoint-rule`. Auto-waypoint: 5 fully resolved (<=2km), 100 partial (waypoints injected, residual >2km mostly due to inland endpoints). Waypoint rules DO help massively where coords are clean (Jeddah→AMAALA 295→33km, Langkawi→SG 617→33km, RSG→Shura 747→77km).
  - Classification (review_reason + endpoint_on_land + gc-vs-declared ratio) is what makes the 786-edge queue actionable — without it the queue is 96% pre-existing-data-noise. Injection skipped for coord-suspect edges (can't deflect broken coords).
  - ACTIONABLE 28 new auto_waypoints rule candidates surfaced: NEOM↔Aqaba/Eilat Gulf-of-Aqaba axis (NB: NEOM↔Eilat is flag-and-exclude security per conventions — should be excluded not routed), Wakatobi↔Manado, Manado↔Bunaken.
suggested-update:
  - target-file: app/data-spine (parent-owned): the 115 coord-suspect + 643 inland-anchor crossings are the real signal — `_ww__` waterways-prose geocoder needs disambiguation (West Bay→Singapore) and country-shell/city anchors need harbour-override coastal coords (per `is_country_shell`/HIDE_ON_MAP convention). Land-xing validator now surfaces both as structured review_reason buckets.

### coastal-poi BATCH (Conveyor v2.1 sweep, 2026-05-28) — 8 new + 7 revalidated cities

**Coord retrofit**: added `turkey-bodrum` [27.4305,37.0344] (Bodrum Marina) and `japan-setouchi` [133.7706,34.3853] (Onomichi) to data-spine/manual-coords/city-anchors.json — both were `coords: None` blocking render (v10 audit).

**Conveyor tooling note (IMPORTANT for future runs)**: the installed conveyor (`run_conveyor.py`→`conveyor.py`) names reject sidecars by **`city_id`**, NOT filename slug, and writes `_audit/{city_id}.audit.json`. Pre-existing files where `city_id != filename` (e.g. da-nang-hoi-an.json has city_id `da-nang-hoi-an-vietnam`) silently produced no triageable reject path under a filename assumption. Built `process_city.sh` + `triage_overrides.py` + `force_override_rejects.py` (in app/poi-precision/) that resolve rejects by city_id. Reject file is NOT overwritten on a 0-reject pass (stale files linger) — delete before trusting `rejects=` counts.

**Snap-to-coast failure mode**: ~50-65% of POIs reject on pass 1 as `rejected_inland`, but almost all are OSM-water-cache coverage gaps, not coord errors. Two distinct sub-cases: (a) coords 200-3000m inland (municipal piers / small harbours placed at road/plaza centroid) → manual_override w/ undermapped-coast reason; (b) remote outer islands (Okinawa Yaeyama/Kerama, Penghu outer islets, Marado, Kapalai sandbank) snapping 8-38 km because per-city water cache doesn't extend to far-flung islands → force-override (OSM coverage gap, real marine). True coord errors (placed several km inland) were rare (~1-2 per city) and fixed seaward to harbour basin.

**coastal-poi cesme-izmir**: 33 pts. D-Marin Med↔Aegean chain extends (Çeşme Marina/IC Çeşme); İzmir İzdeniz municipal ferry network (Konak/Karşıyaka/Bostanlı/Üçkuyular/Göztepe) = dense scheduled-incumbent. Aegean Meltemi afternoon-gale overlay. Çeşme↔Chios (TR↔GR) cross-border. Foça/Aliağa industrial split-out.
**coastal-poi izu-shimoda**: 31 pts. Tokyo-Bay-mouth weekend-cruiser arc (Atami/Itō/Shimoda/Dōgashima); Izu-Shichitō island ferries (Ōshima/Niijima); Mt-Fuji-view leg. Sagami-Bay typhoon overlay. Imperial-villa Hayama is N of scope (negative-confirmation per Setouchi pattern).
**coastal-poi hokkaido-niseko**: 28 pts. Otaru canal + Niseko-feeder (Yoichi/Shakotan/Bikuni) + Hakodate + Tōya/Noboribetsu. Heavy hide load (10 skipped): Sea-of-Japan + winter-monsoon + drift-ice (Okhotsk) overlays. Niseko is inland ski-feeder (road-feeder to Otaru/Iwanai).
**coastal-poi yeosu-tongyeong**: 31 pts. Hallyeohaesang = KR's first marine NP (born-protected, Setouchi-analog calm inner-sea = quiet-mode lever). Yeosu Expo-legacy marina class-1 design-in. Asymmetric water-mobility (tourist night-cruise dense, commuter absent). Geomundo = historic Port Hamilton.
**coastal-poi penghu**: 29 pts. Taiwan-Strait DUAL overlay (winter NE-monsoon 7-10 Beaufort + summer typhoon) BUT Penghu Inland Sea structurally calm (intra-archipelago Pioneer II viable). Budai↔Magong shortest mainland crossing (~25nm) + Kaohsiung↔Penghu intra-jurisdiction line-haul. Cross-strait HARD HOLD. UNESCO Biosphere candidacy = future born-protected lever.
**coastal-poi koh-rong-sihanoukville**: 29 pts. Island-as-property triple (Song Saa/Bensley, Six Senses Krabey, Alila Koh Russey) — all solar-microgrid Pioneer II pedestal targets. Sihanoukville↔Phú Quốc cross-border #7 (VN↔KH). Security HARD HOLDs: Ream Naval Base (PRC-linked) + Dara Sakor/Botum Sakor (sanctioned). Taj Cambodia + Sampriti warm-network (do NOT pre-empt HQ).
**coastal-poi sabah-kk**: 37 pts. TAR Park 5-island intra-cluster minutes from KK (Pioneer II hub) + Sutera Harbour class-1 (Sime Darby). Semporna/Sipadan = sharpest MY quiet-mode dive lever, operates WITHIN ESSZONE (operational-envelope-clarification: dive-corridor escort-OK, Sulu-facing waters HARD HOLD). West Sabah BELOW typhoon belt (negative-confirmation calm-coast). YTL Gaya seasonal-pair lever. Istana Negeri TYT appointed-Governor (non-royal-state overlook sub-variant). KK↔Labuan↔BSB Brunei corridor.

**suggested-update (to city files)**: Çeşme Marina + IC Çeşme should be explicit in turkey-cesme-izmir.md (D-Marin Med-Aegean chain MOU lever); Sabah ESSZONE operational-envelope-clarification + West-Sabah-below-typhoon-belt advantage belong in malaysia-sabah-kk.md Holds; Penghu-Inland-Sea-calm vs Strait-DUAL-overlay contrast belongs in taiwan-penghu.md.

**Revalidated (7 pre-existing, ran through Conveyor v2.1, 0 rejects each)**: antalya 49, penang 46, da-nang-hoi-an 61, busan-geoje 57, okinawa-yaeyama 86, jeju 50, kaohsiung-taiwan 40. okinawa needed 18 force-overrides (Yaeyama/Kerama outer islands beyond water cache); busan 5, jeju 1, da-nang 1.

## 2026-05-29 — Confidentiality partition architecture (parent agent)
- The atlas leaked confidential content publicly (principals, Sheikha gate, Series B,
  deal terms). Root cause: build embedded raw spine fields + story deal_shape/next_steps,
  and the boarding-point injection path (separate from node partition) carried
  bp_relevance/notes with strategy + principal names.
- SOLUTION (now standard): two-partition model.
  * `atlas-external/partition/partition_spec.py` — external_safe whitelists per type +
    exclusion blocklist (case-insensitive + case-sensitive posture codes).
  * `partition_filter.py` — projects spine output → output-external/ (whitelist only) +
    output-internal/. Fail closed. Humans layer fully internal. Suppresses internal-marker
    nodes (out-of-scope/pitch-trap) + internal-classification edges (flag-and-exclude).
    Story free-text neutralized via NEUTRAL_SUBS then hard-redacted.
  * `enrich_external.py` — adds partner_view, vessel_specs, confidence/status (derived,
    external_safe only).
  * `externalization_check.py` — HARD GATE; scans built HTML; blocks deploy on any hit.
  * `build_safe.sh` — partition → enrich → build → gate. NEVER deploy without it passing.
- build.py repointed to output-external/; POI injection sanitized; dead internal-render
  JS removed; supplemental-nodes/edges/stories merge points added for coverage growth.
- LESSON: any NEW injection path into the atlas (POIs, supplemental files) must be
  external_safe by construction AND is caught by the gate. Always run build_safe.sh.
- LESSON: keep a "CONFIDENTIAL — do not distribute" header even on the external build;
  GTM geography is competitively sensitive even without deal terms.

## 2026-05-29 — SECURITY INCIDENT: three live leak vectors found + closed (parent agent)
Severity: HIGH. Found while starting queued story work. Three independent vectors:

1. **Story residual-meaning leaks.** Source story files ({slug}.json) were authored with
   internal content; the partition sanitizer redacted *names* (Sampriti/Sheikha/Jaideep)
   but left leaky *structure* the gate didn't catch: "John Pagano / RSG CEO" (third-party
   exec, never in blocklist), "use case score" (sanitizer turned "archetype score"→"use
   case score", evading the archetype-only pattern), "Gate / hold pending intro",
   "Royal channel / convener / confirm with", "Jaideep channel / founder-level relationship".
   FIX: hardened EXCLUSION_RE (pagano, (use-case|archetype) scores, convener, royal channel,
   counterparty role, gated behind, hold pending, named/anchor contact, self-finances, etc.);
   rewrote all 6 source stories' exec_summary+headline_metrics+leaky narrative blocks to
   clean partner-frame copy; stashed originals under non-whitelisted `internal_strategy`
   (routes to internal partition).
2. **WHOLE-DIRECTORY DEPLOY (worst).** Vercel serves EVERY file in the deploy root. Deploying
   from atlas-external/ exposed humans.json (629 contacts!), output-internal/, raw source
   story files, nodes/orgs/edges.json, route-review-queue.json — ALL at public HTTP 200.
   The externalization gate only scanned index.html, so it passed while siblings leaked.
   FIX: deploy ONLY from isolated ../\_dist containing index.html + vercel.json (+ copied
   .vercel/project.json so it targets the navier-atlas project, not a new one). build_safe.sh
   now: partition→enrich→build→gate→assemble dist→DEPLOY-SURFACE GUARD (asserts root has only
   those 2 files)→gate dist copy→deploy. Added .vercelignore (whitelist) as defense-in-depth.
3. **ORPHAN PRE-PARTITION DEPLOYMENT.** An old `atlas-v7` Vercel project was still LIVE and
   public, leaking everything (Sampriti×23, Series B×13, Sheikha×10, Pagano, finder's fee,
   humans.json @200). FIX: `vercel remove atlas-v7`. Also removed stray `_dist` project
   accidentally created by first deploy from the new folder.

STANDING RULES (added):
- NEVER deploy from atlas-external/ (or any dir containing internal files). Only from \_dist.
- The content gate is necessary but NOT sufficient — it scans one file. The deploy-surface
  guard (root = exactly index.html+vercel.json) is the other half. Both must pass.
- After any deploy, curl-verify: (a) index.html has 0 leak tokens, (b) humans.json/nodes.json/
  source story files return 404.
- Periodically run `vercel projects ls` and curl each *.vercel.app for principal/funding
  tokens — orphan deployments are a leak surface. (atlas-v7 sat exposed for ~22h.)
- Deploying from a tiny isolated folder is also FAST (5-8s) vs slow FUSE dir scans (timeouts).

## LEARNING 2026-05-30 (intra-cluster sea-route generation) — parent agent
- Split (country-shell) nodes have ZERO curated edges; derive a hub-radial-spoke network from
  the node's own boarding-point `linked_locale` groupings (anchor = `city_anchor`).
- The hand-authored `auto_waypoints.py` is a region-PAIR rule table — returns None for nearly
  all intra-cluster hops, so straight lines cross land and get dropped (whole clusters → 0).
  FIX: built a GENERAL grid A* sea-router (`sea_router.py`) using `global_land_mask`; one
  `SeaGrid` per cluster (bbox of anchor+reps), `target_cells≈520`, res floor 0.005°, LOS-simplify
  at 0.2nm sampling. Threads bays/channels generically — no per-region rules.
- `global_land_mask` SILENTLY no-ops without numpy (try/except import) → land validation is
  skipped and `dropped_land=0` is the tell. ALWAYS build with `uv run --with global_land_mask
  --with numpy`. (build_safe.sh calls bare `python3 build.py` — does NOT have these; build the
  intra-cluster step manually with uv until build_safe.sh is updated.)
- Arc interpolation MUST be LINEAR in lng/lat, NOT spherical great_circle: a great-circle bows
  off the straight line the router validated and drifts onto coast near land. Switching to linear
  + a final `_arc_mid_land_nm > 2.0nm` guard (excluding a 1.5nm endpoint buffer, since jetties
  read as land at the ~1km mask res) drove worst-case mid-route land 8.4nm → 1.78nm, 0 over 2nm.
- Tight archipelagos (Penghu) have sub-km channels the 1km mask can't resolve → A* finds NO path
  and returns None. Don't drop on None; FALL BACK to straight line and let the mid-route land
  guard arbitrate — short inter-island hops pass, real land-crossers don't. Single arbiter > two.
- Force-INCLUDE the split-node set in intra-cluster targeting even if a single national-hub
  curated spoke already touches them: one hub spoke is not an island-hopping network, and some
  (penghu) only had a dangling phantom-endpoint edge. Result: 238 spokes across all 19 splits.

## LEARNING 2026-05-30 (SECURITY INCIDENT 5 — plural-escaping exclusion term) — parent agent
- "Quanta-LR **wedges**" shipped to the LIVE site though the gate passed. Root cause: both the
  gate (`\bwedge\b`) and the sanitizer sub (`\bwedge\b`→"entry point") used a trailing word
  boundary, so the PLURAL "wedges" matched neither. A substring `curl | grep -io wedge` caught it
  post-deploy; the word-boundary gate did not.
- FIX: `\bwedges?\b` in partition_spec EXCLUSION_RE + a "wedges"→"entry points" sub ahead of the
  "wedge"→"entry point" sub. Regenerated stories via partition_filter, rebuilt, re-gated, redeployed.
- STANDING RULE: every exclusion/sanitizer pattern for a noun must handle the plural (`s?`/`(s|es)`).
  The post-deploy verification MUST use substring grep (not just the word-boundary gate) — the two
  catch different classes; substring grep is the backstop that found this. Gate ≠ substring sweep.

## LEARNING 2026-05-30 (Claude presentation-layer merge — land-routing fix + front-end swap) — parent agent
- MERGE PROTOCOL that worked: Claude handed back a git-apply patch + drop-in build-template.html against
  a REFERENCE build.py. Mine had drifted (BP_CITY_MAP +9 entries). `git apply --check` tolerated the
  7-line offset and all hunks landed clean — so a precise unified diff survives moderate drift; verify
  with --check before applying, keep _backup-pre-merge copies, security-scan the inbound template FIRST.
- TEMPLATE SWAP without inlining pain: split build.py on `HTML = r"""` … closing `"""` (before `out = (HTML`),
  reassemble head + new template + tail. Pre-check the template has no `"""` and no trailing backslash
  (would break the raw string). The `__N_CITY__`-style stat placeholders became harmless no-ops (new
  front-end computes header stats at runtime) — absent-substring .replace() is safe.
- LAND-ROUTING PERF: routing EVERY edge through SeaGrid A* (the patch as written) timed out at 300s.
  Fix = cheap-first short-circuit: validate hand-waypoints, then the straight line, and ONLY build a
  grid + run A* for edges that actually cross land (most open-ocean long-haul is already clean). Add a
  span cap (>7°) to DROP doomed cross-landmass hops rather than paying for a full-grid A* that can only fail.
- THRESHOLD UNIT BUG (generalizable): a "land guard" that compares `_arc_mid_land_nm(arc) > 2.0` is 2.0
  NAUTICAL MILES (~3.7km); a gate measuring 2.0 KM will reject the gap between them. Always reconcile NM
  vs KM across every guard + gate; convert (`*1.852`) and add a small margin (drop at 1.9km vs 2.0km gate).
  Result: 163/467 (35%) land-crossers → 0/373.
- GATE FALSE-POSITIVE pattern: `\bP2\b` (internal posture code) collided with Claude's JS layer comment
  "QLR, P2" (P2 = Pioneer II). Case-sensitive gate → only uppercase hit. Fix the COMMENT (→"PioII") rather
  than weakening the gate; fix it in BOTH the template (source of truth) and the built index.html so you
  can ship without a 254s rebuild while keeping future builds clean.
- build_safe.sh hardened: build line now `uv run --with global_land_mask --with numpy` (plain python3
  silently no-ops land validation) and a LAND-CROSSING GATE step added (qa_land_crossing.py). 7 steps now.

## LEARNING 2026-05-30 (route network rebuilt ON the boarding-point graph + demand model) — parent agent
ROOT CAUSE of "routes cross land / connect to nothing / not dense": routes were drawn from the
data-spine edge set (city-centroid + locale node ids) while the ~1,885 curated boarding points were a
SEPARATE pin layer in a different id space. Result: ~0% of BPs sat on a route; 340/400 route endpoints
were raw label strings. The fix is a DATA fix, not a render fix (squarely Tasklet's half of the contract).
- NEW: `route_network.py` builds a LAYERED, demand-weighted network ON the BP graph for the marquee
  markets (UAE, Singapore, Bali, Phuket): `local` BP↔BP mesh (hub-spokes + k=2 nearest-neighbour chords,
  deduped to ~1km grid) + `regional`/`trunk` featured corridors from `route-demand-config.json`.
  Endpoints land on REAL BP coords (front-end's geometric on_route + terminus nodes then light the pins)
  and `from`/`to` are real node ids — city node id for inter-cluster (so degree lights the hub), or the
  EXACT `bp-<md5((bp.id or name)+node_id)[:10]>` hash build.py assigns to pins for intra-cluster/local.
  Result: marquee-market BP→route connectivity 18% → 88% (Dubai/Langkawi/Fujairah 100%); 373 → 861 routes.
- DEMAND MODEL (reference/route-demand-model.md, LOCKED): `traffic_weight = expected_volume × navier_fit`,
  calibrated to OBSERVED flows (ferry pax, air O&D, border crossings, hotel keys) — NOT graph degree.
  `trip_purpose` is a separate tag mapping to the partner (commuter→transit/super-app, tourism→hospitality,
  luxury→charter, local→intra-city). Bands: trunk 0.80–1.00 · regional 0.45–0.79 · local 0.10–0.40.
  Evidence/anchor strings stay in the config (INTERNAL); only numeric weight + neutral enums ship.
- COARSE-LAND-MASK / LAGOON-JETTY TRAP (generalizable): global_land_mask (~1km) reads urban
  lagoon/channel jetties (Al Majaz up Khalid Lagoon, HarbourFront behind Sentosa, Batam Centre, DCFT) as
  sitting IN land, so SeaGrid A* can't even start there and short approaches read as land-crossings.
  Three-part fix: (1) `_water_anchor()` radial search for the nearest open-water point — route from THERE,
  prepend the true jetty; the connector leg falls inside the gate's 1.5nm endpoint buffer so it's ignored;
  cap anchor distance ≤1.5nm or the leg leaks past the buffer. (2) If the iconic pin is too deep in
  mask-land (Al Majaz, DCFT), re-point the corridor to a sea-facing pin in the same cluster (Sharjah
  Corniche; Tanjung Belungkor — which is also the REAL Tanah-Merah↔Desaru ferry terminal). (3) For
  channels narrower than grid res (Tanah Merah↔Batam threads a strait island), a single hand `waypoints`
  entry in the config overrides A*. Net: 13/13 featured corridors emit, 0/861 routes cross land.
- THRESHOLD: route_network LAND_GATE_KM=1.99 (strictly under qa_land_crossing.py's 2.0km, with margin) —
  recovered Dubai↔Abu Dhabi which routed at exactly 1.92km (was being dropped by an over-conservative 1.9).
- INTEGRATION: build.py skips intra-managed-market data-spine edges (`_owning(from) & _owning(to)` both in
  MANAGED_OWNERS) so the two layers don't double-draw; cross-market long-hauls still come from the spine.
  ICS spoke generator also skips MANAGED_SLUGS. route_network is called after ICS, extends route_features.
- OPEN: riau (60%, only 5 BPs incl remote Anambas), desaru (72%), abu-dhabi (76%), bali (73%) sit below the
  90% bar — long-tail BPs far from cluster core; raise per-cluster spoke cap / add sub-cluster hubs to lift.

## Entry (2026-05-30): Route data-integrity gates + Quanta-LR legibility + build speed

**Context:** Jaideep flagged routes-to-nowhere ("Phuket → malaysia", "vietnam → Da Nang"), an infeasible Pioneer II @ 430 nm, a random-looking Quanta-LR network spraying to unlabelled BPs (Manila→Bacuit Bay, Likupang→Davao), and slow/expensive build-deploy.

**Fixes (all at the source in build.py / route_network.py / config):**
1. **Country-shell endpoint gate** — `COUNTRY_SHELL_IDS` set; drop any curated edge whose from/to (or its `_owning` prefix) is a bare country centroid (japan/malaysia/vietnam/korea/taiwan/…). Killed 21 nonsense routes. Root cause = country-shell auto-split dumped `country→subcity` edges using the country centroid as an endpoint.
2. **Range/platform gate on the REAL routed arc** — compute `_arc_nm(arc)` from the routed geometry (NOT the stale stored `distance_nm`); >70 nm forces Quanta-LR, >2000 nm drops. Stop trusting upstream `platform`/`distance_nm`.
3. **ICR = Pioneer-only** — intra-cluster spoke generator now drops anything >70 nm (was 320 nm Quanta cap). Every long-range Quanta-LR line must be a CURATED corridor between named hubs — never an auto-sprayed spoke to an unlabelled far BP. This removed the "random" look.
4. **Curated Quanta-LR hero chains** in route-demand-config.json between PRIORITISED, LABELLED city nodes: Andaman = Phuket→Langkawi→Penang; Lesser Sunda = Bali→Lombok→Komodo(Labuan Bajo). Add the hub slug to BOTH the market's `clusters` and build.py `MANAGED_SLUGS` (else ICR double-generates).

**Waypoint solving for hard island-threading corridors (PROMOTED METHOD):**
- The per-market SeaGrid is too coarse/large for long hops (Lombok→Komodo, 234 nm across Sumbawa) → it silently falls back to a straight line → crosses land → corridor dropped.
- RELIABLE FIX: solve waypoints offline with a fine grid A* (res 0.025°, water cells only, 8-neighbour) between water-anchored endpoints, then **line-of-sight simplify at a FINE step (0.35 km)** — each kept leg is provably water at fine sampling. 6 waypoints cleared Lombok→Komodo. Feed as `corridors[].waypoints`; route_network uses hand_wps verbatim (skips grid) and its endpoint buffer (1.5 nm) absorbs the jetty-coast clip.
- Do NOT hand-guess waypoints and iterate via full builds — solve once offline against global_land_mask, verify mid-legs clear, THEN build once.

**Build speed (Jaideep: builds too slow/expensive):**
- **Route-network geometry cache** (`.rn_cache.json`) keyed on sha256(route_network.py + config + every managed BP file). Unchanged inputs → reuse cached A* geometry (the slow step), seconds instead of minutes. `RN_NO_CACHE=1` forces regen.
- **Dev vs deploy split**: iterate with a plain `python3 build.py` (cache hit, fast, no gates); run the full 7-step `build_safe.sh` (partition→enrich→build→externalization gate→land gate→surface guard→deploy) ONLY at deploy time. The 923-route land gate is the expensive part and is not needed on every dev build.

**Verified live (navier-atlas.vercel.app):** 923 routes · 0 country-shell endpoints · 0 Pioneer II >70 nm · 0/923 cross land · all 4 hero-chain corridors present · leak sweep 0.

## Entry (2026-05-30): Named-pin promotion, marquee always-on labels, SEA+MENA chain extension
- **Marquee always-on cities (Singapore problem)**: flagship city pins were absorbed into Mapbox clusters at default zoom (clusterMaxZoom:5) AND lost label placement to dense neighbours (text-allow-overlap:false). Fix = dedicated `priority_city` feature bucket (build.py PRIORITY_CITIES set) rendered from a SEPARATE UNCLUSTERED source with text-allow-overlap:true + text-ignore-placement:true. Always edit BOTH the data split (build.py) and template.html render (priority-* layers, click handlers, DEFAULT_OPACITY, applyStoryFocus). 13 marquee cities now always labelled.
- **Named-hub promotion for mega-clusters**: a single city node covering many marquee islands (Philippines: Manila/Cebu/El Nido/Coron/Boracay/Siargao) reads as ONE pin. Promote sub-hubs by matching their boarding-point name in build.py BP-injection loop → override feature type "poi"→"city", set clean shortName + seed degree + promoted flag. They then render as city-tier pins that declutter by zoom. PROMOTED_HUBS table keyed by (cluster slug -> [(BP-name substring, label, degree)]). Also add CITY_SHORTNAME_OVERRIDE to rename the now-redundant verbose cluster centroid (e.g. "Manila / Cebu / Palawan / Bora…" -> "Manila").
- **Data bug found**: mega-cluster sub-POIs (`...__cebu`, `...__el-nido`) had broken coords clustered near the centroid; several `_ww__*` POIs had garbage coords dumped exactly on Singapore (103.86,1.27). All already suppressed by the covered-city bbox filter — but worth a future upstream data-spine cleanup.
- **Coarse global_land_mask hard limits (route endpoints)**: two failure modes block curated corridors and CANNOT be waypoint-fixed:
  (1) **Unanchorable jetty** — `_water_anchor` needs open water within 1.5nm; dredged artificial lagoons (AMAALA/Red Sea Global Triple Bay) and deeply-enclosed bays read as solid land with NO water cell in radius → corridor always drops. Switching to alternate seaward BPs in the same complex may still fail.
  (2) **Disconnected water pocket** — a jetty anchors fine (Coron, 0.18nm) but its water cell is isolated from the open-sea A* grid by sub-grid-resolution channels (Calamianes) → A* between anchors finds no path. Coron reachable from NE (Manila side anchored) but not SW/SE.
  Decision rule: try fine-A* offline; if anchor-fail OR astar-fail persist at res 0.008, DROP the corridor and keep the endpoint as a named pin only. Log for a future high-res OSM-coastline pass. Do NOT keep burning cycles against the 1km mask.
- **Waypoint precision matters for the gate**: route_network densifies waypoints at 0.6nm then checks each point against the mask. Waypoints rounded to 3 decimals (~110m) can shift a point onto a mask-land cell → GATE-FAIL even though the unrounded path was clean. Emit corridor waypoints at **4 decimals**. (El Nido→Puerto Princesa: FAIL@3dec → PASS@4dec.)
- **Validate against route_network's EXACT anchor+gate offline**: replicate `_water_anchor` (0.003° rings, max 1.5nm) and `_arc_mid_land_nm` (0.6nm densify, 1.5nm endpoint buffer, ≤1.99km interior land) in the offline solver and route between the ANCHOR points (not raw piers) — otherwise the unvalidated anchor→first-waypoint and last-waypoint→anchor connector legs silently fail in the real build.
- **RN_DEBUG=1 env flag** added to route_network corridor loop: prints each dropped corridor's from/to cluster+match+coords + reason (endpoint vs route). Use the parse-BP_CITY_MAP-from-build-source harness (ast.literal_eval on the dict literal — never hardcode) to run route_network.generate standalone (~2-3 min) instead of a full 6-min build when iterating on corridors.

## Entry (2026-05-30): Bidirectional GitHub sync — push gated builds back to main
**Trigger:** Jaideep flagged that Claude branches off a stale repo `index.html`, forcing repeated 3-way hand-merges (his words: "perhaps you also need to be pushing changes to github so claude can keep in mind your changes going forward").

**New standing protocol (drift fix):** After every gated production deploy, Tasklet pushes back to `main`:
1. production `index.html` (from `_dist/`, already externalization-gated + live-swept)
2. refreshed `data-clean/` 4-blob JSONs (FEATURES_BY_TYPE, ROUTES, STORIES, VESSEL_SPECS) — extract from the built index.html via `claude_to_template._find_value_span` (reuse de-baker, don't re-derive)
3. `docs/CHANGES-FROM-TASKLET.md` documenting any render-layer additions so Claude preserves them on regenerate
Claude branches from `main` → inherits Tasklet's render+data → no more hand-merges.

**3-way merge method (when Claude's branch predates a Tasklet render-layer change):** de-bake all three (BASE=prev Claude render, MINE=current template.html, THEIRS=new Claude render) to placeholder templates, then `diff3 -m MINE BASE THEIRS`. Verify BOTH feature sets survive AND that shared control arrays (DEFAULT_OPACITY, focus/dim loop, highlight/toggle) contain both layer groups — diff3 textual-clean ≠ semantically complete; grep the control regions.

**Gotchas:**
- `github_*` tool `localPath` MUST be under `/agent/` — `/tmp/` paths fail with "Path not found". Copy to `/agent/home/...` first.
- Pulling Claude's 2.7MB render + repo content reads can exhaust the GitHub hourly API limit fast; large multi-file pushes then 429. Cooldown ~hourly-window; retry. Live site unaffected — push is a mirror step, never blocks deploy.
- Vessel-spec placeholder token is `__VESSELS__` (not `__VESSEL_SPECS__`); the JS const is `VESSEL_SPECS`. Don't confuse when validating blob round-trips.
- Repo `data-clean/FEATURES_BY_TYPE.json` must include the `priority_city` bucket (separate from `city`) or Claude's source won't populate the always-on layer.

## Entry (2026-05-30): First live 3-way merge of a Claude push that predated a Tasklet render change
**Context:** Claude pushed `claude/trusting-clarke-OUKMt`@5a19988 (v17 look-feel: basemap lift, locale/Areas tier removed end-to-end, toggle bar deleted, nav re-tiered) — but branched from 3137f61, BEFORE my v19 priority-city always-on layers landed on main. Blind de-bake→template would have wiped the priority layers and re-hidden Singapore.

**Fetching Claude's render when repo is anonymous-404:** the app-installed repo returns 404 to ALL anonymous access (raw.githubusercontent, codeload, github.com/raw, api.github.com) despite `private:false` in the webhook. `curl` cannot get it. Use **`github_download_file`** (connection tool, uses the app token, saves to /agent/, handles >1MB) — NOT `github_get_file_content` (returns null content for files >1MB; its downloadUrl 404s anon).

**3-way merge recipe (proven):**
1. `github_download_file` THEIRS (new commit sha) and BASE (the push's `before` sha) to /agent/.
2. De-bake all to placeholder templates: `claude_to_template.py <in> --out <tmpl>`. MINE = current `template.html` (already de-baked + my edits).
3. Confirm BASE is MINE's true ancestor: `diff BASE_tmpl MINE` should show ONLY your own edits. If it shows Claude changes too, you picked the wrong BASE.
4. `diff3 -m MINE BASE THEIRS > merged`. Resolve conflicts with a Python regex over the full conflict block: `<<<<<<< mine\n(.*?)\|\|\|\|\|\|\| base\n(.*?)=======\n(.*?)>>>>>>> theirs\n` with DOTALL. **The middle/THEIRS section can be EMPTY** (pure deletion) — your regex MUST allow empty capture groups or it silently under-counts conflicts.
5. Resolution rule when your additions sit adjacent to a tier the other side deleted: **your additions IN, their deletions honoured** (here: priority-* layers kept, locale-* removed everywhere — click list, DEFAULT_OPACITY, focus/dim block, source/layer defs, stat counts).
6. Verify: 0 conflict markers; your feature refs present; deleted-tier refs match THEIRS's intended residual count exactly (don't expect 0 — Claude kept defensive locale CSS/search-skip/popup-label; cross-check `grep -c locale theirs_tmpl` == merged); placeholders intact (4).
7. Install as template.html (back up prior as `.vN.bak`), run full `build_safe.sh`, then push fused result to main so it's the new baseline.

**Bidirectional-sync payoff confirmed:** because I'd already pushed v19 + CHANGES-FROM-TASKLET.md to main, the *next* Claude branch (if it forks from main) won't need this merge. This push was the last one forked from the old point.

**Why:** the Tasklet full-build-on-every-Claude-push loop (A* regen + land gate + partition + Vercel CLI) was the dominant credit sink. Jaideep: hand publish to Claude Code so Tasklet does research/enrichment.
**New model (DIVISION-OF-LABOR v3):**
- Tasklet runs the EXPENSIVE gates ONCE per *data* change (partition→externalization→land→BP-on-water), then SEALS: `seal_bundle.py` writes `data-clean/*.json` + `SEAL.json` (canonical-JSON sha256 per blob + gate verdicts). Pushed to `main`.
- Claude Code owns build + deploy. Cheap pre-flight before every prod deploy: (1) blob hash-match vs SEAL.json (anti-tamper), (2) substring grep of final index.html vs `docs/EXCLUSION-TOKENS.txt`, (3) MapLibre style smoke test (route line layers present + 0 rejected layers — catches the F-01 class).
- **Safe-by-construction:** data-clean carries zero internal fields (those live only in internal tree + humans.json, never in git), so direct deploy can't leak. Land-crossing can't regress from a render-only change (geometry frozen in seal).
- Retired GitHub auto-build trigger `wti_rgbw9hgvcftgb8qps2j2` (the sink). Added cheap daily backstop leak sweep `cti_0wqh97xhsejqh859m8dn` (curl live URL + grep EXCLUSION_PATTERNS; silent unless hit).
**Tooling added:** `atlas-external/seal_bundle.py`, `atlas-external/bp_on_water_gate.py`. `docs/EXCLUSION-TOKENS.txt` auto-generated from partition_spec.py (78 patterns) — single source of truth keeps both gates in sync.

## Entry (2026-05-30): BP-on-water gate — F-03 triage
QA flagged inland boarding points. `bp_on_water_gate.py` over 1875 POIs: 1179 read "on land" by global_land_mask BUT 1049 are ≤2.5nm from water (coarse-mask false positives on creeks/lagoons/rivers — e.g. Dubai Creek marinas; NOT real errors). 130 genuinely mis-geocoded, in 3 buckets: (a) legit-inland *gateway/adjacency* context nodes mislabeled as marine BPs (Liwa, Jebel Jais, Surat Thani mainland, KL/Klang) → exclude from marine layer or re-glyph; (b) river/lake ports the OCEAN mask can't see (Han River, Mekong, Tonle Sap, Lake Van, Lake Toba) → need inland-water mask not is_ocean; (c) true bad coords (Red Sea Airport, AMAALA lagoon). **Lesson:** is_ocean is too strict for marine BPs — must combine with near-coast tolerance + inland-navigable-water set, and tier-filter context nodes BEFORE the water test.

## Entry (2026-05-30): Authoritative POI re-geocode + high-res coastline snap (precise_regeocode.py)
**Problem (Jaideep):** boarding points "way off in the ocean" (Zaya Nurai, Saadiyat Marina, Yas Beach, Zabeel Saray) and many Palm Jumeirah pins inaccurate. **Root cause:** the precision pipeline marked bad POIs `prec=rejected` but STILL SHIPPED their garbage coordinates. Rejected = must NOT ship those coords.
**Fix — two-stage, tool `app/poi-precision/precise_regeocode.py`:**
1. **Google Places Text Search (New)** with a locationBias circle around city anchor → TRUE location of the named place. Take the **top (most-relevant) result**; use anchor distance only as a *sanity gate* (REGION_KM=300), NEVER to re-rank local candidates (re-ranking by anchor picked inland "jetty office" over the real waterfront resort — caused false NOWATER flags). Tiered: ≤50km central, 50-300 far_region (real same-emirate islands like Sir Bani Yas/Delma/Sila — keep + tag), >300 reject (catches wrong-continent matches e.g. "Lulu Island" 11825km, "Côte d'Azur" 12097km).
2. **Mapbox Tilequery** (`mapbox-streets-v8` `water` layer) for high-res land/water — global_land_mask (~1km) is useless for Palm Jumeirah fronds. Key facts learned:
   - `tilequery.distance == 0` → point inside water; `>0` → on land, value = metres to nearest water.
   - Returned feature `geometry` is a **Point = nearest point ON the water feature to the query** (the `geometry=polygon` param is a *type filter*, not a geometry format). So ONE call gives both the distance AND the coastline snap target → no brute-force ring search (that fired ~190 calls/POI and blew the tilequery rate limit, whose 429s were silently misread as "no water").
   - **Snap = take that nearest-water Point directly**, then minimal outward nudge (15-180m) along P→coast bearing; do NOT step `base+large_extra` (overshoots a narrow channel back onto land).
   - **WATER_TOL_M=2.0**: treat distance ≤2m as water (floating-point boundary: Anantara's nearest-water read 0.05m and a strict `==0` false-rejected it).
   - **Retry/backoff on 429/5xx**; NEVER conflate an API throttle with "land" (separate FLAG_APIFAIL vs FLAG_NOWATER).
**Result:** Dubai 71 / Abu Dhabi 43 / Singapore 51 = **165/165 on water, 0 on land, 0 deep-ocean**, independently verified by a fresh tilequery sweep + satellite static-image eyeball. Backups at `boarding-points/_backup_precore/`.
**Reusable:** run `precise_regeocode.py <city.json> --region <hint>` for any coastal city; flags <~3% genuinely-hard cases (planned/World-islands, working shipyards that geocode to inland offices, far islands) for one-line manual override via better query string.

---
## Entry (2026-05-30): Marquee-wide precision rollout — batch + second-pass + final water gate
Rolled `precise_regeocode` across all 54 coastal boarding-point files (2,307 BPs). Three-stage pipeline, each stage a reusable tool in `app/poi-precision/`:
1. **`batch_regeocode.py`** — per-file geocode+snap with a slug→country REGION map (query qualifier + sanity gate). Ran in background (nohup + PROGRESS.log poll); ~15s/city. **Lesson: long multi-city API jobs MUST run detached with a line-by-line progress log — a single foreground command exceeds the 5-min cap and one hung city (yeosu, transient 429 storm) stalls the whole run; kill + re-run that one city standalone.**
2. **`second_pass.py`** — surgical recovery of ONLY `confidence==flagged` points (fixed points untouched) with relaxed params: distance gate 300→**900 km** and snap radius 1450→**4000 m**, and the **parenthetical KEPT in the query**. Recovered 221 pts. **Key insight — multi-island CLUSTER files (Manila+Cebu+Palawan, Okinawa+Yaeyama, Jakarta+Batam/Riau, Bali+Sumba/Komodo, Banda+Ternate) bundle places hundreds of km from the single `city_anchor`; the 300 km gate (right for single cities, kills wrong-continent matches) wrongly rejects legit far islands. 900 km recovers them (Cebu 574, Naha 411, Batam 868) while STILL rejecting absurd wrong-continent hits (Egypt "Marassi" 1281, RA Phinisi 1518).** Manila/Cebu/Palawan 32→91 high, Okinawa/Yaeyama 32→79.
3. **`final_water_gate.py`** — **CRITICAL**: `build.py` renders EVERY BP at its coords regardless of confidence, so any still-`flagged` point keeps its (possibly wrong) original coord and would still render off-location. Rule: a flagged, non-hidden BP renders only if its CURRENT coord is strict-on-water AND a coastline lies within 5 km (catches both on-land AND deep-ocean); else `relevance="hide"` (build skips hide). **Fail-closed on API failure → hide.** Kept 47 (good originals that merely failed geocode), hid 75 (bad coords / unmappable village piers / brand-new unmapped islands like NEOM Sindalah).
**Final state: 2,307 BPs · 2,107 (91.3%) high-confidence precise+on-water · 47 verified-coastal kept · render set = 2,154 points, every one coastal-on-water (0 land, 0 deep-ocean).** Backups: `boarding-points/_backup_precore/*.bak` (53). Visual proofs (`app/poi-precision/proof/*.png`) confirm Dubai/AD/Singapore/Bali/Phuket/Male/Bodrum/Hong Kong all clean.
**Genuinely-hard long tail (~59, now hidden if non-coastal):** remote village piers absent from Google Places (Raja Ampat Sawinggrai/Samate), national-park anchorages with no fixed jetty, brand-new unmapped resort islands (NEOM Sindalah, some Red Sea), small-atoll resort centroids where Mapbox water doesn't render the lagoon. These need manual coord overrides (like the Dubai 3 specials) — best done targeted for marquee resorts only.

## Entry (2026-05-30): De-fusing multi-place cluster nodes + poisoned route-cache trap
**Context:** Jaideep flagged fused nodes (Manila/Cebu/Palawan, Okinawa/Yaeyama, Izu/Shimoda) — bundling Cebu under Manila's anchor was the ROOT CAUSE of the Cebu-POI mis-geocode (POIs anchored on Manila 600km away). De-fusing fixes names AND prevents that geocoding-error class.

**Methodology — `defuse_clusters.py`:**
1. Split a fused BP file into constituent cities by id-prefix / linked_locale / geographic clustering; each constituent gets its OWN city_anchor = median of its real BPs (snapped to a recognizable on-coast hub, e.g. Palawan→El Nido 119.39/11.18, not the open-sea median).
2. Inject constituents as `supplemental-nodes.json` entries (persistent; partition does NOT regenerate this file — safe injection path without a partition round-trip).
3. Retire fused parents via `HIDE_ON_MAP` + suppress stray `__` sub-POIs.
4. Single-node label cleanup (Hokkaido, Maldives) edited in BOTH output-external AND data-spine source so partition stays consistent.
5. **Pre-existing supplemental node trap:** if a constituent id already exists in supplemental-nodes (e.g. palawan-philippines), the merge SKIPS it → your anchor/name not applied. Always OVERWRITE matched ids, don't skip.

**Build-time reference normalization (durable):** spine had 100 edge + 150 org refs to the fused parent — too many to hand-redistribute safely. Added `RETIRED_REMAP` in build.py that remaps retired-parent ids → primary constituent on edge endpoints, story `city_id`, and org `city_presence` at BUILD time. Holds regardless of partition state. Don't mass-edit spine for this.

**⚠️ POISONED ROUTE-CACHE TRAP (critical):** Splitting clusters changes route-network cluster membership → generates NEW inter-constituent regional/local connectors. The `.rn_cache.json` from a prior (mask-less or pre-split) build was reused on CACHE HIT, so route_network did NOT re-run its land guard on the new geometry → 500/1676 land crossings slipped to the qa gate while generation reported `dropped_land=0`. **Live v19 passed 0/1354; my first rebuild failed 500/1676 — diagnosed by running qa_land_crossing on the LIVE production html for comparison.** FIX: after ANY cluster-membership change, `rm .rn_cache.json` and force full A* regen WITH `--with global_land_mask --with numpy` so the land guard actually drops crossers. Result: 0/1504 clean. **Rule: cache key must include cluster-config hash; until then, purge cache on any node split/merge.**

**Verification:** Mapbox satellite proof maps per cluster (5 PH pins, 3 S-Japan pins) confirm each constituent on its correct island. Live externalization gate + split-presence grep on the deployed URL.

## Entry (2026-05-30): cache-regen-over-the-wall, path-independent route sweep, seal=release-only
- **F-11 root-caught + fixed (path-independent final route sweep):** a fresh route regen surfaced 9 QLR routes ≤70nm + geopolitically-impossible NEOM/Sharm→Eilat routes that build.py's Rule A *missed* — because Rule A only ran on the curated-edge loop, NOT on route_network/ICR-sourced edges. **Fix: one authoritative sweep over the MERGED `route_features` list (after all sources extend it, before labeling) that (a) re-applies Rule A ≤70nm→Pioneer II and (b) HARD-drops flag-and-exclude routes per `politically-active-flag-and-exclude-zones.md`.** Being post-merge makes it source-agnostic and immune to F-11 route-count non-determinism. Result: `qlr_under_pioneer: []`, Eilat routes dropped (incl. a 1.0nm coord-bug leg). **Rule: enforce platform/geo invariants at the merge point, not per-source.**
- **Completing a route-cache regen that exceeds the 300s tool wall (RN_PREWARM_EXIT pattern):** the `.rn_cache.json` key hashes managed BP files, so ANY BP edit (e.g. Riau density) busts it → every build attempts a full A* regen → 290s timeout → no cache write → stuck. Monolithic cache can't be partial-warmed. **Fix: two env-guarded shortcuts in build.py — `RN_PREWARM_EXIT=1` (1) skips ICR and (2) `sys.exit(0)` immediately after the route-network cache WRITE.** This dedicates the whole window to `generate()` alone (228s, fits), writes the new cache; the *next* normal build gets CACHE HIT (instant route_network) + ICR + tail (218s, fits). Two builds, both under the wall.
- **ICR — not route_network — is now the heavy step (~200s even on CACHE HIT).** The route cache only covers route_network's A*; intra_cluster_routes re-runs its own A*+land-guard every build with no cache. That's why a CACHE-HIT build still took 218s. Future opt: cache ICR too (key on BP files + targets). For now, split enrich out and run synchronous.
- **uv resolver overhead steals the budget:** `uv run --with numpy --with global_land_mask` re-resolves/downloads each invocation. For the tight builds, `pip install numpy global_land_mask` once into system python and run `python3 -u build.py` directly — saves the resolver/download seconds that were pushing generate() over the wall.
- **Seal = RELEASE-mode only (deploy-protocol fix):** build.py reads ONLY `output-external/` (partitioned clean data), never the spine → render/route/content iterations are leak-safe by construction. So a stale `SEAL.json` must NOT block a dev deploy. Authored `_ingest/DEPLOY-PROTOCOL.md`: dev pre-flight = pitch-content grep only; seal regenerated + enforced only in weekly `release.sh`. Told Claude to scope pre-flight §3.1 behind a `--release` flag. Kills the recurring "stale seal blocks deploy" friction.
- **Partner `phase.cities` node-id contract:** phase tokens (and brief keys) must EQUAL canonical node ids (`bali-indonesia`, `phuket-phang-nga-thailand`) or map highlighting + panel lookup silently no-op. Grab phase-3 used bare `bali`/`phuket` → fixed. **Audit every partner's phase.cities against `nodes.json` ids whenever authoring/editing a partner proposal.**

Also `markets[]` for multi-market partners (Uber MENA/US/Global) and `rationale`/`use_cases`/`timeline` on phases. All optional → existing renders unaffected; Claude opts in. **Commercial model stays OFF the website (deck-only) per Jaideep — site shows need + journeys, not unit economics.**
- **vs_candela rebuttal is mandatory for foiling-vessel partners.** Candela has 8×P-12 at NEOM + Red Sea orders; a serious partner WILL ask. Frame = market-validation + complementarity (P-12 = fixed-route 30-pax public shuttle; Navier = software-defined on-demand premium tier + Quanta-LR 2,000nm hybrid, no charging infra). Researched & sourced in `partner-pitch/research/_research-sprint-2026-05-30.md`.
- **Leak-grep catches strategist jargon, which is ALSO bad partner copy.** `check_pitch_content.sh` flagged "wedge", "Founders Fund"/"investor", from partner-facing JSON (these ship as `window.PARTNERS`). Scrub "wedge"→"opportunity/corridor", drop named investor funds → "a marquee cap table", "investor relations"→"Q1 2026". Win-win: passes gate + reads as partner benefit, not internal framing. Field key `navier_moat` is not gated (key, not value) but consider renaming for partner optics.
- **BULK NODE DATA-QUALITY CLEANUP (general, scriptable — never hardcode data):**
  1. **coords:None backfill** — 19 country-shell split nodes had no coords (won't render). Backfilled DETERMINISTICALLY from each node's resolved `{country}__…` sibling via country-prefix + token-overlap match (score≥2); 2 false hits (Desaru matched KL, Da Nang matched HCMC — both score-1/weak) overridden explicitly. Set `coords_source` for provenance. Apply to BOTH data-spine AND output-external.
  2. **Duplicate-id collapse** — output-external had 165 dup ids (incl. the named Penghu *phantom*: a 2nd `taiwan__penghu-archipelago` at Taipei coords resolved=False, and a Bodrum dup at Istanbul coords). Group by id, keep best by tuple-score (has-coords, resolved, not-SG-default, most-keys), drop rest. Collapsed 165→0 dups; phantom auto-removed (it was the resolved=False twin).
  3. **Garbage SG-default POIs** — 124 `_ww__*` POIs (type=poi, parent=None) sat exactly on the Singapore anchor `[103.86333,1.27111]` (failed geocodes defaulting to SG) — Bacan, Bai Tu Long, Banda Is., plus junk ("Air","airshots"). Verified **0 edge/route references** → safe to delete (removing a *referenced* waypoint would create the dangling endpoints we fixed before — always grep edges/routes first). Removed from both files.
  - **These node-set edits do NOT bust the route cache** (cache key hashes supplemental BP/edge/waypoint files, not output-external/nodes.json) → rebuild = CACHE HIT, fast. Newly-coorded P2 cities get pins but no routes until a regen — acceptable.
- **Riau→Bintan reclassification with a STABLE id:** Jaideep wanted the recognizable destination (Bintan, where the Lagoi resorts are) emphasized over "Riau Islands." Changed node `name` + `label-overrides.json` value to "Bintan" but KEPT id `riau-islands-indonesia` so 17 BPs, cross-border routes, and Grab phase-2 don't break. Rename labels, never ids, once a node is wired.
- **GitHub primary rate-limit (5000/hr) can exhaust mid-session** → `github_push_to_branch` returns "API rate limit exceeded for user ID …"; resets on the hourly window, NOT after a short sleep. Dev deploy is independent (Vercel), so the live site updates regardless — push the GitHub mirror on the next window. Don't burn many retry cycles inside the limit window.
